The planning process for each adult mental health initiative shall include, but not be limited to, mental health consumers, families, advocates, local mental health advisory councils, local and state providers, representatives of state and local public employee bargaining units, and the department of human services. As part of the planning process, the county board or boards shall designate a managing entity responsible for receipt of funds and management of adult mental health initiatives.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4661.5a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5a.<span class="headnote">Evaluations.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of management and budget, in consultation with the commissioner of human services and within available appropriations, shall create and maintain an inventory of adult mental health initiative services administered by the county boards, identifying evidence-based services and services that are theory-based or promising practices. The commissioner of management and budget, in consultation with the commissioner of human services, shall select adult mental health initiative services that are promising practices or theory-based activities for which the commissioner of management and budget shall conduct evaluations using experimental or quasi-experimental design. The commissioner of human services, in consultation with the commissioner of management and budget, shall encourage county boards to administer adult mental health initiative services to support experimental or quasi-experimental evaluation and shall require county boards to collect and report information that is needed to complete the inventory and evaluation for any adult mental health initiative service that is selected for an evaluation. The commissioner of management and budget, under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/15.08">15.08</a>, may obtain additional relevant data to support the inventory and the experimental or quasi-experimental evaluation studies.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4661.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Duties of commissioner.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) For purposes of adult mental health initiatives, the commissioner shall facilitate integration of funds or other resources as needed and requested by each adult mental health initiative. These resources may include:</p>
          <p>(1) community support services funds administered under Minnesota Rules, parts 9535.1700 to 9535.1760;</p>
          <p>(2) other mental health special project funds;</p>
          <p>(3) medical assistance, MinnesotaCare, and housing support under chapter 256I if requested by the adult mental health initiative's managing entity and if the commissioner determines this would be consistent with the state's overall health care reform efforts; and</p>
          <p>(4) regional treatment center resources, with consent from the Direct Care and Treatment executive board.</p>
          <p>(b) The commissioner shall consider the following criteria in awarding grants for adult mental health initiatives:</p>
          <p>(1) the ability of the initiatives to accomplish the objectives described in subdivision 2;</p>
          <p>(2) the size of the target population to be served; and</p>
          <p>(3) geographical distribution.</p>
          <p>(c) The commissioner shall review overall status of the initiatives at least every two years and recommend any legislative changes needed by January 15 of each odd-numbered year.</p>
          <p>(d) The commissioner may waive administrative rule requirements that are incompatible with the implementation of the adult mental health initiative.</p>
          <p>(e) The commissioner may exempt the participating counties from fiscal sanctions for noncompliance with requirements in laws and rules that are incompatible with the implementation of the adult mental health initiative.</p>
          <p>(f) The commissioner may award grants to an entity designated by a county board or group of county boards to pay for start-up and implementation costs of the adult mental health initiative.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4661.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Duties of adult mental health initiative board.</span></h2>
          <p>The adult mental health initiative board, or other entity which is approved to administer an adult mental health initiative, shall:</p>
          <p>(1) administer the initiative in a manner that is consistent with the objectives described in subdivision 2 and the planning process described in subdivision 5;</p>
          <p>(2) assure that no one is denied services that they would otherwise be eligible for; and</p>
          <p>(3) provide the commissioner of human services with timely and pertinent information through the following methods:</p>
          <p>(i) submission of mental health plans and plan amendments which are based on a format and timetable determined by the commissioner;</p>
          <p>(ii) submission of social services expenditure and grant reconciliation reports, based on a coding format to be determined by mutual agreement between the initiative's managing entity and the commissioner; and</p>
          <p>(iii) submission of data and participation in an evaluation of the adult mental health initiatives, to be designed cooperatively by the commissioner and the initiatives.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4661.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.</h2>
          <p>MS 2020 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2022/0/99/#laws.1.49.0">2022 c 99 art 1 s 49</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4661.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Services and programs.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The following three distinct grant programs are funded under this section:</p>
          <p>(1) mental health crisis services;</p>
          <p>(2) housing with supports for adults with serious mental illness; and</p>
          <p>(3) projects for assistance in transitioning from homelessness (PATH program).</p>
          <p>(b) In addition, the following are eligible for grant funds:</p>
          <p>(1) community education and prevention;</p>
          <p>(2) client outreach;</p>
          <p>(3) early identification and intervention;</p>
          <p>(4) adult outpatient diagnostic assessment and psychological testing;</p>
          <p>(5) peer support services;</p>
          <p>(6) community support program services (CSP);</p>
          <p>(7) adult residential crisis stabilization;</p>
          <p>(8) supported employment;</p>
          <p>(9) assertive community treatment (ACT);</p>
          <p>(10) housing subsidies;</p>
          <p>(11) basic living, social skills, and community intervention;</p>
          <p>(12) emergency response services;</p>
          <p>(13) adult outpatient psychotherapy;</p>
          <p>(14) adult outpatient medication management;</p>
          <p>(15) adult mobile crisis services;</p>
          <p>(16) adult day treatment;</p>
          <p>(17) partial hospitalization;</p>
          <p>(18) adult residential treatment;</p>
          <p>(19) adult mental health targeted case management; and</p>
          <p>(20) transportation.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4661.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Commissioner duty to report on use of grant funds biennially.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) By November 1, 2016, and biennially thereafter, the commissioner of human services shall provide sufficient information to the members of the legislative committees having jurisdiction over mental health funding and policy issues to evaluate the use of funds appropriated under this section. The commissioner shall provide, at a minimum, the following information:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) the amount of funding to adult mental health initiatives, what programs and services were funded in the previous two years, gaps in services that each initiative brought to the attention of the commissioner, and outcome data for the programs and services that were funded; and</p>
          <p class="in">(2) the amount of funding for other targeted services and the location of services.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) This subdivision expires January 1, 2032.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4661.11">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11.<span class="headnote">Adult mental health initiative funding.</span></h2>
          <p>When implementing the funding formula to distribute adult mental health initiative funds, the commissioner shall ensure that no adult mental health initiative region receives less than the amount the region received in fiscal year 2022 in combined adult mental health initiative funding.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245.4661.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/245/#laws.5.21.0">1999 c 245 art 5 s 21</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/98/#laws.3.6.0">2005 c 98 art 3 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/4/#laws.2.2.0">1Sp2005 c 4 art 2 s 2</a>; art 5 s 4,5; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/107/#laws.4.1.0">2013 c 107 art 4 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/108/#laws.4.2.0">2013 c 108 art 4 s 2</a>,3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/0/71/#laws.2.8.0">2015 c 71 art 2 s 8</a>-11; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/158/#laws.1.84.0">2016 c 158 art 1 s 84</a>; art 2 s 45; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/1/6/#laws.2.39.0">1Sp2017 c 6 art 2 s 39</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/0/30/#laws.17.21.0">2021 c 30 art 17 s 21</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2022/0/98/#laws.14.10.0">2022 c 98 art 14 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2022/0/99/#laws.1.6.0">2022 c 99 art 1 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/25/#laws.0.116.0">2023 c 25 s 116</a>,117; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/70/#laws.17.3.0">2023 c 70 art 17 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/79/#laws.8.7.0">2024 c 79 art 8 s 7</a>,8</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245.4662">
        <h1 class="shn">245.4662 MENTAL HEALTH INNOVATION GRANT PROGRAM.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4662.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) For purposes of this section, the following terms have the meanings given them.</p>
          <p>(b) "Community partnership" means a project involving the collaboration of two or more eligible applicants.</p>
          <p>(c) "Eligible applicant" means an eligible county, Indian tribe, mental health service provider, hospital, or community partnership. Eligible applicant does not include a state-operated direct care and treatment facility or program under chapters 246 and 246C.</p>
          <p>(d) "Intensive residential treatment services" has the meaning given in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.0622">256B.0622</a>.</p>
          <p>(e) "Metropolitan area" means the seven-county metropolitan area, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/473.121#stat.473.121.2">473.121, subdivision 2</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4662.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Grants authorized.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of human services shall, in consultation with stakeholders, award grants to eligible applicants to plan, establish, or operate programs to improve accessibility and quality of community-based, outpatient mental health services and reduce the number of clients admitted to regional treatment centers and community behavioral health hospitals. The commissioner shall award half of all grant funds to eligible applicants in the metropolitan area and half of all grant funds to eligible applicants outside the metropolitan area. An applicant may apply for and the commissioner may award grants for two-year periods. The commissioner may reallocate underspending among grantees within the same grant period. The mental health innovation account is established under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/246.18">246.18</a> for ongoing funding.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4662.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Allocation of grants.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) An application must be on a form and contain information as specified by the commissioner but at a minimum must contain:</p>
          <p>(1) a description of the purpose or project for which grant funds will be used;</p>
          <p>(2) a description of the specific problem the grant funds will address;</p>
          <p>(3) a letter of support from the local mental health authority;</p>
          <p>(4) a description of achievable objectives, a work plan, and a timeline for implementation and completion of processes or projects enabled by the grant; and</p>
          <p>(5) a process for documenting and evaluating results of the grant.</p>
          <p>(b) The commissioner shall review each application to determine whether the application is complete and whether the applicant and the project are eligible for a grant. In evaluating applications according to paragraph (c), the commissioner shall establish criteria including, but not limited to: the eligibility of the project; the applicant's thoroughness and clarity in describing the problem grant funds are intended to address; a description of the applicant's proposed project; a description of the population demographics and service area of the proposed project; the manner in which the applicant will demonstrate the effectiveness of any projects undertaken; the proposed project's longevity and demonstrated financial sustainability after the initial grant period; and evidence of efficiencies and effectiveness gained through collaborative efforts. The commissioner may also consider other relevant factors. In evaluating applications, the commissioner may request additional information regarding a proposed project, including information on project cost. An applicant's failure to provide the information requested disqualifies an applicant. The commissioner shall determine the number of grants awarded.</p>
          <p>(c) Eligible applicants may receive grants under this section for purposes including, but not limited to, the following:</p>
          <p>(1) intensive residential treatment services providing time-limited mental health services in a residential setting;</p>
          <p>(2) the creation of stand-alone urgent care centers for mental health and psychiatric consultation services, crisis residential services, or collaboration between crisis teams and critical access hospitals;</p>
          <p>(3) establishing new community mental health services or expanding the capacity of existing services, including supportive housing; and</p>
          <p>(4) other innovative projects that improve options for mental health services in community settings and reduce the number of clients who remain in regional treatment centers and community behavioral health hospitals beyond when discharge is determined to be clinically appropriate.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4662.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.</h2>
          <p>MS 2020 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2022/0/55/#laws.1.187.0">2022 c 55 art 1 s 187</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245.4662.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/1/6/#laws.8.2.0">1Sp2017 c 6 art 8 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/0/30/#laws.17.22.0">2021 c 30 art 17 s 22</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/79/#laws.10.2.0">2024 c 79 art 10 s 2</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245.4663">
        <h1 class="shn">245.4663 MENTAL HEALTH PROVIDER SUPERVISION GRANT PROGRAM.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4663.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Grant program established.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall award grants to licensed or certified mental health providers that meet the criteria in subdivision 2 to fund supervision of or preceptorships for students, interns, and clinical trainees who are working toward becoming mental health professionals; to subsidize the costs of licensing applications and examination fees for clinical trainees; and to fund training for workers to become supervisors. For purposes of this section, an intern may include an individual who is working toward an undergraduate degree in the behavioral sciences or related field at an accredited educational institution.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4663.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Eligible providers.</span></h2>
          <p>In order to be eligible for a grant under this section, a mental health provider must:</p>
          <p>(1) provide at least 25 percent of the provider's yearly patient encounters to state public program enrollees or patients receiving sliding fee schedule discounts through a formal sliding fee schedule meeting the standards established by the United States Department of Health and Human Services under Code of Federal Regulations, title 42, section 51c.303;</p>
          <p>(2) primarily serve underrepresented communities as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/148E.010">148E.010</a>, subdivision 20; or</p>
          <p>(3) provide services to people in a city or township that is not within the seven-county metropolitan area as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/473.121#stat.473.121.2">473.121, subdivision 2</a>, and is not the city of Duluth, Mankato, Moorhead, Rochester, or St. Cloud.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4663.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Application; grant award.</span></h2>
          <p>A mental health provider seeking a grant under this section must apply to the commissioner at a time and in a manner specified by the commissioner. The commissioner shall review each application to determine if the application is complete, the mental health provider is eligible for a grant, and the proposed project is an allowable use of grant funds. The commissioner must determine the grant amount awarded to applicants that the commissioner determines will receive a grant.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4663.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Allowable uses of grant funds.</span></h2>
          <p>A mental health provider must use grant funds received under this section for one or more of the following:</p>
          <p>(1) to pay for direct supervision hours or preceptorships for students, interns, and clinical trainees, in an amount up to $7,500 per student, intern, or clinical trainee;</p>
          <p>(2) to establish a program to provide supervision to multiple students, interns, or clinical trainees;</p>
          <p>(3) to pay licensing application and examination fees for clinical trainees; or</p>
          <p>(4) to provide a weekend training program for workers to become supervisors.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4663.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Program oversight.</span></h2>
          <p>During the grant period, the commissioner may require grant recipients to provide the commissioner with information necessary to evaluate the program.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245.4663.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2022/0/99/#laws.1.7.0">2022 c 99 art 1 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/70/#laws.5.12.0">2023 c 70 art 5 s 12</a>; art 9 s 2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/127/#laws.61.2.0">2024 c 127 art 61 s 2</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245.467">
        <h1 class="shn">245.467 QUALITY OF SERVICES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.467.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Criteria.</span></h2>
          <p>Mental health services required by this chapter must be:</p>
          <p>(1) based, when feasible, on research findings;</p>
          <p>(2) based on individual clinical needs, cultural and ethnic needs, and other special needs of individuals being served;</p>
          <p>(3) provided in the most appropriate, least restrictive setting available to the county board;</p>
          <p>(4) accessible to all age groups;</p>
          <p>(5) delivered in a manner that provides accountability;</p>
          <p>(6) provided by qualified individuals as required in this chapter;</p>
          <p>(7) coordinated with mental health services offered by other providers; and</p>
          <p>(8) provided under conditions which protect the rights and dignity of the individuals being served.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.467.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Diagnostic assessment.</span></h2>
          <p>A provider of services governed by this section must complete a diagnostic assessment of a client according to the standards of section 245I.10.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.467.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Individual treatment plans.</span></h2>
          <p>A provider of services governed by this section must complete an individual treatment plan for a client according to the standards of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245I.10">245I.10, subdivisions 7</a> and 8.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.467.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Referral for case management.</span></h2>
          <p>Each provider of emergency services, day treatment services, outpatient treatment, community support services, residential treatment, acute care hospital inpatient treatment, or regional treatment center inpatient treatment must inform each of its clients with serious and persistent mental illness of the availability and potential benefits to the client of case management. If the client consents, the provider must refer the client by notifying the county employee designated by the county board to coordinate case management activities of the client's name and address and by informing the client of whom to contact to request case management. The provider must document compliance with this subdivision in the client's record.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.467.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Information for billing.</span></h2>
          <p>Each provider of outpatient treatment, community support services, day treatment services, emergency services, residential treatment, or acute care hospital inpatient treatment must include the name and home address of each client for whom services are included on a bill submitted to a county, if the client has consented to the release of that information and if the county requests the information. Each provider shall attempt to obtain each client's consent and must explain to the client that the information can only be released with the client's consent and may be used only for purposes of payment and maintaining provider accountability. The provider shall document the attempt in the client's record.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.467.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Restricted access to data.</span></h2>
          <p>The county board shall establish procedures to ensure that the names and addresses of persons receiving mental health services are disclosed only to:</p>
          <p>(1) county employees who are specifically responsible for determining county of financial responsibility or making payments to providers;</p>
          <p>(2) staff who provide treatment services or case management and their clinical supervisors; and</p>
          <p>(3) personnel of the welfare system or health care providers who have access to the data under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/13.46#stat.13.46.7">13.46, subdivision 7</a>.</p>
          <p>Release of mental health data on individuals submitted under subdivisions 4 and 5, to persons other than those specified in this subdivision, or use of this data for purposes other than those stated in subdivisions 4 and 5, results in civil or criminal liability under the standards in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/13.08">13.08</a> or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/13.09">13.09</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245.467.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/403/#laws.2.22.0">1987 c 403 art 2 s 22</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/689/#laws.2.78.0">1988 c 689 art 2 s 78</a>-80; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/282/#laws.4.11.0">1989 c 282 art 4 s 11</a>-13; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/568/#laws.5.1.0">1990 c 568 art 5 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/0/86/#laws.0.2.0">2011 c 86 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/0/71/#laws.2.12.0">2015 c 71 art 2 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/158/#laws.2.46.0">2016 c 158 art 2 s 46</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/1/6/#laws.8.3.0">1Sp2017 c 6 art 8 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/0/30/#laws.17.23.0">2021 c 30 art 17 s 23</a>,24; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2022/0/98/#laws.4.4.0">2022 c 98 art 4 s 4</a>,5</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245.468">
        <h1 class="shn">245.468 EDUCATION AND PREVENTION SERVICES.</h1>
        <p>By July 1, 1988, county boards must provide or contract for education and prevention services to adults residing in the county. Education and prevention services must be designed to:</p>
        <p>(1) convey information regarding mental illness and treatment resources to the general public and special high-risk target groups;</p>
        <p>(2) increase understanding and acceptance of problems associated with mental illness;</p>
        <p>(3) improve people's skills in dealing with high-risk situations known to have an impact on adults' mental health functioning;</p>
        <p>(4) prevent development or deepening of mental illness; and</p>
        <p>(5) refer adults with additional mental health needs to appropriate mental health services.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245.468.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/403/#laws.2.23.0">1987 c 403 art 2 s 23</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/282/#laws.4.14.0">1989 c 282 art 4 s 14</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245.4682">
        <h1 class="shn">245.4682 MENTAL HEALTH SERVICE DELIVERY AND FINANCE REFORM.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4682.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Policy.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">The commissioner of human services shall undertake a series of reforms to address the underlying structural, financial, and organizational problems in Minnesota's mental health system with the goal of improving the availability, quality, and accountability of mental health care within the state.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4682.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">General provisions.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) In the design and implementation of reforms to the mental health system, the commissioner shall:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) consult with consumers, families, counties, tribes, advocates, providers, and other stakeholders;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) bring to the legislature, and the State Advisory Council on Mental Health, by January 15, 2008, recommendations for legislation to update the role of counties and to clarify the case management roles, functions, and decision-making authority of health plans and counties, and to clarify county retention of the responsibility for the delivery of social services as required under subdivision 3, paragraph (a);</p>
          <p class="in">(3) withhold implementation of any recommended changes in case management roles, functions, and decision-making authority until after the release of the report due January 15, 2008;</p>
          <p class="in">(4) ensure continuity of care for persons affected by these reforms including ensuring client choice of provider by requiring broad provider networks and developing mechanisms to facilitate a smooth transition of service responsibilities;</p>
          <p class="in">(5) provide accountability for the efficient and effective use of public and private resources in achieving positive outcomes for consumers;</p>
          <p class="in">(6) ensure client access to applicable protections and appeals; and</p>
          <p class="in">(7) make budget transfers necessary to implement the reallocation of services and client responsibilities between counties and health care programs that do not increase the state and county costs and efficiently allocate state funds.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) When making transfers under paragraph (a) necessary to implement movement of responsibility for clients and services between counties and health care programs, the commissioner, in consultation with counties, shall ensure that any transfer of state grants to health care programs, including the value of case management transfer grants under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.0625#stat.256B.0625.20">256B.0625, subdivision 20</a>, does not exceed the value of the services being transferred for the latest 12-month period for which data is available. The commissioner may make quarterly adjustments based on the availability of additional data during the first four quarters after the transfers first occur. If case management transfer grants under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.0625#stat.256B.0625.20">256B.0625, subdivision 20</a>, are repealed and the value, based on the last year prior to repeal, exceeds the value of the services being transferred, the difference becomes an ongoing part of each county's adult mental health grants under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.4661">245.4661</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256K.10">256K.10</a>.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) This appropriation is not authorized to be expended after December 31, 2010, unless approved by the legislature.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4682.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Projects for coordination of care.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) Consistent with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.69">256B.69 </a>and chapter 256L, the commissioner is authorized to solicit, approve, and implement up to three projects to demonstrate the integration of physical and mental health services within prepaid health plans and their coordination with social services. The commissioner shall require that each project be based on locally defined partnerships that include at least one health maintenance organization, community integrated service network, or accountable provider network authorized and operating under chapter 62D, 62N, or 62T, or county-based purchasing entity under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.692">256B.692</a> that is eligible to contract with the commissioner as a prepaid health plan, and the county or counties within the service area. Counties shall retain responsibility and authority for social services in these locally defined partnerships.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) The commissioner, in consultation with consumers, families, and their representatives, shall:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) determine criteria for approving the projects and use those criteria to solicit proposals for preferred integrated networks. The commissioner must develop criteria to evaluate the partnership proposed by the county and prepaid health plan to coordinate access and delivery of services. The proposal must at a minimum address how the partnership will coordinate the provision of:</p>
          <p class="in">(i) client outreach and identification of health and social service needs paired with expedited access to appropriate resources;</p>
          <p class="in">(ii) activities to maintain continuity of health care coverage;</p>
          <p class="in">(iii) children's residential mental health treatment and treatment foster care;</p>
          <p class="in">(iv) court-ordered assessments and treatments;</p>
          <p class="in">(v) prepetition screening and commitments under chapter 253B;</p>
          <p class="in">(vi) assessment and treatment of children identified through mental health screening of child welfare and juvenile corrections cases;</p>
          <p class="in">(vii) home and community-based waiver services;</p>
          <p class="in">(viii) assistance with finding and maintaining employment;</p>
          <p class="in">(ix) housing; and</p>
          <p class="in">(x) transportation;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) determine specifications for contracts with prepaid health plans to improve the plan's ability to serve persons with mental health conditions, including specifications addressing:</p>
          <p class="in">(i) early identification and intervention of physical and behavioral health problems;</p>
          <p class="in">(ii) communication between the enrollee and the health plan;</p>
          <p class="in">(iii) facilitation of enrollment for persons who are also eligible for a Medicare special needs plan offered by the health plan;</p>
          <p class="in">(iv) risk screening procedures;</p>
          <p class="in">(v) health care coordination;</p>
          <p class="in">(vi) member services and access to applicable protections and appeal processes;</p>
          <p class="in">(vii) specialty provider networks;</p>
          <p class="in">(viii) transportation services;</p>
          <p class="in">(ix) treatment planning; and</p>
          <p class="in">(x) administrative simplification for providers;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) begin implementation of the projects no earlier than January 1, 2009, with not more than 40 percent of the statewide population included during calendar year 2009 and additional counties included in subsequent years;</p>
          <p class="in">(4) waive any administrative rule not consistent with the implementation of the projects;</p>
          <p class="in">(5) allow potential bidders at least 90 days to respond to the request for proposals; and</p>
          <p class="in">(6) conduct an independent evaluation to determine if mental health outcomes have improved in that county or counties according to measurable standards designed in consultation with the advisory body established under this subdivision and reviewed by the State Advisory Council on Mental Health.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) Notwithstanding any statute or administrative rule to the contrary, the commissioner may enroll all persons eligible for medical assistance with serious mental illness or emotional disturbance in the prepaid plan of their choice within the project service area unless:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) the individual is eligible for home and community-based services for persons with developmental disabilities and related conditions under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.092">256B.092</a>; or</p>
          <p class="in">(2) the individual has a basis for exclusion from the prepaid plan under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.69#stat.256B.69.4">256B.69, subdivision 4</a>, other than disability, mental illness, or emotional disturbance.</p>
          <p class="in">(d) The commissioner shall involve organizations representing persons with mental illness and their families in the development and distribution of information used to educate potential enrollees regarding their options for health care and mental health service delivery under this subdivision.</p>
          <p class="in">(e) If the person described in paragraph (c) does not elect to remain in fee-for-service medical assistance, or declines to choose a plan, the commissioner may preferentially assign that person to the prepaid plan participating in the preferred integrated network. The commissioner shall implement the enrollment changes within a project's service area on the timeline specified in that project's approved application.</p>
          <p class="in">(f) A person enrolled in a prepaid health plan under paragraphs (c) and (d) may disenroll from the plan at any time.</p>
          <p class="in">(g) The commissioner, in consultation with consumers, families, and their representatives, shall evaluate the projects begun in 2009, and shall refine the design of the service integration projects before expanding the projects. The commissioner shall report to the chairs of the legislative committees with jurisdiction over mental health services by March 1, 2008, on plans for evaluation of preferred integrated networks established under this subdivision.</p>
          <p class="in">(h) The commissioner shall apply for any federal waivers necessary to implement these changes.</p>
          <p class="in">(i) Payment for Medicaid service providers under this subdivision for the months of May and June will be made no earlier than July 1 of the same calendar year.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245.4682.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/147/#laws.8.5.0">2007 c 147 art 8 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/108/#laws.4.4.0">2013 c 108 art 4 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/158/#laws.2.47.0">2016 c 158 art 2 s 47</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/80/#laws.8.68.0">2024 c 80 art 8 s 68</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245.469">
        <h1 class="shn">245.469 EMERGENCY SERVICES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.469.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Availability of emergency services.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) County boards must provide or contract for enough emergency services within the county to meet the needs of adults, children, and families in the county who are experiencing an emotional crisis or mental illness. Emergency service providers must not delay the timely provision of emergency services to a client because of the unwillingness or inability of the client to pay for services. Emergency services must include assessment, crisis intervention, and appropriate case disposition. Emergency services must:</p>
          <p>(1) promote the safety and emotional stability of each client;</p>
          <p>(2) minimize further deterioration of each client;</p>
          <p>(3) help each client to obtain ongoing care and treatment;</p>
          <p>(4) prevent placement in settings that are more intensive, costly, or restrictive than necessary and appropriate to meet client needs; and</p>
          <p>(5) provide support, psychoeducation, and referrals to each client's family members, service providers, and other third parties on behalf of the client in need of emergency services.</p>
          <p>(b) If a county provides engagement services under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/253B.041">253B.041</a>, the county's emergency service providers must refer clients to engagement services when the client meets the criteria for engagement services.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.469.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Specific requirements.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The county board shall require that all service providers of emergency services to adults with mental illness provide immediate direct access to a mental health professional during regular business hours. For evenings, weekends, and holidays, the service may be by direct toll-free telephone access to a mental health professional, clinical trainee, or mental health practitioner.</p>
          <p>(b) The commissioner may waive the requirement in paragraph (a) that the evening, weekend, and holiday service be provided by a mental health professional, clinical trainee, or mental health practitioner if the county documents that:</p>
          <p>(1) mental health professionals, clinical trainees, or mental health practitioners are unavailable to provide this service;</p>
          <p>(2) services are provided by a designated person with training in human services who receives treatment supervision from a mental health professional; and</p>
          <p>(3) the service provider is not also the provider of fire and public safety emergency services.</p>
          <p>(c) The commissioner may waive the requirement in paragraph (b), clause (3), that the evening, weekend, and holiday service not be provided by the provider of fire and public safety emergency services if:</p>
          <p>(1) every person who will be providing the first telephone contact has received at least eight hours of training on emergency mental health services approved by the commissioner;</p>
          <p>(2) every person who will be providing the first telephone contact will annually receive at least four hours of continued training on emergency mental health services approved by the commissioner;</p>
          <p>(3) the local social service agency has provided public education about available emergency mental health services and can assure potential users of emergency services that their calls will be handled appropriately;</p>
          <p>(4) the local social service agency agrees to provide the commissioner with accurate data on the number of emergency mental health service calls received;</p>
          <p>(5) the local social service agency agrees to monitor the frequency and quality of emergency services; and</p>
          <p>(6) the local social service agency describes how it will comply with paragraph (d).</p>
          <p>(d) Whenever emergency service during nonbusiness hours is provided by anyone other than a mental health professional, a mental health professional must be available on call for an emergency assessment and crisis intervention services, and must be available for at least telephone consultation within 30 minutes.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.469.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Mental health crisis services.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of human services shall increase access to mental health crisis services for children and adults. In order to increase access, the commissioner must:</p>
          <p>(1) develop a central phone number where calls can be routed to the appropriate crisis services;</p>
          <p>(2) provide telephone consultation 24 hours a day to mobile crisis teams who are serving people with traumatic brain injury or intellectual disabilities who are experiencing a mental health crisis;</p>
          <p>(3) expand crisis services across the state, including rural areas of the state and examining access per population;</p>
          <p>(4) establish and implement state standards and requirements for crisis services as outlined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.0624">256B.0624</a>; and</p>
          <p>(5) provide grants to adult mental health initiatives, counties, tribes, or community mental health providers to establish new mental health crisis residential service capacity.</p>
          <p>Priority will be given to regions that do not have a mental health crisis residential services program, do not have an inpatient psychiatric unit within the region, do not have an inpatient psychiatric unit within 90 miles, or have a demonstrated need based on the number of crisis residential or intensive residential treatment beds available to meet the needs of the residents in the region. At least 50 percent of the funds must be distributed to programs in rural Minnesota. Grant funds may be used for start-up costs, including but not limited to renovations, furnishings, and staff training. Grant applications shall provide details on how the intended service will address identified needs and shall demonstrate collaboration with crisis teams, other mental health providers, hospitals, and police.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245.469.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/403/#laws.2.24.0">1987 c 403 art 2 s 24</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/689/#laws.2.81.0">1988 c 689 art 2 s 81</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/282/#laws.4.15.0">1989 c 282 art 4 s 15</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/568/#laws.5.3.0">1990 c 568 art 5 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/312/#laws.0.1.0">1991 c 312 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/0/71/#laws.2.42.0">2015 c 71 art 2 s 42</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/0/30/#laws.16.1.0">2021 c 30 art 16 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/70/#laws.17.4.0">2023 c 70 art 17 s 4</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245.470">
        <h1 class="shn">245.470 OUTPATIENT SERVICES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.470.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Availability of outpatient services.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) County boards must provide or contract for enough outpatient services within the county to meet the needs of adults with mental illness residing in the county. Services may be provided directly by the county through county-operated mental health clinics meeting the standards of chapter 245I; by contract with privately operated mental health clinics meeting the standards of chapter 245I; by contract with hospital mental health outpatient programs certified by the Joint Commission on Accreditation of Hospital Organizations; or by contract with a mental health professional. Clients may be required to pay a fee according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.481">245.481</a>. Outpatient services include:</p>
          <p>(1) conducting diagnostic assessments;</p>
          <p>(2) conducting psychological testing;</p>
          <p>(3) developing or modifying individual treatment plans;</p>
          <p>(4) making referrals and recommending placements as appropriate;</p>
          <p>(5) treating an adult's mental health needs through therapy;</p>
          <p>(6) prescribing and managing medication and evaluating the effectiveness of prescribed medication; and</p>
          <p>(7) preventing placement in settings that are more intensive, costly, or restrictive than necessary and appropriate to meet client needs.</p>
          <p>(b) County boards may request a waiver allowing outpatient services to be provided in a nearby trade area if it is determined that the client can best be served outside the county.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.470.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Specific requirements.</span></h2>
          <p>The county board shall require that all service providers of outpatient services:</p>
          <p>(1) meet the professional qualifications contained in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.461">245.461</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.486">245.486</a>;</p>
          <p>(2) use a multidisciplinary mental health professional staff including at a minimum, arrangements for psychiatric consultation, licensed psychologist consultation, and other necessary multidisciplinary mental health professionals;</p>
          <p>(3) develop individual treatment plans;</p>
          <p>(4) provide initial appointments within three weeks, except in emergencies where there must be immediate access as described in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.469">245.469</a>; and</p>
          <p>(5) establish fee schedules approved by the county board that are based on a client's ability to pay.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245.470.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/403/#laws.2.25.0">1987 c 403 art 2 s 25</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/282/#laws.4.16.0">1989 c 282 art 4 s 16</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/568/#laws.2.38.0">1990 c 568 art 2 s 38</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/255/#laws.0.19.0">1991 c 255 s 19</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/339/#laws.0.2.0">1993 c 339 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/79/#laws.7.2.0">2009 c 79 art 7 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/0/30/#laws.17.25.0">2021 c 30 art 17 s 25</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245.4705">
        <h1 class="shn">245.4705 EMPLOYMENT SUPPORT SERVICES AND PROGRAMS.</h1>
        <p>The commissioner of human services shall cooperate with the commissioner of employment and economic development in the operation of a statewide system, as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/268A.14">268A.14</a>, to reimburse providers for employment support services for persons with mental illness.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245.4705.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/223/#laws.2.36.0">1999 c 223 art 2 s 36</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/206/#laws.0.52.0">2004 c 206 s 52</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.245.471"><b>245.471</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/282/#laws.4.64.0">1989 c 282 art 4 s 64</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245.4711">
        <h1 class="shn">245.4711 CASE MANAGEMENT SERVICES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4711.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Availability of case management services.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) By January 1, 1989, the county board shall provide case management services for all adults with serious and persistent mental illness who are residents of the county and who request or consent to the services and to each adult for whom the court appoints a case manager. Staffing ratios must be sufficient to serve the needs of the clients. The case manager must meet the requirements in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.462#stat.245.462.4">245.462, subdivision 4</a>.</p>
          <p>(b) Case management services provided to adults with serious and persistent mental illness eligible for medical assistance must be billed to the medical assistance program under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.02#stat.256B.02.8">256B.02, subdivision 8</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.0625">256B.0625</a>.</p>
          <p>(c) Case management services are eligible for reimbursement under the medical assistance program. Costs associated with mentoring, supervision, and continuing education may be included in the reimbursement rate methodology used for case management services under the medical assistance program.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4711.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Notification and determination of case management eligibility.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The county board shall notify the adult of the adult's potential eligibility for case management services within five working days after receiving a request from an individual or a referral from a provider under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.467#stat.245.467.4">245.467, subdivision 4</a>. The county board shall send a written notice to the adult and the adult's representative, if any, that identifies the designated case management providers.</p>
          <p>(b) The county board must determine whether an adult who requests or is referred for case management services meets the criteria of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.462#stat.245.462.20">245.462, subdivision 20</a>, paragraph (c). If a diagnostic assessment is needed to make the determination, the county board shall offer to assist the adult in obtaining a diagnostic assessment. The county board shall notify, in writing, the adult and the adult's representative, if any, of the eligibility determination. If the adult is determined to be eligible for case management services, the county board shall refer the adult to the case management provider for case management services. If the adult is determined not to be eligible or refuses case management services, the local agency shall offer to refer the adult to a mental health provider or other appropriate service provider and to assist the adult in making an appointment with the provider of the adult's choice.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4711.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Duties of case manager.</span></h2>
          <p>Upon a determination of eligibility for case management services, and if the adult consents to the services, the case manager shall complete a written functional assessment according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.462#stat.245.462.11a">245.462, subdivision 11a</a>. The case manager shall develop an individual community support plan for the adult according to subdivision 4, paragraph (a), review the adult's progress, and monitor the provision of services. If services are to be provided in a host county that is not the county of financial responsibility, the case manager shall consult with the host county and obtain a letter demonstrating the concurrence of the host county regarding the provision of services.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4711.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Individual community support plan.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The case manager must develop an individual community support plan for each adult that incorporates the client's individual treatment plan. The individual treatment plan may not be a substitute for the development of an individual community support plan. The individual community support plan must be developed within 30 days of client intake and reviewed at least every 180 days after it is developed, unless the case manager receives a written request from the client or the client's family for a review of the plan every 90 days after it is developed. The case manager is responsible for developing the individual community support plan based on a diagnostic assessment and a functional assessment and for implementing and monitoring the delivery of services according to the individual community support plan. To the extent possible, the adult with serious and persistent mental illness, the person's family, advocates, service providers, and significant others must be involved in all phases of development and implementation of the individual community support plan.</p>
          <p>(b) The client's individual community support plan must state:</p>
          <p>(1) the goals of each service;</p>
          <p>(2) the activities for accomplishing each goal;</p>
          <p>(3) a schedule for each activity; and</p>
          <p>(4) the frequency of face-to-face contacts by the case manager, as appropriate to client need and the implementation of the individual community support plan.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4711.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Coordination between case manager and community support services.</span></h2>
          <p>The county board must establish procedures that ensure ongoing contact and coordination between the case manager and the community support services program as well as other mental health services.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4711.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/568/#laws.5.35.0">1990 c 568 art 5 s 35</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4711.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/568/#laws.5.35.0">1990 c 568 art 5 s 35</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4711.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/568/#laws.5.35.0">1990 c 568 art 5 s 35</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4711.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/93/#laws.0.4.0">1997 c 93 s 4</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245.4711.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/282/#laws.4.17.0">1989 c 282 art 4 s 17</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/568/#laws.5.4.0">1990 c 568 art 5 s 4</a>-6; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/292/#laws.6.5.0">1991 c 292 art 6 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/93/#laws.0.1.0">1997 c 93 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/245/#laws.5.4.0">1999 c 245 art 5 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2022/0/98/#laws.17.26.0">2022 c 98 art 17 s 26</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/50/#laws.1.3.0">2023 c 50 art 1 s 3</a>,4</p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">COMMUNITY SUPPORT AND DAY TREATMENT SERVICES</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245.4712">
        <h1 class="shn">245.4712 COMMUNITY SUPPORT AND DAY TREATMENT SERVICES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4712.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Availability of community support services.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) County boards must provide or contract for sufficient community support services within the county to meet the needs of adults with serious and persistent mental illness who are residents of the county. Adults may be required to pay a fee according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.481">245.481</a>. The community support services program must be designed to improve the ability of adults with serious and persistent mental illness to:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) find and maintain competitive employment;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) handle basic activities of daily living;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) participate in leisure time activities;</p>
          <p class="in">(4) set goals and plans; and</p>
          <p class="in">(5) obtain and maintain appropriate living arrangements.</p>
          <p class="in">The community support services program must also be designed to reduce the need for and use of more intensive, costly, or restrictive placements both in number of admissions and length of stay.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) Community support services are those services that are supportive in nature and not necessarily treatment oriented, and include:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) conducting outreach activities such as home visits, health and wellness checks, and problem solving;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) connecting people to resources to meet their basic needs;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) finding, securing, and supporting people in their housing;</p>
          <p class="in">(4) attaining and maintaining health insurance benefits;</p>
          <p class="in">(5) assisting with job applications, finding and maintaining employment, and securing a stable financial situation;</p>
          <p class="in">(6) fostering social support, including support groups, mentoring, peer support, and other efforts to prevent isolation and promote recovery; and</p>
          <p class="in">(7) educating about mental illness, treatment, and recovery.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) Community support services shall use all available funding streams. The county shall maintain the level of expenditures for this program, as required under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.4835">245.4835</a>. County boards must continue to provide funds for those services not covered by other funding streams and to maintain an infrastructure to carry out these services. The county is encouraged to fund evidence-based practices such as Individual Placement and Supported Employment and Illness Management and Recovery.</p>
          <p class="in">(d) The commissioner shall collect data on community support services programs, including, but not limited to, demographic information such as age, sex, race, the number of people served, and information related to housing, employment, hospitalization, symptoms, and satisfaction with services.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4712.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Day treatment services provided.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Day treatment services must be developed as a part of the community support services available to adults with serious and persistent mental illness residing in the county. Adults may be required to pay a fee according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.481">245.481</a>. Day treatment services must be designed to:</p>
          <p>(1) provide a structured environment for treatment;</p>
          <p>(2) provide support for residing in the community;</p>
          <p>(3) prevent placement in settings that are more intensive, costly, or restrictive than necessary and appropriate to meet client need;</p>
          <p>(4) coordinate with or be offered in conjunction with a local education agency's special education program; and</p>
          <p>(5) operate on a continuous basis throughout the year.</p>
          <p>(b) An adult day treatment program must comply with medical assistance requirements in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.0671#stat.256B.0671.3">256B.0671, subdivision 3</a>.</p>
          <p>(c) County boards may request a waiver from including day treatment services if they can document that:</p>
          <p>(1) an alternative plan of care exists through the county's community support services for clients who would otherwise need day treatment services;</p>
          <p>(2) day treatment, if included, would be duplicative of other components of the community support services; and</p>
          <p>(3) county demographics and geography make the provision of day treatment services cost ineffective and infeasible.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4712.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Benefits assistance.</span></h2>
          <p>The county board must offer to help adults with serious and persistent mental illness in applying for state and federal benefits, including Supplemental Security Income, medical assistance, Medicare, general assistance, and Minnesota supplemental aid. The help must be offered as part of the community support program available to adults with serious and persistent mental illness for whom the county is financially responsible and who may qualify for these benefits.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245.4712.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/568/#laws.5.7.0">1990 c 568 art 5 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/245/#laws.5.5.0">1999 c 245 art 5 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/147/#laws.8.6.0">2007 c 147 art 8 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/85/#laws.3.19.0">2013 c 85 art 3 s 19</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/275/#laws.1.36.0">2014 c 275 art 1 s 36</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/158/#laws.2.48.0">2016 c 158 art 2 s 48</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/0/30/#laws.17.26.0">2021 c 30 art 17 s 26</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245.472">
        <h1 class="shn">245.472 RESIDENTIAL TREATMENT SERVICES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.472.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Availability of residential treatment services.</span></h2>
          <p>By July 1, 1988, county boards must provide or contract for enough residential treatment services to meet the needs of all adults with mental illness residing in the county and needing this level of care. Residential treatment services include both intensive and structured residential treatment with length of stay based on client residential treatment need. Services must be as close to the county as possible. Residential treatment must be designed to:</p>
          <p>(1) prevent placement in settings that are more intensive, costly, or restrictive than necessary and appropriate to meet client needs;</p>
          <p>(2) help clients achieve the highest level of independent living;</p>
          <p>(3) help clients gain the necessary skills to function in a less structured setting; and</p>
          <p>(4) stabilize crisis admissions.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.472.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Specific requirements.</span></h2>
          <p>Providers of residential services must be licensed under chapter 245I or applicable rules adopted by the commissioner. Residential services must be provided under treatment supervision.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.472.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Transition to community.</span></h2>
          <p>Residential treatment programs must plan for and assist clients in making a transition from residential treatment facilities to other community-based services. In coordination with the client's case manager, if any, residential treatment facilities must also arrange for appropriate follow-up care in the community during the transition period. Before a client is discharged, the residential treatment facility must notify the client's case manager, so that the case manager can monitor and coordinate the transition and arrangements for the client's appropriate follow-up care in the community.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.472.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Admission, continued stay, and discharge criteria.</span></h2>
          <p>No later than January 1, 1992, the county board shall ensure that placement decisions for residential services are based on the clinical needs of the adult. The county board shall ensure that each entity under contract with the county to provide residential treatment services has admission, continued stay, discharge criteria and discharge planning criteria as part of the contract. Contracts shall specify specific responsibilities between the county and service providers to ensure comprehensive planning and continuity of care between needed services according to data privacy requirements. All contracts for the provision of residential services must include provisions guaranteeing clients the right to appeal under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.477">245.477</a> and to be advised of their appeal rights.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245.472.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/403/#laws.2.27.0">1987 c 403 art 2 s 27</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/689/#laws.2.84.0">1988 c 689 art 2 s 84</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/282/#laws.4.18.0">1989 c 282 art 4 s 18</a>,19; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/292/#laws.6.6.0">1991 c 292 art 6 s 6</a>,7; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/158/#laws.1.214.0">2016 c 158 art 1 s 214</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/0/30/#laws.17.27.0">2021 c 30 art 17 s 27</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245.473">
        <h1 class="shn">245.473 ACUTE CARE HOSPITAL INPATIENT SERVICES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.473.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Availability of acute care inpatient services.</span></h2>
          <p>By July 1, 1988, county boards must make available through contract or direct provision enough acute care hospital inpatient treatment services as close to the county as possible for adults with mental illness residing in the county. Acute care hospital inpatient treatment services must be designed to:</p>
          <p>(1) stabilize the medical and mental health condition for which admission is required;</p>
          <p>(2) improve functioning to the point where discharge to residential treatment or community-based mental health services is possible; and</p>
          <p>(3) facilitate appropriate referrals for follow-up mental health care in the community.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.473.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Specific requirements.</span></h2>
          <p>Providers of acute care hospital inpatient services must meet applicable standards established by the commissioners of health and human services.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.473.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Admission, continued stay, and discharge criteria.</span></h2>
          <p>No later than January 1, 1992, the county board shall ensure that placement decisions for acute care inpatient services are based on the clinical needs of the adult. The county board shall ensure that each entity under contract with the county to provide acute care hospital treatment services has admission, continued stay, discharge criteria and discharge planning criteria as part of the contract. Contracts shall specify specific responsibilities between the county and service providers to ensure comprehensive planning and continuity of care between needed services according to data privacy requirements. All contracts for the provision of acute care hospital inpatient treatment services must include provisions guaranteeing clients the right to appeal under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.477">245.477</a> and to be advised of their appeal rights.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.473.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Individual placement agreement.</span></h2>
          <p>Except for services reimbursed under chapters 256B and 256D, the county board shall enter into an individual placement agreement with a provider of acute care hospital inpatient treatment services to an adult eligible for services under this section. The agreement must specify the payment rate and the terms and conditions of county payment for the placement.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245.473.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/403/#laws.2.28.0">1987 c 403 art 2 s 28</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/282/#laws.4.20.0">1989 c 282 art 4 s 20</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/292/#laws.6.8.0">1991 c 292 art 6 s 8</a>,9</p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.245.474"><b>245.474</b> [Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/246C.12">246C.12</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.245.475"><b>245.475</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/282/#laws.4.64.0">1989 c 282 art 4 s 64</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245.476">
        <h1 class="shn">245.476 SCREENING FOR INPATIENT AND RESIDENTIAL TREATMENT.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.476.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/292/#laws.6.59.0">1991 c 292 art 6 s 59</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.476.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/292/#laws.6.59.0">1991 c 292 art 6 s 59</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.476.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/292/#laws.6.59.0">1991 c 292 art 6 s 59</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.476.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/337/#laws.0.20.0">1993 c 337 s 20</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.476.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Report on preadmission screening.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall review the statutory preadmission screening requirements for psychiatric hospitalization, both in the regional treatment centers and other hospitals, to determine if changes in preadmission screening are needed. The commissioner shall deliver a report of the review to the legislature by January 31, 1990.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245.476.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/403/#laws.2.31.0">1987 c 403 art 2 s 31</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/689/#laws.2.87.0">1988 c 689 art 2 s 87</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/282/#laws.4.22.0">1989 c 282 art 4 s 22</a>-24; art 6 s 4</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245.477">
        <h1 class="shn">245.477 APPEALS.</h1>
        <p>Any adult who requests mental health services under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.461">245.461</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.486">245.486</a> must be advised of services available and the right to appeal at the time of the request and each time the individual community support plan or individual treatment plan is reviewed. Any adult whose request for mental health services under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.461">245.461 </a>to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.486">245.486</a> is denied, not acted upon with reasonable promptness, or whose services are suspended, reduced, or terminated by action or inaction for which the county board is responsible under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.461">245.461</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.486">245.486</a> may contest that action or inaction before the state agency as specified in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256.045">256.045</a>. The commissioner shall monitor the nature and frequency of administrative appeals under this section.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245.477.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/403/#laws.2.32.0">1987 c 403 art 2 s 32</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/689/#laws.2.88.0">1988 c 689 art 2 s 88</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/282/#laws.4.25.0">1989 c 282 art 4 s 25</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2022/0/98/#laws.17.26.0">2022 c 98 art 17 s 26</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/50/#laws.1.5.0">2023 c 50 art 1 s 5</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.245.478"><b>245.478</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/14/#laws.11.12.0">1Sp2003 c 14 art 11 s 12</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245.479">
        <h1 class="shn">245.479 COUNTY OF FINANCIAL RESPONSIBILITY.</h1>
        <p>For purposes of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.461">245.461</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.486">245.486</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.487">245.487</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.4889">245.4889</a>, the county of financial responsibility is determined under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256G.02#stat.256G.02.4">256G.02, subdivision 4</a>. Disputes between counties regarding financial responsibility must be resolved by the commissioner in accordance with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256G.09">256G.09</a>.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245.479.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/403/#laws.2.35.0">1987 c 403 art 2 s 35</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/689/#laws.2.92.0">1988 c 689 art 2 s 92</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/282/#laws.4.28.0">1989 c 282 art 4 s 28</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/292/#laws.6.58.0">1991 c 292 art 6 s 58</a> subd 1; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/14/#laws.11.11.0">1Sp2003 c 14 art 11 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/147/#laws.8.38.0">2007 c 147 art 8 s 38</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.245.48"><b>245.48</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/264/#laws.3.51.0">1995 c 264 art 3 s 51</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245.481">
