        <h1 class="shn">245.481 FEES FOR MENTAL HEALTH SERVICES.</h1>
        <p>A client or, in the case of a child, the child or the child's parent may be required to pay a fee for mental health services provided under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.461">245.461</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.486">245.486</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.487">245.487</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.4889">245.4889</a>. The fee must be based on the person's ability to pay according to the fee schedule adopted by the county board. In adopting the fee schedule for mental health services, the county board may adopt the fee schedule provided by the commissioner or adopt a fee schedule recommended by the county board and approved by the commissioner. Agencies or individuals under contract with a county board to provide mental health services under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.461">245.461</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.486">245.486</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.487">245.487</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.4889">245.4889</a> must not charge clients whose mental health services are paid wholly or in part from public funds fees which exceed the county board's adopted fee schedule. This section does not apply to regional treatment center fees, which are governed by sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/246.50">246.50</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/246.55">246.55</a>.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245.481.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/282/#laws.4.30.0">1989 c 282 art 4 s 30</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/292/#laws.6.58.0">1991 c 292 art 6 s 58</a> subd 1; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/14/#laws.11.11.0">1Sp2003 c 14 art 11 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/147/#laws.8.38.0">2007 c 147 art 8 s 38</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245.482">
        <h1 class="shn">245.482 REPORTING AND EVALUATION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.482.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Reports.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall specify requirements for reports, including quarterly fiscal reports, according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256.01#stat.256.01.2">256.01, subdivision 2</a>, paragraph (o).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.482.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Fiscal reports.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall develop a unified format for quarterly fiscal reports that will include information that the commissioner determines necessary to carry out sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.461">245.461</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.486">245.486</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.487">245.487</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.4889">245.4889</a>. The county board shall submit a completed fiscal report in the required format no later than 30 days after the end of each quarter.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.482.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Program reports.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall develop unified formats for reporting, which will include information that the commissioner determines necessary to carry out sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.461">245.461</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.486">245.486</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.487">245.487</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.4889">245.4889</a>. The county board shall submit completed program reports in the required format according to the reporting schedule developed by the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.482.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Provider reports.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may develop formats and procedures for direct reporting from providers to the commissioner to include information that the commissioner determines necessary to carry out sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.461">245.461</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.486">245.486</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.487">245.487</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.4889">245.4889</a>. In particular, the provider reports must include aggregate information by county of residence about mental health services paid for by funding sources other than counties.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.482.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/158/#laws.1.215.0">2016 c 158 art 1 s 215</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.482.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Inaccurate or incomplete reports.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall promptly notify a county or provider if a required report is clearly inaccurate or incomplete. The commissioner may delay all or part of a mental health fund payment if an appropriately completed report is not received as required by this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.482.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Statewide evaluation.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall use the county and provider reports required by this section to complete the statewide report required in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.461">245.461</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.487">245.487</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245.482.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/403/#laws.2.36.0">1987 c 403 art 2 s 36</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/689/#laws.2.93.0">1988 c 689 art 2 s 93</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/89/#laws.0.1.0">1989 c 89 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/282/#laws.4.31.0">1989 c 282 art 4 s 31</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/292/#laws.6.58.0">1991 c 292 art 6 s 58</a> subd 1; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/465/#laws.3.17.0">1994 c 465 art 3 s 17</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/14/#laws.11.11.0">1Sp2003 c 14 art 11 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/147/#laws.8.38.0">2007 c 147 art 8 s 38</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/107/#laws.4.3.0">2013 c 107 art 4 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/0/78/#laws.4.61.0">2015 c 78 art 4 s 61</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245.483">
        <h1 class="shn">245.483 TERMINATION OR RETURN OF AN ALLOCATION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.483.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Funds not properly used.</span></h2>
          <p>If the commissioner determines that a county is not meeting the requirements of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.461">245.461</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.486">245.486</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.487">245.487</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.4889">245.4889</a>, or that funds are not being used according to the approved mental health plan, all or part of the mental health funds may be terminated upon 30 days' notice to the county board. The commissioner may require repayment of any funds not used according to the approved mental health plan. If the commissioner receives a written appeal from the county board within the 30-day period, opportunity for a hearing under the Minnesota Administrative Procedure Act, chapter 14, must be provided before the allocation is terminated or is required to be repaid. The 30-day period begins when the county board receives the commissioner's notice by certified mail.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.483.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Use of returned funds.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may reallocate the funds returned.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.483.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Delayed payments.</span></h2>
          <p>If the commissioner finds that a county board or its contractors are not in compliance with the approved mental health plan or sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.461">245.461</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.486">245.486</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.487">245.487</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.4889">245.4889</a>, the commissioner may delay payment of all or part of the quarterly mental health funds until the county board and its contractors meet the requirements. The commissioner shall not delay a payment longer than three months without first issuing a notice under subdivision 2 that all or part of the allocation will be terminated or required to be repaid. After this notice is issued, the commissioner may continue to delay the payment until completion of the hearing in subdivision 2.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.483.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">State assumption of responsibility.</span></h2>
          <p>If the commissioner determines that services required by sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.461">245.461</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.486">245.486</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.487">245.487</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.4889">245.4889</a> will not be provided by the county board in the manner or to the extent required by sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.461">245.461</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.486">245.486</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.487">245.487</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.4889">245.4889</a>, the commissioner shall contract directly with providers to ensure that clients receive appropriate services. In this case, the commissioner shall use the county's mental health funds to the extent necessary to carry out the county's responsibilities under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.461">245.461</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.486">245.486</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.487">245.487</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.4889">245.4889</a>. The commissioner shall work with the county board to allow for a return of authority and responsibility to the county board as soon as compliance with sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.461">245.461</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.486">245.486</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.487">245.487</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.4889">245.4889</a> can be assured.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245.483.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/403/#laws.2.37.0">1987 c 403 art 2 s 37</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/282/#laws.4.32.0">1989 c 282 art 4 s 32</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/94/#laws.0.24.0">1991 c 94 s 24</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/292/#laws.6.58.0">1991 c 292 art 6 s 58</a> subd 1; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/14/#laws.11.11.0">1Sp2003 c 14 art 11 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/98/#laws.3.7.0">2005 c 98 art 3 s 7</a>,8,24; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/147/#laws.8.38.0">2007 c 147 art 8 s 38</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245.4835">
        <h1 class="shn">245.4835 COUNTY MAINTENANCE OF EFFORT.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4835.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Required expenditures.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Counties must maintain a level of expenditures for mental health services under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.461">245.461</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.484">245.484</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.487">245.487</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.4889">245.4889</a> so that each year's county expenditures are at least equal to that county's average expenditures for those services for calendar years 2004 and 2005. The commissioner will adjust each county's base level for minimum expenditures in each year by the amount of any increase or decrease in that county's state grants or other noncounty revenues for mental health services under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.461">245.461</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.484">245.484</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.487">245.487</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.4889">245.4889</a>.</p>
          <p>(b) In order to simplify administration and improve budgeting predictability, the commissioner:</p>
          <p>(1) shall use each county's actual prior year revenues to adjust the county's minimum required expenditures for the coming year;</p>
          <p>(2) may use more current information regarding major changes in revenues if the change is known early enough to allow counties time to adjust their budgets;</p>
          <p>(3) shall allocate each county's revenues proportionally across applicable expenditures; and</p>
          <p>(4) may adjust a county's base if the county's population is substantially declining and the county's per capita mental health expenditures are substantially higher than the state average, and the commissioner has determined that mental health services in that county would not be negatively impacted.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4835.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Failure to maintain expenditures.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) If a county does not comply with subdivision 1, the commissioner shall require the county to develop a corrective action plan according to a format and timeline established by the commissioner. If the commissioner determines that a county has not developed an acceptable corrective action plan within the required timeline, or that the county is not in compliance with an approved corrective action plan, the protections provided to that county under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.485">245.485</a> do not apply.</p>
          <p>(b) The commissioner shall consider the following factors to determine whether to approve a county's corrective action plan:</p>
          <p>(1) the degree to which a county is maximizing revenues for mental health services from noncounty sources;</p>
          <p>(2) the degree to which a county is expanding use of alternative services that meet mental health needs, but do not count as mental health services within existing reporting systems. If approved by the commissioner, the alternative services must be included in the county's base as well as subsequent years. The commissioner's approval for alternative services must be based on the following criteria:</p>
          <p>(i) the service must be provided to children with emotional disturbance or adults with mental illness;</p>
          <p>(ii) the services must be based on an individual treatment plan or individual community support plan as defined in the Comprehensive Mental Health Act; and</p>
          <p>(iii) the services must be supervised by a mental health professional and provided by staff who meet the staff qualifications defined in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.0943#stat.256B.0943.7">256B.0943, subdivision 7</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.0623">256B.0623</a>, subdivision 5.</p>
          <p>(c) Additional county expenditures to make up for the prior year's underspending may be spread out over a two-year period.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245.4835.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2006/0/282/#laws.16.4.0">2006 c 282 art 16 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/147/#laws.8.38.0">2007 c 147 art 8 s 38</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/167/#laws.0.2.0">2009 c 167 s 2</a>,3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/187/#laws.1.35.0">2012 c 187 art 1 s 35</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/0/40/#laws.1.48.0">2017 c 40 art 1 s 48</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2022/0/98/#laws.17.26.0">2022 c 98 art 17 s 26</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/50/#laws.1.6.0">2023 c 50 art 1 s 6</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245.484">
        <h1 class="shn">245.484 RULES.</h1>
        <p>The commissioner shall adopt emergency rules to govern implementation of case management services for eligible children in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.4881">245.4881</a> and professional home-based family treatment services for medical assistance eligible children, in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.4884#stat.245.4884.3">245.4884, subdivision 3</a>, by January 1, 1992, and must adopt permanent rules by January 1, 1993.</p>
        <p>The commissioner shall adopt permanent rules as necessary to carry out sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.461">245.461</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.486">245.486</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.487">245.487</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.4889">245.4889</a>. The commissioner shall reassign agency staff as necessary to meet this deadline.</p>
        <p>By January 1, 1994, the commissioner shall adopt permanent rules specifying program requirements for family community support services.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245.484.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/403/#laws.2.38.0">1987 c 403 art 2 s 38</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/282/#laws.4.33.0">1989 c 282 art 4 s 33</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/292/#laws.6.10.0">1991 c 292 art 6 s 10</a>,58 subd 1; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/571/#laws.10.10.0">1992 c 571 art 10 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/1/1/#laws.7.7.0">1Sp1993 c 1 art 7 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/14/#laws.11.11.0">1Sp2003 c 14 art 11 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/147/#laws.8.38.0">2007 c 147 art 8 s 38</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245.485">
        <h1 class="shn">245.485 WHERE A CLAIM MUST BE BROUGHT.</h1>
        <p>Sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.461">245.461</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.484">245.484</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.487">245.487</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.4889">245.4889</a> do not independently establish a right of action on behalf of recipients of services or service providers against a county board or the commissioner. A claim for monetary damages must be brought under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/3.736">3.736</a> or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/3.751">3.751</a>.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245.485.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/403/#laws.2.39.0">1987 c 403 art 2 s 39</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/282/#laws.4.34.0">1989 c 282 art 4 s 34</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/292/#laws.6.58.0">1991 c 292 art 6 s 58</a> subd 1; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/14/#laws.11.11.0">1Sp2003 c 14 art 11 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/147/#laws.8.38.0">2007 c 147 art 8 s 38</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245.486">
        <h1 class="shn">245.486 LIMITED APPROPRIATIONS.</h1>
        <p>Nothing in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.461">245.461</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.485">245.485</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.487">245.487</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.4889">245.4889</a> shall be construed to require the commissioner or county boards to fund services beyond the limits of legislative appropriations.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245.486.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/403/#laws.2.40.0">1987 c 403 art 2 s 40</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/282/#laws.4.35.0">1989 c 282 art 4 s 35</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/292/#laws.6.58.0">1991 c 292 art 6 s 58</a> subd 1; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/14/#laws.11.11.0">1Sp2003 c 14 art 11 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/147/#laws.8.38.0">2007 c 147 art 8 s 38</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.245.4861"><b>245.4861</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/262/#laws.3.18.0">2014 c 262 art 3 s 18</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245.4862">
        <h1 class="shn">245.4862 MENTAL HEALTH URGENT CARE AND PSYCHIATRIC CONSULTATION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4862.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Mental health urgent care and psychiatric consultation.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall include mental health urgent care and psychiatric consultation services as part of, but not limited to, the redesign of six community-based behavioral health hospitals and the Anoka-Metro Regional Treatment Center. These services must not duplicate existing services in the region, and must be implemented as specified in subdivisions 3 to 7.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4862.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>For purposes of this section:</p>
          <p>(a) Mental health urgent care includes:</p>
          <p>(1) initial mental health screening;</p>
          <p>(2) mobile crisis assessment and intervention;</p>
          <p>(3) rapid access to psychiatry, including psychiatric evaluation, initial treatment, and short-term psychiatry;</p>
          <p>(4) nonhospital crisis stabilization residential beds; and</p>
          <p>(5) health care navigator services that include, but are not limited to, assisting uninsured individuals in obtaining health care coverage.</p>
          <p>(b) Psychiatric consultation services includes psychiatric consultation to primary care practitioners.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4862.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Rapid access to psychiatry.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall develop rapid access to psychiatric services based on the following criteria:</p>
          <p>(1) the individuals who receive the psychiatric services must be at risk of hospitalization and otherwise unable to receive timely services;</p>
          <p>(2) where clinically appropriate, the service may be provided via interactive video where the service is provided in conjunction with an emergency room, a local crisis service, or a primary care or behavioral care practitioner; and</p>
          <p>(3) the commissioner may integrate rapid access to psychiatry with the psychiatric consultation services in subdivision 4.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4862.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Collaborative psychiatric consultation.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner shall establish a collaborative psychiatric consultation service based on the following criteria:</p>
          <p>(1) the service may be available via telephone, interactive video, email, or other means of communication to emergency rooms, local crisis services, mental health professionals, and primary care practitioners, including pediatricians;</p>
          <p>(2) the service shall be provided by a multidisciplinary team including, at a minimum, a child and adolescent psychiatrist, an adult psychiatrist, and a licensed clinical social worker;</p>
          <p>(3) the service shall include a triage-level assessment to determine the most appropriate response to each request, including appropriate referrals to other mental health professionals, as well as provision of rapid psychiatric access when other appropriate services are not available;</p>
          <p>(4) the first priority for this service is to provide the consultations required under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.0625#stat.256B.0625.13j">256B.0625, subdivision 13j</a>; and</p>
          <p>(5) the service must encourage use of cognitive and behavioral therapies and other evidence-based treatments in addition to or in place of medication, where appropriate.</p>
          <p>(b) The commissioner shall appoint an interdisciplinary work group to establish appropriate medication and psychotherapy protocols to guide the consultative process, including consultation with the Drug Utilization Review Board, as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.0625#stat.256B.0625.13j">256B.0625, subdivision 13j</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4862.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Phased availability.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner may phase in the availability of mental health urgent care services based on the limits of appropriations and the commissioner's determination of level of need and cost-effectiveness.</p>
          <p>(b) For subdivisions 3 and 4, the first phase must focus on adults in Hennepin and Ramsey Counties and children statewide who are affected by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.0625">256B.0625</a>, subdivision 13j, and must include tracking of costs for the services provided and associated impacts on utilization of inpatient, emergency room, and other services.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4862.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Limited appropriations.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall maximize use of available health care coverage for the services provided under this section. The commissioner's responsibility to provide these services for individuals without health care coverage must not exceed the appropriations for this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4862.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Flexible implementation.</span></h2>
          <p>To implement this section, the commissioner shall select the structure and funding method that is the most cost-effective for each county or group of counties. This may include grants, contracts, service agreements with the Direct Care and Treatment executive board, and public-private partnerships. Where feasible, the commissioner shall make any grants under this section a part of the integrated adult mental health initiative grants under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.4661">245.4661</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245.4862.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/200/#laws.1.1.0">2010 c 200 art 1 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/79/#laws.8.9.0">2024 c 79 art 8 s 9</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245.4863">
        <h1 class="shn">245.4863 INTEGRATED CO-OCCURRING DISORDER TREATMENT.</h1>
        <p>(a) The commissioner shall require individuals who perform substance use disorder assessments to screen clients for co-occurring mental health disorders, and staff who perform mental health diagnostic assessments to screen for co-occurring substance use disorders. Screening tools must be approved by the commissioner. If a client screens positive for a co-occurring mental health or substance use disorder, the individual performing the screening must document what actions will be taken in response to the results and whether further assessments must be performed.</p>
        <p>(b) Notwithstanding paragraph (a), screening is not required when:</p>
        <p>(1) the presence of co-occurring disorders was documented for the client in the past 12 months;</p>
        <p>(2) the client is currently receiving co-occurring disorders treatment;</p>
        <p>(3) the client is being referred for co-occurring disorders treatment; or</p>
        <p>(4) a mental health professional who is competent to perform diagnostic assessments of co-occurring disorders is performing a diagnostic assessment to identify whether the client may have co-occurring mental health and substance use disorders. If an individual is identified to have co-occurring mental health and substance use disorders, the assessing mental health professional must document what actions will be taken to address the client's co-occurring disorders.</p>
        <p>(c) The commissioner shall adopt rules as necessary to implement this section. The commissioner shall ensure that the rules are effective on July 1, 2013, thereby establishing a certification process for integrated dual disorder treatment providers and a system through which individuals receive integrated dual diagnosis treatment if assessed as having both a substance use disorder and either a serious mental illness or emotional disturbance.</p>
        <p>(d) The commissioner shall apply for any federal waivers necessary to secure, to the extent allowed by law, federal financial participation for the provision of integrated dual diagnosis treatment to persons with co-occurring disorders.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245.4863.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/1/9/#laws.8.9.0">1Sp2011 c 9 art 8 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/106/#laws.0.1.0">2016 c 106 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/0/30/#laws.17.28.0">2021 c 30 art 17 s 28</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2022/0/98/#laws.4.51.0">2022 c 98 art 4 s 51</a></p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">CHILDREN'S MENTAL HEALTH ACT</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245.487">
        <h1 class="shn">245.487 CITATION; DECLARATION OF POLICY; MISSION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.487.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Citation.</span></h2>
          <p>Sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.487">245.487</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.4889">245.4889</a> may be cited as the "Minnesota Comprehensive Children's Mental Health Act."</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.487.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Findings.</span></h2>
          <p>The legislature finds there is a need for further development of existing clinical services for emotionally disturbed children and their families and the creation of new services for this population. Although the services specified in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.487">245.487</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.4889">245.4889</a> are mental health services, sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.487">245.487</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.4889">245.4889</a> emphasize the need for a child-oriented and family-oriented approach of therapeutic programming and the need for continuity of care with other community agencies. At the same time, sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.487">245.487</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.4889">245.4889</a> emphasize the importance of developing special mental health expertise in children's mental health services because of the unique needs of this population.</p>
          <p>Nothing in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.487">245.487</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.4889">245.4889</a> shall be construed to abridge the authority of the court to make dispositions under chapter 260, but the mental health services due any child with serious and persistent mental illness, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.462#stat.245.462.20">245.462, subdivision 20</a>, or with severe emotional disturbance, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.4871#stat.245.4871.6">245.4871, subdivision 6</a>, shall be made a part of any disposition affecting that child.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.487.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Mission of children's mental health service system.</span></h2>
          <p>As part of the comprehensive children's mental health system established under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.487">245.487</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.4889">245.4889</a>, the commissioner of human services shall create and ensure a unified, accountable, comprehensive children's mental health service system that is consistent with the provision of public social services for children and that:</p>
          <p>(1) identifies children who are eligible for mental health services;</p>
          <p>(2) makes preventive services available to all children;</p>
          <p>(3) assures access to a continuum of services that:</p>
          <p>(i) educate the community about the mental health needs of children;</p>
          <p>(ii) address the unique physical, emotional, social, and educational needs of children;</p>
          <p>(iii) are coordinated with the range of social and human services provided to children and their families by the Departments of Education, Human Services, Health, and Corrections;</p>
          <p>(iv) are appropriate to the developmental needs of children; and</p>
          <p>(v) are sensitive to cultural differences and special needs;</p>
          <p>(4) includes early screening and prompt intervention to:</p>
          <p>(i) identify and treat the mental health needs of children in the least restrictive setting appropriate to their needs; and</p>
          <p>(ii) prevent further deterioration;</p>
          <p>(5) provides mental health services to children and their families in the context in which the children live and go to school;</p>
          <p>(6) addresses the unique problems of paying for mental health services for children, including:</p>
          <p>(i) access to private insurance coverage; and</p>
          <p>(ii) public funding;</p>
          <p>(7) includes the child and the child's family in planning the child's program of mental health services, unless clinically inappropriate to the child's needs; and</p>
          <p>(8) when necessary, assures a smooth transition from mental health services appropriate for a child to mental health services needed by a person who is at least 18 years of age.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.487.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/262/#laws.3.18.0">2014 c 262 art 3 s 18</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.487.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/262/#laws.3.18.0">2014 c 262 art 3 s 18</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.487.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Funding from the federal government and other sources.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall seek and apply for federal and other nonstate, nonlocal government funding for mental health services specified in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.487">245.487</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.4889">245.4889</a>, in order to maximize nonstate, nonlocal dollars for these services.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.487.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Diagnostic codes list.</span></h2>
          <p>By July 1, 2013, the commissioner of human services shall develop a list of diagnostic codes to define the range of child and adult mental illnesses for the statewide mental health system. The commissioner may use the International Classification of Diseases (ICD); the American Psychiatric Association's Diagnostic and Statistical Manual (DSM); or a combination of both to develop the list. The commissioner shall establish an advisory committee, comprising mental health professional associations, counties, tribes, managed care organizations, state agencies, and consumer organizations that shall advise the commissioner regarding development of the diagnostic codes list. The commissioner shall annually notify providers of changes to the list.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245.487.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/282/#laws.4.37.0">1989 c 282 art 4 s 37</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/568/#laws.5.9.0">1990 c 568 art 5 s 9</a>,10; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/199/#laws.2.1.0">1991 c 199 art 2 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/292/#laws.6.11.0">1991 c 292 art 6 s 11</a>,12,58 subd 1; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.16.13.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 16 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/130/#laws.0.12.0">2003 c 130 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/14/#laws.11.11.0">1Sp2003 c 14 art 11 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/147/#laws.8.38.0">2007 c 147 art 8 s 38</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/216/#laws.12.3.0">2012 c 216 art 12 s 3</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245.4871">
        <h1 class="shn">245.4871 DEFINITIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4871.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>The definitions in this section apply to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.487">245.487</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.4889">245.4889</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4871.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Acute care hospital inpatient treatment.</span></h2>
          <p>"Acute care hospital inpatient treatment" means short-term medical, nursing, and psychosocial services provided in an acute care hospital licensed under chapter 144.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4871.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Case management services.</span></h2>
          <p>"Case management services" means activities that are coordinated with the family community support services and are designed to help the child with severe emotional disturbance and the child's family obtain needed mental health services, social services, educational services, health services, vocational services, recreational services, and related services in the areas of volunteer services, advocacy, transportation, and legal services. Case management services include assisting in obtaining a comprehensive diagnostic assessment, developing an individual family community support plan, and assisting the child and the child's family in obtaining needed services by coordination with other agencies and assuring continuity of care. Case managers must assess and reassess the delivery, appropriateness, and effectiveness of services over time.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4871.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Case management service provider.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) "Case management service provider" means a case manager or case manager associate employed by the county or other entity authorized by the county board to provide case management services specified in subdivision 3 for the child with severe emotional disturbance and the child's family.</p>
          <p>(b) A case manager must:</p>
          <p>(1) have experience and training in working with children;</p>
          <p>(2) have at least a bachelor's degree in one of the behavioral sciences or a related field including, but not limited to, social work, psychology, or nursing from an accredited college or university or meet the requirements of paragraph (d);</p>
          <p>(3) have experience and training in identifying and assessing a wide range of children's needs;</p>
          <p>(4) be knowledgeable about local community resources and how to use those resources for the benefit of children and their families; and</p>
          <p>(5) meet the supervision and continuing education requirements of paragraphs (e), (f), and (g), as applicable.</p>
          <p>(c) A case manager may be a member of any professional discipline that is part of the local system of care for children established by the county board.</p>
          <p>(d) A case manager without a bachelor's degree must meet one of the requirements in clauses (1) to (3):</p>
          <p>(1) have three or four years of experience as a case manager associate;</p>
          <p>(2) be a registered nurse without a bachelor's degree who has a combination of specialized training in psychiatry and work experience consisting of community interaction and involvement or community discharge planning in a mental health setting totaling three years; or</p>
          <p>(3) be a person who qualified as a case manager under the 1998 Department of Human Services waiver provision and meets the continuing education, supervision, and mentoring requirements in this section.</p>
          <p>(e) A case manager with at least 2,000 hours of supervised experience in the delivery of mental health services to children must receive regular ongoing supervision and clinical supervision totaling 38 hours per year, of which at least one hour per month must be clinical supervision regarding individual service delivery with a case management supervisor. The other 26 hours of supervision may be provided by a case manager with two years of experience. Group supervision may not constitute more than one-half of the required supervision hours.</p>
          <p>(f) A case manager without 2,000 hours of supervised experience in the delivery of mental health services to children with emotional disturbance must:</p>
          <p>(1) begin 40 hours of training approved by the commissioner of human services in case management skills and in the characteristics and needs of children with severe emotional disturbance before beginning to provide case management services; and</p>
          <p>(2) receive clinical supervision regarding individual service delivery from a mental health professional at least one hour each week until the requirement of 2,000 hours of experience is met.</p>
          <p>(g) A case manager who is not licensed, registered, or certified by a health-related licensing board must receive 30 hours of continuing education and training in severe emotional disturbance and mental health services every two years.</p>
          <p>(h) Clinical supervision must be documented in the child's record. When the case manager is not a mental health professional, the county board must provide or contract for needed clinical supervision.</p>
          <p>(i) The county board must ensure that the case manager has the freedom to access and coordinate the services within the local system of care that are needed by the child.</p>
          <p>(j) A case manager associate (CMA) must:</p>
          <p>(1) work under the direction of a case manager or case management supervisor;</p>
          <p>(2) be at least 21 years of age;</p>
          <p>(3) have at least a high school diploma or its equivalent; and</p>
          <p>(4) meet one of the following criteria:</p>
          <p>(i) have an associate of arts degree in one of the behavioral sciences or human services;</p>
          <p>(ii) be a registered nurse without a bachelor's degree;</p>
          <p>(iii) have three years of life experience as a primary caregiver to a child with serious emotional disturbance as defined in subdivision 6 within the previous ten years;</p>
          <p>(iv) have 6,000 hours work experience as a nondegreed state hospital technician; or</p>
          <p>(v) have 6,000 hours of supervised work experience in the delivery of mental health services to children with emotional disturbances; hours worked as a mental health behavioral aide I or II under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.0943#stat.256B.0943.7">256B.0943, subdivision 7</a>, may count toward the 6,000 hours of supervised work experience.</p>
          <p>Individuals meeting one of the criteria in items (i) to (iv) may qualify as a case manager after four years of supervised work experience as a case manager associate. Individuals meeting the criteria in item (v) may qualify as a case manager after three years of supervised experience as a case manager associate.</p>
          <p>(k) Case manager associates must meet the following supervision, mentoring, and continuing education requirements;</p>
          <p>(1) have 40 hours of preservice training described under paragraph (f), clause (1);</p>
          <p>(2) receive at least 40 hours of continuing education in severe emotional disturbance and mental health service annually; and</p>
          <p>(3) receive at least five hours of mentoring per week from a case management mentor. A "case management mentor" means a qualified, practicing case manager or case management supervisor who teaches or advises and provides intensive training and clinical supervision to one or more case manager associates. Mentoring may occur while providing direct services to consumers in the office or in the field and may be provided to individuals or groups of case manager associates. At least two mentoring hours per week must be individual and face-to-face.</p>
          <p>(l) A case management supervisor must meet the criteria for a mental health professional as specified in subdivision 27.</p>
          <p>(m) An immigrant who does not have the qualifications specified in this subdivision may provide case management services to child immigrants with severe emotional disturbance of the same ethnic group as the immigrant if the person:</p>
          <p>(1) is currently enrolled in and is actively pursuing credits toward the completion of a bachelor's degree in one of the behavioral sciences or related fields at an accredited college or university;</p>
          <p>(2) completes 40 hours of training as specified in this subdivision; and</p>
          <p>(3) receives clinical supervision at least once a week until the requirements of obtaining a bachelor's degree and 2,000 hours of supervised experience are met.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4871.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Child.</span></h2>
          <p>"Child" means a person under 18 years of age.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4871.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Child with severe emotional disturbance.</span></h2>
          <p>For purposes of eligibility for case management and family community support services, "child with severe emotional disturbance" means a child who has an emotional disturbance and who meets one of the following criteria:</p>
          <p>(1) the child has been admitted within the last three years or is at risk of being admitted to inpatient treatment or residential treatment for an emotional disturbance; or</p>
          <p>(2) the child is a Minnesota resident and is receiving inpatient treatment or residential treatment for an emotional disturbance through the interstate compact; or</p>
          <p>(3) the child has one of the following as determined by a mental health professional:</p>
          <p>(i) psychosis or a clinical depression; or</p>
          <p>(ii) risk of harming self or others as a result of an emotional disturbance; or</p>
          <p>(iii) psychopathological symptoms as a result of being a victim of physical or sexual abuse or of psychic trauma within the past year; or</p>
          <p>(4) the child, as a result of an emotional disturbance, has significantly impaired home, school, or community functioning that has lasted at least one year or that, in the written opinion of a mental health professional, presents substantial risk of lasting at least one year.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4871.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/262/#laws.3.18.0">2014 c 262 art 3 s 18</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4871.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Commissioner.</span></h2>
          <p>"Commissioner" means the commissioner of human services.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4871.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">County board.</span></h2>
          <p>"County board" means the county board of commissioners or board established under the Joint Powers Act, section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/471.59">471.59</a>, or the Human Services Act, sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/402.01">402.01</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/402.10">402.10</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4871.9a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9a.<span class="headnote">Crisis planning.</span></h2>
          <p>"Crisis planning" means the development of a written plan to assist a child and the child's family in preventing and addressing a potential crisis and is distinct from mobile crisis services defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.0624">256B.0624</a>. The plan must address prevention, de-escalation, and intervention strategies to be used in a crisis. The plan identifies factors that might precipitate a crisis, behaviors or symptoms related to the emergence of a crisis, and the resources available to resolve a crisis. The plan must address the following potential needs: (1) acute care; (2) crisis placement; (3) community resources for follow-up; and (4) emotional support to the family during crisis. When appropriate for the child's needs, the plan must include strategies to reduce the child's risk of suicide and self-injurious behavior. Crisis planning does not include services designed to secure the safety of a child who is at risk of abuse or neglect or necessary emergency services.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4871.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Day treatment services.</span></h2>
          <p>"Day treatment," "day treatment services," or "day treatment program" means a structured program of treatment and care provided to a child in:</p>
          <p>(1) an outpatient hospital accredited by the Joint Commission on Accreditation of Health Organizations and licensed under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/144.50">144.50</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/144.55">144.55</a>;</p>
          <p>(2) a community mental health center under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.62">245.62</a>;</p>
          <p>(3) an entity that is under contract with the county board to operate a program that meets the requirements of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.4884#stat.245.4884.2">245.4884, subdivision 2</a>, and Minnesota Rules, parts <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/9505.0170">9505.0170 </a>to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/9505.0475">9505.0475</a>;</p>
          <p>(4) an entity that operates a program that meets the requirements of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.4884#stat.245.4884.2">245.4884, subdivision 2</a>, and Minnesota Rules, parts <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/9505.0170">9505.0170</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/9505.0475">9505.0475</a>, that is under contract with an entity that is under contract with a county board; or</p>
          <p>(5) a program certified under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.0943">256B.0943</a>.</p>
          <p>Day treatment consists of group psychotherapy and other intensive therapeutic services that are provided for a minimum two-hour time block by a multidisciplinary staff under the treatment supervision of a mental health professional. Day treatment may include education and consultation provided to families and other individuals as an extension of the treatment process. The services are aimed at stabilizing the child's mental health status, and developing and improving the child's daily independent living and socialization skills. Day treatment services are distinguished from day care by their structured therapeutic program of psychotherapy services. Day treatment services are not a part of inpatient hospital or residential treatment services.</p>
          <p>A day treatment service must be available to a child up to 15 hours a week throughout the year and must be coordinated with, integrated with, or part of an education program offered by the child's school.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4871.11">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/262/#laws.3.18.0">2014 c 262 art 3 s 18</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4871.11a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11a.<span class="headnote">Diagnostic assessment.</span></h2>
          <p> "Diagnostic assessment" has the meaning given in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245I.10">245I.10</a>, subdivisions 4 to 6.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4871.12">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 12.<span class="headnote">Mental health identification and intervention services.</span></h2>
          <p>"Mental health identification and intervention services" means services that are designed to identify children who are at risk of needing or who need mental health services and that arrange for intervention and treatment.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4871.13">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 13.<span class="headnote">Education and prevention services.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) "Education and prevention services" means services designed to:</p>
          <p>(1) educate the general public;</p>
          <p>(2) increase the understanding and acceptance of problems associated with emotional disturbances;</p>
          <p>(3) improve people's skills in dealing with high-risk situations known to affect children's mental health and functioning; and</p>
          <p>(4) refer specific children or their families with mental health needs to mental health services.</p>
          <p>(b) The services include distribution to individuals and agencies identified by the county board and the local children's mental health advisory council of information on predictors and symptoms of emotional disturbances, where mental health services are available in the county, and how to access the services.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4871.14">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 14.<span class="headnote">Emergency services.</span></h2>
          <p>"Emergency services" means an immediate response service available on a 24-hour, seven-day-a-week basis for each child having a psychiatric crisis, a mental health crisis, or a mental health emergency.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4871.15">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 15.<span class="headnote">Emotional disturbance.</span></h2>
          <p>"Emotional disturbance" means an organic disorder of the brain or a clinically significant disorder of thought, mood, perception, orientation, memory, or behavior that:</p>
          <p>(1) is detailed in a diagnostic codes list published by the commissioner; and</p>
          <p>(2) seriously limits a child's capacity to function in primary aspects of daily living such as personal relations, living arrangements, work, school, and recreation.</p>
          <p>"Emotional disturbance" is a generic term and is intended to reflect all categories of disorder described in the clinical code list published by the commissioner as "usually first evident in childhood or adolescence."</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4871.16">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 16.<span class="headnote">Family.</span></h2>
          <p>"Family" means a child and one or more of the following persons whose participation is necessary to accomplish the child's treatment goals: (1) a person related to the child by blood, marriage, or adoption; (2) a person who is the child's foster parent or significant other; (3) a person who is the child's legal representative.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4871.17">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 17.<span class="headnote">Family community support services.</span></h2>
          <p>"Family community support services" means services provided under the treatment supervision of a mental health professional and designed to help each child with severe emotional disturbance to function and remain with the child's family in the community. Family community support services do not include acute care hospital inpatient treatment, residential treatment services, or regional treatment center services. Family community support services include:</p>
          <p>(1) client outreach to each child with severe emotional disturbance and the child's family;</p>
          <p>(2) medication monitoring where necessary;</p>
          <p>(3) assistance in developing independent living skills;</p>
          <p>(4) assistance in developing parenting skills necessary to address the needs of the child with severe emotional disturbance;</p>
          <p>(5) assistance with leisure and recreational activities;</p>
          <p>(6) crisis planning, including crisis placement and respite care;</p>
          <p>(7) professional home-based family treatment;</p>
          <p>(8) foster care with therapeutic supports;</p>
          <p>(9) day treatment;</p>
          <p>(10) assistance in locating respite care and special needs day care; and</p>
          <p>(11) assistance in obtaining potential financial resources, including those benefits listed in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.4884#stat.245.4884.5">245.4884, subdivision 5</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4871.18">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 18.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/262/#laws.3.18.0">2014 c 262 art 3 s 18</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4871.18a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 18a.<span class="headnote">Functional assessment.</span></h2>
          <p>"Functional assessment" means an assessment by the case manager of the child's:</p>
          <p>(1) mental health symptoms as presented in the child's diagnostic assessment;</p>
          <p>(2) mental health needs as presented in the child's diagnostic assessment;</p>
          <p>(3) use of drugs and alcohol;</p>
          <p>(4) vocational and educational functioning;</p>
          <p>(5) social functioning, including the use of leisure time;</p>
          <p>(6) interpersonal functioning, including relationships with the child's family;</p>
          <p>(7) self-care and independent living capacity;</p>
          <p>(8) medical and dental health;</p>
          <p>(9) financial assistance needs;</p>
          <p>(10) housing and transportation needs; and</p>
          <p>(11) other needs and problems.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4871.19">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 19.<span class="headnote">Individual family community support plan.</span></h2>
          <p>"Individual family community support plan" means a written plan developed by a case manager in conjunction with the family and the child with severe emotional disturbance on the basis of a diagnostic assessment and a functional assessment. The plan identifies specific services needed by a child and the child's family to:</p>
          <p>(1) treat the symptoms and dysfunctions determined in the diagnostic assessment;</p>
          <p>(2) relieve conditions leading to emotional disturbance and improve the personal well-being of the child;</p>
          <p>(3) improve family functioning;</p>
          <p>(4) enhance daily living skills;</p>
          <p>(5) improve functioning in education and recreation settings;</p>
          <p>(6) improve interpersonal and family relationships;</p>
          <p>(7) enhance vocational development; and</p>
          <p>(8) assist in obtaining transportation, housing, health services, and employment.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4871.20">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 20.<span class="headnote">Individual placement agreement.</span></h2>
          <p>"Individual placement agreement" means a written agreement or supplement to a service contract entered into between the county board and a service provider on behalf of a child to provide residential treatment services.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4871.21">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 21.<span class="headnote">Individual treatment plan.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) "Individual treatment plan" means the formulation of planned services that are responsive to the needs and goals of a client. An individual treatment plan must be completed according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245I.10">245I.10, subdivisions 7 </a>and 8.</p>
          <p>(b) A children's residential facility licensed under Minnesota Rules, chapter 2960, is exempt from the requirements of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245I.10">245I.10, subdivisions 7</a> and 8. Instead, the individual treatment plan must:</p>
          <p>(1) include a written plan of intervention, treatment, and services for a child with an emotional disturbance that the service provider develops under the clinical supervision of a mental health professional on the basis of a diagnostic assessment;</p>
          <p>(2) be developed in conjunction with the family unless clinically inappropriate; and</p>
          <p>(3) identify goals and objectives of treatment, treatment strategy, a schedule for accomplishing treatment goals and objectives, and the individuals responsible for providing treatment to the child with an emotional disturbance.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4871.22">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 22.<span class="headnote">Legal representative.</span></h2>
          <p>"Legal representative" means a guardian, conservator, or guardian ad litem of a child with an emotional disturbance authorized by the court to make decisions about mental health services for the child.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4871.23">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 23.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/94/#laws.0.25.0">1991 c 94 s 25</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4871.24">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 24.<span class="headnote">Local system of care.</span></h2>
          <p>"Local system of care" means services that are locally available to the child and the child's family. The services are mental health, social services, correctional services, education services, health services, and vocational services.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4871.24a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 24a.<span class="headnote">Mental health crisis services.</span></h2>
          <p>"Mental health crisis services" means crisis assessment, crisis intervention, and crisis stabilization services.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4871.25">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 25.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/262/#laws.3.18.0">2014 c 262 art 3 s 18</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4871.26">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 26.<span class="headnote">Mental health practitioner.</span></h2>
          <p>"Mental health practitioner" means a staff person who is qualified according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245I.04#stat.245I.04.4">245I.04, subdivision 4</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4871.27">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 27.<span class="headnote">Mental health professional.</span></h2>
          <p>"Mental health professional" means a staff person who is qualified according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245I.04#stat.245I.04.2">245I.04, subdivision 2</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4871.28">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 28.<span class="headnote">Mental health services.</span></h2>
          <p>"Mental health services" means at least all of the treatment services and case management activities that are provided to children with emotional disturbances and are described in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.487">245.487</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.4889">245.4889</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4871.29">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 29.<span class="headnote">Outpatient services.</span></h2>
          <p>"Outpatient services" means mental health services, excluding day treatment and community support services programs, provided by or under the treatment supervision of a mental health professional to children with emotional disturbances who live outside a hospital. Outpatient services include clinical activities such as individual, group, and family therapy; individual treatment planning; diagnostic assessments; medication management; and psychological testing.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4871.30">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 30.<span class="headnote">Parent.</span></h2>
          <p>"Parent" means the birth or adoptive mother or father of a child. This definition does not apply to a person whose parental rights have been terminated in relation to the child.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4871.31">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 31.<span class="headnote">Professional home-based family treatment.</span></h2>
          <p>"Professional home-based family treatment" means intensive mental health services provided to children because of an emotional disturbance (1) who are at risk of out-of-home placement; (2) who are in out-of-home placement; or (3) who are returning from out-of-home placement. Services are provided to the child and the child's family primarily in the child's home environment. Services may also be provided in the child's school, child care setting, or other community setting appropriate to the child. Services must be provided on an individual family basis, must be child-oriented and family-oriented, and must be designed using information from diagnostic and functional assessments to meet the specific mental health needs of the child and the child's family. Examples of services are: (1) individual therapy; (2) family therapy; (3) client outreach; (4) assistance in developing individual living skills; (5) assistance in developing parenting skills necessary to address the needs of the child; (6) assistance with leisure and recreational services; (7) crisis planning, including crisis respite care and arranging for crisis placement; and (8) assistance in locating respite and child care. Services must be coordinated with other services provided to the child and family.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4871.32">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 32.<span class="headnote">Residential treatment.</span></h2>
          <p>"Residential treatment" means a 24-hour-a-day program under the treatment supervision of a mental health professional, in a community residential setting other than an acute care hospital or regional treatment center inpatient unit, that must be licensed as a residential treatment program for children with emotional disturbances under Minnesota Rules, parts <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/2960.0580">2960.0580</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/2960.0700">2960.0700</a>, or other rules adopted by the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4871.32a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 32a.</h2>
          <p>MS 2020 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/1/7/#laws.11.49.0">1Sp2021 c 7 art 11 s 49</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4871.33">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 33.<span class="headnote">Service provider.</span></h2>
          <p>"Service provider" means either a county board or an entity under contract with the county board that provides children's mental health services funded under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.487">245.487 </a>to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.4889">245.4889</a>. A contracting entity may be an individual, organization, or agency, including a regional treatment center.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4871.33a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 33a.<span class="headnote">Culturally informed mental health consultant.</span></h2>
          <p>"Culturally informed mental health consultant" is a person who is recognized by the culture as one who has knowledge of a particular culture and its definition of health and mental health; and who is used as necessary to assist the county board and its mental health providers in assessing and providing appropriate mental health services for children from that particular cultural, linguistic, or racial heritage and their families.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4871.34">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 34.<span class="headnote">Therapeutic support of foster care.</span></h2>
          <p>"Therapeutic support of foster care" means the mental health training and mental health support services and treatment supervision provided by a mental health professional to foster families caring for children with severe emotional disturbance to provide a therapeutic family environment and support for the child's improved functioning. Therapeutic support of foster care includes services provided under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.0946">256B.0946</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4871.35">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 35.<span class="headnote">Transition services.</span></h2>
          <p>"Transition services" means mental health services, designed within an outcome oriented process that promotes movement from school to postschool activities, including postsecondary education, vocational training, integrated employment including supported employment, continuing and adult education, adult mental health and social services, other adult services, independent living, or community participation.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4871.36">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 36.<span class="headnote">Treatment supervision.</span></h2>
          <p>"Treatment supervision" means the treatment supervision described by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245I.06">245I.06</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245.4871.history
			">

      <div class="sr" id="stat.245.4872"><b>245.4872</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/262/#laws.3.18.0">2014 c 262 art 3 s 18</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245.4873">
        <h1 class="shn">245.4873 COORDINATION OF CHILDREN'S MENTAL HEALTH SYSTEM.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4873.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">State and local coordination.</span></h2>
          <p>Coordination of the development and delivery of mental health services for children shall occur on the state and local levels to assure the availability of services to meet the mental health needs of children in a cost-effective manner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4873.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">State level; coordination.</span></h2>
          <p>The Children's Cabinet, under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/4.045">4.045</a>, in consultation with a representative of the Minnesota District Judges Association Juvenile Committee, shall:</p>
          <p>(1) educate each agency about the policies, procedures, funding, and services for children with emotional disturbances of all agencies represented;</p>
          <p>(2) develop mechanisms for interagency coordination on behalf of children with emotional disturbances;</p>
          <p>(3) identify barriers including policies and procedures within all agencies represented that interfere with delivery of mental health services for children;</p>
          <p>(4) recommend policy and procedural changes needed to improve development and delivery of mental health services for children in the agency or agencies they represent; and</p>
          <p>(5) identify mechanisms for better use of federal and state funding in the delivery of mental health services for children.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4873.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/262/#laws.3.18.0">2014 c 262 art 3 s 18</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4873.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Individual case coordination.</span></h2>
          <p>The case manager designated under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.4881">245.4881</a> is responsible for ongoing coordination with any other person responsible for planning, development, and delivery of social services, education, corrections, health, or vocational services for the individual child. The individual family community support plan developed by the case manager shall reflect the coordination among the local service system providers.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4873.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Duties of the commissioner.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall supervise the development and coordination of locally available children's mental health services by the county boards in a manner consistent with sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.487">245.487</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.4889">245.4889</a>. The commissioner shall provide technical assistance to county boards in developing and maintaining locally available and coordinated children's mental health services. The commissioner shall monitor the county board's progress in developing its full system capacity and quality through ongoing review of the county board's children's mental health proposals and other information as required by sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.487">245.487</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.4889">245.4889</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4873.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/262/#laws.3.18.0">2014 c 262 art 3 s 18</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245.4873.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
