          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Individual placement agreement.</span></h2>
          <p>The county board shall enter into an individual placement agreement with a provider of residential treatment services to a child eligible for county-paid services under this section. The agreement must specify the payment rate and terms and conditions of county payment for the placement.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4885.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/337/#laws.0.20.0">1993 c 337 s 20</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4885.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Summary data collection.</span></h2>
          <p>The county board shall annually collect summary information on the number of children screened, the age and racial or ethnic background of the children, the presenting problem, and the screening recommendations. The county shall include information on the degree to which these recommendations are followed and the reasons for not following recommendations. Summary data shall be available to the public and shall be used by the county board and local children's advisory council to identify needed service development.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245.4885.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/282/#laws.4.51.0">1989 c 282 art 4 s 51</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/568/#laws.5.26.0">1990 c 568 art 5 s 26</a>,27; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/292/#laws.6.23.0">1991 c 292 art 6 s 23</a>-25; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/207/#laws.8.11.0">1995 c 207 art 8 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/139/#laws.4.2.0">1999 c 139 art 4 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/9/#laws.9.17.0">1Sp2001 c 9 art 9 s 17</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/379/#laws.1.113.0">2002 c 379 art 1 s 113</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/14/#laws.11.11.0">1Sp2003 c 14 art 11 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/4/#laws.2.4.0">1Sp2005 c 4 art 2 s 4</a>-6; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/167/#laws.0.5.0">2009 c 167 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/174/#laws.1.2.0">2009 c 174 art 1 s 2</a>,3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/303/#laws.0.1.0">2010 c 303 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2020/1/2/#laws.5.2.0">1Sp2020 c 2 art 5 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/0/30/#laws.10.1.0">2021 c 30 art 10 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/1/7/#laws.11.5.0">1Sp2021 c 7 art 11 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2022/0/98/#laws.17.26.0">2022 c 98 art 17 s 26</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2022/0/99/#laws.1.10.0">2022 c 99 art 1 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/25/#laws.0.118.0">2023 c 25 s 118</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/50/#laws.1.12.0">2023 c 50 art 1 s 12</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.245.4886"><b>245.4886</b> MS 1990 [Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.4887">245.4887</a>] </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.245.4886"><b>245.4886</b> MS 2002 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/14/#laws.11.12.0">1Sp2003 c 14 art 11 s 12</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.245.4887"><b>245.4887</b> MS 1990 [Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.4888">245.4888</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245.4887">
        <h1 class="shn">245.4887 APPEALS.</h1>
        <p>A child or a child's family, as appropriate, who requests mental health services under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.487">245.487</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.4889">245.4889</a> must be advised of services available and the right to appeal as described in this section at the time of the request and each time the individual family community support plan or individual treatment plan is reviewed. A child whose request for mental health services under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.487">245.487</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.4889">245.4889</a> is denied, not acted upon with reasonable promptness, or whose services are suspended, reduced, or terminated by action or inaction for which the county board is responsible under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.487">245.487</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.4889">245.4889</a> may contest that action or inaction before the state agency according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256.045">256.045</a>. The commissioner shall monitor the nature and frequency of administrative appeals under this section.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245.4887.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/282/#laws.4.52.0">1989 c 282 art 4 s 52</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/292/#laws.6.58.0">1991 c 292 art 6 s 58</a> subd 1; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/14/#laws.11.11.0">1Sp2003 c 14 art 11 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/147/#laws.8.38.0">2007 c 147 art 8 s 38</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2022/0/98/#laws.17.26.0">2022 c 98 art 17 s 26</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/50/#laws.1.13.0">2023 c 50 art 1 s 13</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.245.4888"><b>245.4888</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/14/#laws.11.12.0">1Sp2003 c 14 art 11 s 12</a>]</div>
      <h1 class="fgh">CHILDREN'S MENTAL HEALTH GRANTS</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245.4889">
        <h1 class="shn">245.4889 CHILDREN'S MENTAL HEALTH GRANTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4889.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Establishment and authority.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) The commissioner is authorized to make grants from available appropriations to assist:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) counties;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) Indian tribes;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) children's collaboratives under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142D.15">142D.15</a> or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.493">245.493</a>; or</p>
          <p class="in">(4) mental health service providers.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) The following services are eligible for grants under this section:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) services to children with emotional disturbances as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.4871">245.4871</a>, subdivision 15, and their families;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) transition services under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.4875#stat.245.4875.8">245.4875, subdivision 8</a>, for young adults under age 21 and their families;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) respite care services for children with emotional disturbances or severe emotional disturbances who are at risk of residential treatment or hospitalization, who are already in out-of-home placement in family foster settings as defined in chapter 142B and at risk of change in out-of-home placement or placement in a residential facility or other higher level of care, who have utilized crisis services or emergency room services, or who have experienced a loss of in-home staffing support. Allowable activities and expenses for respite care services are defined under subdivision 4. A child is not required to have case management services to receive respite care services. Counties must work to provide access to regularly scheduled respite care;</p>
          <p class="in">(4) children's mental health crisis services;</p>
          <p class="in">(5) child-, youth-, and family-specific mobile response and stabilization services models;</p>
          <p class="in">(6) mental health services for people from cultural and ethnic minorities, including supervision of clinical trainees who are Black, indigenous, or people of color;</p>
          <p class="in">(7) children's mental health screening and follow-up diagnostic assessment and treatment;</p>
          <p class="in">(8) services to promote and develop the capacity of providers to use evidence-based practices in providing children's mental health services;</p>
          <p class="in">(9) school-linked mental health services under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.4901">245.4901</a>;</p>
          <p>(10) building evidence-based mental health intervention capacity for children birth to age five;</p>
          <p class="in">(11) suicide prevention and counseling services that use text messaging statewide;</p>
          <p>(12) mental health first aid training;</p>
          <p>(13) training for parents, collaborative partners, and mental health providers on the impact of adverse childhood experiences and trauma and development of an interactive website to share information and strategies to promote resilience and prevent trauma;</p>
          <p>(14) transition age services to develop or expand mental health treatment and supports for adolescents and young adults 26 years of age or younger;</p>
          <p>(15) early childhood mental health consultation;</p>
          <p>(16) evidence-based interventions for youth at risk of developing or experiencing a first episode of psychosis, and a public awareness campaign on the signs and symptoms of psychosis;</p>
          <p>(17) psychiatric consultation for primary care practitioners; and</p>
          <p>(18) providers to begin operations and meet program requirements when establishing a new children's mental health program. These may be start-up grants.</p>
          <p>(c) Services under paragraph (b) must be designed to help each child to function and remain with the child's family in the community and delivered consistent with the child's treatment plan. Transition services to eligible young adults under this paragraph must be designed to foster independent living in the community.</p>
          <p>(d) As a condition of receiving grant funds, a grantee shall obtain all available third-party reimbursement sources, if applicable.</p>
          <p>(e) The commissioner may establish and design a pilot program to expand the mobile response and stabilization services model for children, youth, and families. The commissioner may use grant funding to consult with a qualified expert entity to assist in the formulation of measurable outcomes and explore and position the state to submit a Medicaid state plan amendment to scale the model statewide.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4889.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Grant application and reporting requirements.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">To apply for a grant, an applicant organization shall submit an application and budget for the use of the money in the form specified by the commissioner. The commissioner shall make grants only to entities whose applications and budgets are approved by the commissioner. In awarding grants, the commissioner shall give priority to applications that indicate plans to collaborate in the development, funding, and delivery of services with other agencies in the local system of care. The commissioner shall specify requirements for reports, including quarterly fiscal reports under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256.01#stat.256.01.2">256.01, subdivision 2</a>, paragraph (o). The commissioner shall require collection of data and periodic reports that the commissioner deems necessary to demonstrate the effectiveness of each service.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4889.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Commissioner duty to report on use of grant funds biennially.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) By November 1, 2016, and biennially thereafter, the commissioner of human services shall provide sufficient information to the members of the legislative committees having jurisdiction over mental health funding and policy issues to evaluate the use of funds appropriated under this section. The commissioner shall provide, at a minimum, the following information:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) the amount of funding for children's mental health grants, what programs and services were funded in the previous two years, and outcome data for the programs and services that were funded; and</p>
          <p class="in">(2) the amount of funding for other targeted services and the location of services.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) This subdivision expires January 1, 2032.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4889.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Respite care services.</span></h2>
          <p>Respite care services under subdivision 1, paragraph (b), clause (3), include hourly or overnight stays at a licensed foster home or with a qualified and approved family member or friend and may occur at a child's or provider's home. Respite care services may also include the following activities and expenses:</p>
          <p>(1) recreational, sport, and nonsport extracurricular activities and programs for the child including camps, clubs, lessons, group outings, sports, or other activities and programs;</p>
          <p>(2) family activities, camps, and retreats that the family does together and provide a break from the family's circumstance;</p>
          <p>(3) cultural programs and activities for the child and family designed to address the unique needs of individuals who share a common language, racial, ethnic, or social background; and</p>
          <p>(4) costs of transportation, food, supplies, and equipment directly associated with approved respite care services and expenses necessary for the child and family to access and participate in respite care services.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245.4889.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/147/#laws.8.8.0">2007 c 147 art 8 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/0/71/#laws.2.14.0">2015 c 71 art 2 s 14</a>,15; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/1/6/#laws.8.7.0">1Sp2017 c 6 art 8 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/9/#laws.6.2.0">1Sp2019 c 9 art 6 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2020/1/2/#laws.5.3.0">1Sp2020 c 2 art 5 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/1/7/#laws.11.6.0">1Sp2021 c 7 art 11 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2022/0/98/#laws.6.4.0">2022 c 98 art 6 s 4</a>,5; art 14 s 11; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/70/#laws.9.3.0">2023 c 70 art 9 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/80/#laws.2.74.0">2024 c 80 art 2 s 74</a>; art 4 s 26; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/115/#laws.16.41.0">2024 c 115 art 16 s 41</a>,42; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/127/#laws.61.3.0">2024 c 127 art 61 s 3</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.245.490"><b>245.490</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/262/#laws.3.18.0">2014 c 262 art 3 s 18</a>]</div>
      <h1 class="fgh">SCHOOL-LINKED MENTAL HEALTH GRANTS</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245.4901">
        <h1 class="shn">245.4901 SCHOOL-LINKED BEHAVIORAL HEALTH GRANTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4901.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Establishment.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of human services shall establish a school-linked behavioral health grant program to provide early identification and intervention for students with mental health and substance use disorder needs and to build the capacity of schools to support students with mental health and substance use disorder needs in the classroom.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4901.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Eligible applicants.</span></h2>
          <p>An eligible applicant for a school-linked behavioral health grant is an entity or provider that is:</p>
          <p>(1) a mental health clinic certified under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245I.20">245I.20</a>;</p>
          <p>(2) a community mental health center under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.0625#stat.256B.0625.5">256B.0625, subdivision 5</a>;</p>
          <p>(3) an Indian health service facility or a facility owned and operated by a tribe or tribal organization operating under United States Code, title 25, section 5321;</p>
          <p>(4) a provider of children's therapeutic services and supports as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.0943">256B.0943</a>;</p>
          <p>(5) enrolled in medical assistance as a mental health or substance use disorder provider agency and employs at least two full-time equivalent mental health professionals qualified according to section 245I.04, subdivision 2, or two alcohol and drug counselors licensed or exempt from licensure under chapter 148F who are qualified to provide clinical services to children and families;</p>
          <p>(6) licensed under chapter 245G and in compliance with the applicable requirements in chapters 245A, 245C, and 260E, section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/626.557">626.557</a>, and Minnesota Rules, chapter 9544; or</p>
          <p>(7) a licensed professional in private practice as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245G.01">245G.01, subdivision </a>17, who meets the requirements of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/254B.05#stat.254B.05.1">254B.05, subdivision 1</a>, paragraph (b).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4901.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Allowable grant activities and related expenses.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Allowable grant activities and related expenses may include but are not limited to:</p>
          <p>(1) identifying and diagnosing mental health conditions and substance use disorders of students;</p>
          <p>(2) delivering mental health and substance use disorder treatment and services to students and their families, including via telehealth consistent with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.0625#stat.256B.0625.3b">256B.0625, subdivision 3b</a>;</p>
          <p>(3) supporting families in meeting their child's needs, including navigating health care, social service, and juvenile justice systems;</p>
          <p>(4) providing transportation for students receiving school-linked behavioral health services when school is not in session;</p>
          <p>(5) building the capacity of schools to meet the needs of students with mental health and substance use disorder concerns, including school staff development activities for licensed and nonlicensed staff; and</p>
          <p>(6) purchasing equipment, connection charges, on-site coordination, set-up fees, and site fees in order to deliver school-linked behavioral health services via telehealth.</p>
          <p>(b) Grantees shall obtain all available third-party reimbursement sources as a condition of receiving a grant. For purposes of this grant program, a third-party reimbursement source excludes a public school as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120A.20#stat.120A.20.1">120A.20, subdivision 1</a>. Grantees shall serve students regardless of health coverage status or ability to pay.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4901.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Data collection and outcome measurement.</span></h2>
          <p>Grantees shall provide data to the commissioner for the purpose of evaluating the effectiveness of the school-linked behavioral health grant program, no more frequently than twice per year. Data provided by grantees must include only the number of clients served, client demographics, payor information, client-related clinical and ancillary services including hours of direct client services, and hours of ancillary support services. Qualitative data may also be collected to demonstrate outcomes. The commissioner must consult with grantees to develop ongoing outcome measures for program capacity and performance.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4901.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Consultation; grant awards.</span></h2>
          <p>In administering the grant program, the commissioner shall consult with school districts that have not received grants under this section but that wish to collaborate with a community mental health provider. The commissioner shall also work with culturally specific providers to allow the providers to serve students from their community in multiple schools. When awarding grants, the commissioner shall consider the need to have consistency of providers over time among schools and students.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245.4901.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/9/#laws.6.3.0">1Sp2019 c 9 art 6 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/0/30/#laws.17.44.0">2021 c 30 art 17 s 44</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/1/7/#laws.6.28.0">1Sp2021 c 7 art 6 s 28; art 11 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/70/#laws.9.4.0">2023 c 70 art 9 s 4</a>,5</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245.4902">
        <h1 class="shn">245.4902 CULTURALLY INFORMED AND CULTURALLY RESPONSIVE MENTAL HEALTH TASK FORCE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4902.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Establishment; duties.</span></h2>
          <p>The Culturally Informed and Culturally Responsive Mental Health Task Force is established to evaluate and make recommendations on improving the provision of culturally informed and culturally responsive mental health services throughout Minnesota. The task force must make recommendations on:</p>
          <p>(1) recruiting mental health providers from diverse racial and ethnic communities;</p>
          <p>(2) training all mental health providers on cultural competency and cultural humility;</p>
          <p>(3) assessing the extent to which mental health provider organizations embrace diversity and demonstrate proficiency in culturally competent mental health treatment and services; and</p>
          <p>(4) increasing the number of mental health organizations owned, managed, or led by individuals who are Black, indigenous, or people of color.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4902.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Membership.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The task force must consist of the following 16 members:</p>
          <p>(1) the commissioner of human services or the commissioner's designee;</p>
          <p>(2) one representative from the Board of Psychology;</p>
          <p>(3) one representative from the Board of Marriage and Family Therapy;</p>
          <p>(4) one representative from the Board of Behavioral Health and Therapy;</p>
          <p>(5) one representative from the Board of Social Work;</p>
          <p>(6) three members representing undergraduate- and graduate-level mental health professional education programs, one appointed by the governor, one appointed by the speaker of the house of representatives, and one appointed by the senate majority leader;</p>
          <p>(7) three mental health providers who are members of communities of color or underrepresented communities, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/148E.010#stat.148E.010.20">148E.010, subdivision 20</a>, one appointed by the governor, one appointed by the speaker of the house of representatives, and one appointed by the senate majority leader;</p>
          <p>(8) two members representing mental health advocacy organizations, appointed by the governor;</p>
          <p>(9) two mental health providers, appointed by the governor; and</p>
          <p>(10) one expert in providing training and education in cultural competency and cultural responsiveness, appointed by the governor.</p>
          <p>(b) Appointments to the task force must be made no later than June 1, 2022.</p>
          <p>(c) Member compensation and reimbursement for expenses are governed by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/15.059#stat.15.059.3">15.059, subdivision 3</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4902.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Chairs; meetings.</span></h2>
          <p>The members of the task force must elect two cochairs of the task force no earlier than July 1, 2022, and the cochairs must convene the first meeting of the task force no later than August 15, 2022. The task force must meet upon the call of the cochairs, sufficiently often to accomplish the duties identified in this section. The task force is subject to the open meeting law under chapter 13D.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4902.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Administrative support.</span></h2>
          <p>The Department of Human Services must provide administrative support and meeting space for the task force.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4902.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Reports.</span></h2>
          <p>No later than January 1, 2023, and by January 1 of each year thereafter, the task force must submit a written report to the members of the legislative committees with jurisdiction over health and human services on the recommendations developed under subdivision 1.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4902.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Expiration.</span></h2>
          <p>The task force expires on January 1, 2025.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245.4902.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/1/7/#laws.11.8.0">1Sp2021 c 7 art 11 s 8</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245.4903">
        <h1 class="shn">245.4903 CULTURAL AND ETHNIC MINORITY INFRASTRUCTURE GRANT PROGRAM.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4903.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Establishment.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of human services must establish a cultural and ethnic minority infrastructure grant program to ensure that mental health and substance use disorder treatment supports and services are culturally specific and culturally responsive to meet the cultural needs of communities served.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4903.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Eligible applicants.</span></h2>
          <p>An eligible applicant is a licensed entity or provider from a cultural or ethnic minority population that:</p>
          <p>(1) provides mental health or substance use disorder treatment services and supports to individuals from cultural and ethnic minority populations, including members of those populations who identify as lesbian, gay, bisexual, transgender, or queer;</p>
          <p>(2) provides, or is qualified and has the capacity to provide, clinical supervision and support to members of culturally diverse and ethnic minority communities so they may become qualified mental health and substance use disorder treatment providers; or</p>
          <p>(3) has the capacity and experience to provide training for mental health and substance use disorder treatment providers on cultural competency and cultural humility.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4903.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Allowable grant activities.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Grantees must engage in activities and provide supportive services to ensure and increase equitable access to culturally specific and responsive care and build organizational and professional capacity for licensure and certification for the communities served. Allowable grant activities include but are not limited to:</p>
          <p>(1) providing workforce development activities focused on recruiting, supporting, training, and supervising mental health and substance use disorder practitioners and professionals from diverse racial, cultural, and ethnic communities;</p>
          <p>(2) helping members of racial and ethnic minority communities become qualified mental health and substance use disorder professionals, practitioners, clinical supervisors, recovery peer specialists, mental health certified peer specialists, and mental health certified family peer specialists;</p>
          <p>(3) providing culturally specific outreach, early intervention, trauma-informed services, and recovery support in mental health and substance use disorder services;</p>
          <p>(4) providing trauma-informed and culturally responsive mental health and substance use disorder supports and services to children and families, youth, or adults who are from cultural and ethnic minority backgrounds and are uninsured or underinsured;</p>
          <p>(5) expanding mental health and substance use disorder services, particularly in greater Minnesota;</p>
          <p>(6) training mental health and substance use disorder treatment providers on cultural competency and cultural humility;</p>
          <p>(7) providing activities that increase the availability of culturally responsive mental health and substance use disorder services for children and families, youth, or adults, or that increase the availability of substance use disorder services for individuals from cultural and ethnic minorities in the state;</p>
          <p>(8) providing interpreter services at intensive residential treatment facilities, children's residential treatment centers, or psychiatric residential treatment facilities in order for children or adults with limited English proficiency or children or adults who are fluent in another language to be able to access treatment; and</p>
          <p>(9) paying for case-specific consultation between a mental health professional and the appropriate diverse mental health professional in order to facilitate the provision of services that are culturally appropriate to a client's needs.</p>
          <p>(b) The commissioner must assist grantees with meeting third-party credentialing requirements, and grantees must obtain all available third-party reimbursement sources as a condition of receiving grant money. Grantees must serve individuals from cultural and ethnic minority communities regardless of health coverage status or ability to pay.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4903.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Data collection and outcomes.</span></h2>
          <p>Grantees must provide regular data summaries to the commissioner for purposes of evaluating the effectiveness of the cultural and ethnic minority infrastructure grant program. The commissioner must use identified culturally appropriate outcome measures instruments to evaluate outcomes and must evaluate program activities by analyzing whether the program:</p>
          <p>(1) increased access to culturally specific services for individuals from cultural and ethnic minority communities across the state;</p>
          <p>(2) increased the number of individuals from cultural and ethnic minority communities served by grantees;</p>
          <p>(3) increased cultural responsiveness and cultural competency of mental health and substance use disorder treatment providers;</p>
          <p>(4) increased the number of mental health and substance use disorder treatment providers and clinical supervisors from cultural and ethnic minority communities;</p>
          <p>(5) increased the number of mental health and substance use disorder treatment organizations owned, managed, or led by individuals who are Black, Indigenous, or people of color;</p>
          <p>(6) reduced health disparities through improved clinical and functional outcomes for those accessing services; and</p>
          <p>(7) led to an overall increase in culturally specific mental health and substance use disorder service availability.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245.4903.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/70/#laws.9.6.0">2023 c 70 art 9 s 6</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245.4905">
        <h1 class="shn">245.4905 FIRST EPISODE OF PSYCHOSIS GRANT PROGRAM.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4905.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Creation.</span></h2>
          <p>The first episode of psychosis grant program is established in the Department of Human Services to fund evidence-based interventions for youth at risk of developing or experiencing a first episode of psychosis and a public awareness campaign on the signs and symptoms of psychosis. First episode of psychosis services are eligible for children's mental health grants as specified in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.4889#stat.245.4889.1">245.4889, subdivision 1</a>, paragraph (b), clause (15).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4905.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Activities.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) All first episode of psychosis grant programs must:</p>
          <p>(1) provide intensive treatment and support for adolescents and adults experiencing or at risk of experiencing a first psychotic episode. Intensive treatment and support includes medication management, psychoeducation for an individual and an individual's family, case management, employment support, education support, cognitive behavioral approaches, social skills training, peer support, crisis planning, and stress management;</p>
          <p>(2) conduct outreach and provide training and guidance to mental health and health care professionals, including postsecondary health clinicians, on early psychosis symptoms, screening tools, and best practices;</p>
          <p>(3) ensure access for individuals to first psychotic episode services under this section, including access for individuals who live in rural areas; and</p>
          <p>(4) use all available funding streams.</p>
          <p>(b) Grant money may also be used to pay for housing or travel expenses for individuals receiving services or to address other barriers preventing individuals and their families from participating in first psychotic episode services.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4905.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Eligibility.</span></h2>
          <p>Program activities must be provided to people 15 to 40 years old with early signs of psychosis.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4905.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Outcomes.</span></h2>
          <p>Evaluation of program activities must utilize evidence-based practices and must include the following outcome evaluation criteria:</p>
          <p>(1) whether individuals experience a reduction in psychotic symptoms;</p>
          <p>(2) whether individuals experience a decrease in inpatient mental health hospitalizations; and</p>
          <p>(3) whether individuals experience an increase in educational attainment.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4905.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Federal aid or grants.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of human services must comply with all conditions and requirements necessary to receive federal aid or grants.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245.4905.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2022/0/99/#laws.1.11.0">2022 c 99 art 1 s 11</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245.4906">
        <h1 class="shn">245.4906 MENTAL HEALTH CERTIFIED PEER SPECIALIST GRANT PROGRAM.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4906.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Establishment.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of human services must establish a mental health certified peer specialist grant program to provide funding for the training of mental health certified peer specialists who provide services to support individuals with lived experience of mental illness under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.0615">256B.0615</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4906.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Eligible applicants.</span></h2>
          <p>An eligible applicant is a licensed entity or provider that employs a mental health certified peer specialist qualified under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245I.04">245I.04, subdivision </a>10, and that provides services to individuals receiving assertive community treatment or intensive residential treatment services under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.0622">256B.0622</a>, adult rehabilitative mental health services under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.0623">256B.0623</a>, or crisis response services under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.0624">256B.0624</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4906.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Allowable grant activities.</span></h2>
          <p>Grantees must use grant funding to provide training for mental health certified peer specialists as specified in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.0615">256B.0615, subdivision </a>5.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4906.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Outcomes.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Grantees must provide an annual report to the commissioner for the purposes of evaluating the effectiveness of the grant program. The report must include:</p>
          <p>(1) the number of mental health certified peer specialists who received training using the grant funds under this section; and</p>
          <p>(2) the extent to which individuals receiving peer services experienced progress on achieving treatment goals and experienced a reduction in hospital admissions.</p>
          <p>(b) The commissioner must submit the results of the evaluation to the chairs and ranking minority members of the legislative committees with jurisdiction over mental health.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245.4906.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/70/#laws.17.5.0">2023 c 70 art 17 s 5</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245.4907">
        <h1 class="shn">245.4907 MENTAL HEALTH CERTIFIED FAMILY PEER SPECIALIST GRANT PROGRAM.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4907.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Establishment.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of human services must establish a mental health certified peer family specialist grant program to provide funding for training for mental health certified peer family specialists who provide services to support individuals with lived experience of mental illness under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.0616">256B.0616</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4907.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Eligible applicants.</span></h2>
          <p>An eligible applicant is a licensed entity or provider that employs a mental health certified peer family specialist qualified under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245I.04">245I.04</a>, subdivision 12, and that provides services to families who have a child:</p>
          <p>(1) with an emotional disturbance or severe emotional disturbance under chapter 245;</p>
          <p>(2) receiving inpatient hospitalization under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.0625#stat.256B.0625.1">256B.0625, subdivision 1</a>;</p>
          <p>(3) admitted to a residential treatment facility under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.4882">245.4882</a>;</p>
          <p>(4) receiving children's intensive behavioral health services under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.0946">256B.0946</a>;</p>
          <p>(5) receiving day treatment or children's therapeutic services and supports under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.0943">256B.0943</a>; or</p>
          <p>(6) receiving crisis response services under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.0624">256B.0624</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4907.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Allowable grant activities.</span></h2>
          <p>Grantees must use grant funding to provide training for mental health certified family peer specialists as specified in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.0616">256B.0616</a>, subdivision 5.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4907.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Outcomes.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Grantees must provide an annual report to the commissioner for the purposes of evaluating the effectiveness of the grant program. The report must include:</p>
          <p>(1) the number of mental health certified peer specialists who received training using the grant funds under this section; and</p>
          <p>(2) the extent to which individuals receiving family peer services experienced progress on achieving treatment goals and experienced a reduction in hospital admissions.</p>
          <p>(b) The commissioner must submit the results of the evaluation to the chairs and ranking minority members of the legislative committees with jurisdiction over mental health.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245.4907.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/70/#laws.17.6.0">2023 c 70 art 17 s 6</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">CHILDREN'S MENTAL HEALTH INTEGRATED FUND</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245.491">
        <h1 class="shn">245.491 CITATION; DECLARATION OF PURPOSE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.491.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Citation.</span></h2>
          <p>Sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.491">245.491</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.495">245.495</a> may be cited as "the children's mental health integrated fund."</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.491.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Purpose.</span></h2>
          <p>The legislature finds that children with emotional or behavioral disturbances or who are at risk of suffering such disturbances often require services from multiple service systems including mental health, social services, education, corrections, juvenile court, health, and employment and economic development. In order to better meet the needs of these children, it is the intent of the legislature to establish an integrated children's mental health service system that:</p>
          <p>(1) allows local service decision makers to draw funding from a single local source so that funds follow clients and eliminates the need to match clients, funds, services, and provider eligibilities;</p>
          <p>(2) creates a local pool of state, local, and private funds to procure a greater medical assistance federal financial participation;</p>
          <p>(3) improves the efficiency of use of existing resources;</p>
          <p>(4) minimizes or eliminates the incentives for cost and risk shifting; and</p>
          <p>(5) increases the incentives for earlier identification and intervention.</p>
          <p class="bl">The children's mental health integrated fund established under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.491">245.491</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.495">245.495</a> must be used to develop and support this integrated mental health service system. In developing this integrated service system, it is not the intent of the legislature to limit any rights available to children and their families through existing federal and state laws.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245.491.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/1/1/#laws.7.11.0">1Sp1993 c 1 art 7 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/483/#laws.0.1.0">1994 c 483 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/14/#laws.11.11.0">1Sp2003 c 14 art 11 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/86/#laws.1.89.0">2009 c 86 art 1 s 89</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245.492">
        <h1 class="shn">245.492 DEFINITIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.492.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>The definitions in this section apply to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.491">245.491</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.495">245.495</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.492.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Base level funding.</span></h2>
          <p>"Base level funding" means funding received from state, federal, or local sources and expended across the local system of care in fiscal year 1995 for children's mental health services, for special education services, and for other services for children with emotional or behavioral disturbances and their families.</p>
          <p class="bl">In subsequent years, base level funding may be adjusted to reflect decreases in the numbers of children in the target population.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.492.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Children with emotional or behavioral disturbances.</span></h2>
          <p>"Children with emotional or behavioral disturbances" includes children with emotional disturbances as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.4871#stat.245.4871.15">245.4871, subdivision 15</a>, and children with emotional or behavioral disorders as defined in Minnesota Rules, part <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/3525.1329">3525.1329</a>, subpart 1.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.492.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Family.</span></h2>
          <p>"Family" has the definition provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.4871#stat.245.4871.16">245.4871, subdivision 16</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.492.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Family community support services.</span></h2>
          <p>"Family community support services" has the definition provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.4871#stat.245.4871.17">245.4871, subdivision 17</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.492.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/262/#laws.3.18.0">2014 c 262 art 3 s 18</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.492.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Integrated fund.</span></h2>
          <p>"Integrated fund" is a pool of both public and private local, state, and federal resources, consolidated at the local level, to accomplish locally agreed-upon service goals for the target population. The fund is used to help the local children's mental health collaborative to serve the mental health needs of children in the target population by allowing the local children's mental health collaboratives to develop and implement an integrated service system.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.492.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/262/#laws.3.18.0">2014 c 262 art 3 s 18</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.492.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Integrated service system.</span></h2>
          <p>"Integrated service system" means a coordinated set of procedures established by the local children's mental health collaborative for coordinating services and actions across categorical systems and agencies that results in:</p>
          <p>(1) integrated funding;</p>
          <p>(2) improved outreach, early identification, and intervention across systems;</p>
          <p>(3) strong collaboration between parents and professionals in identifying children in the target population facilitating access to the integrated system, and coordinating care and services for these children;</p>
          <p>(4) a coordinated assessment process across systems that determines which children need multiagency care coordination and wraparound services;</p>
          <p>(5) multiagency plan of care; and</p>
          <p>(6) individualized rehabilitation services.</p>
          <p class="bl">Services provided by the integrated service system must meet the requirements set out in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.487">245.487</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.4889">245.4889</a>. Children served by the integrated service system must be economically and culturally representative of children in the service delivery area.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.492.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Interagency early intervention committee.</span></h2>
          <p>"Interagency early intervention committee" refers to the committee established under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.30">125A.30</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.492.11">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11.<span class="headnote">Local children's advisory council.</span></h2>
          <p>"Local children's advisory council" refers to the council established under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.4875#stat.245.4875.5">245.4875, subdivision 5</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.492.12">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 12.<span class="headnote">Local children's mental health collaborative.</span></h2>
          <p>"Local children's mental health collaborative" or "collaborative" means an entity formed by the agreement of representatives of the local system of care including mental health services, social services, correctional services, education services, health services, and vocational services for the purpose of developing and governing an integrated service system. A local coordinating council, a community transition interagency committee as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.22">125A.22</a>, or an interagency early intervention committee may serve as a local children's mental health collaborative if its representatives are capable of carrying out the duties of the local children's mental health collaborative set out in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.491">245.491</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.495">245.495</a>. Where a local coordinating council is not the local children's mental health collaborative, the local children's mental health collaborative must work closely with the local coordinating council in designing the integrated service system.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.492.13">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 13.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/262/#laws.3.18.0">2014 c 262 art 3 s 18</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.492.14">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 14.<span class="headnote">Local system of care.</span></h2>
          <p>"Local system of care" has the definition provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.4871#stat.245.4871.24">245.4871, subdivision 24</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.492.15">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 15.<span class="headnote">Mental health services.</span></h2>
          <p>"Mental health services" has the definition provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.4871#stat.245.4871.28">245.4871, subdivision 28</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.492.16">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 16.<span class="headnote">Multiagency plan of care.</span></h2>
          <p>"Multiagency plan of care" means a written plan of intervention and integrated services developed by a multiagency team in conjunction with the child and family based on their unique strengths and needs as determined by a multiagency assessment. The plan must outline measurable client outcomes and specific services needed to attain these outcomes, the agencies responsible for providing the specified services, funding responsibilities, timelines, the judicial or administrative procedures needed to implement the plan of care, the agencies responsible for initiating these procedures and designate one person with lead responsibility for overseeing implementation of the plan.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.492.17">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 17.<span class="headnote">Respite care.</span></h2>
          <p>"Respite care" is planned routine care to support the continued residence of a child with emotional or behavioral disturbance with the child's family or long-term primary caretaker.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.492.18">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 18.<span class="headnote">Service delivery area.</span></h2>
          <p>"Service delivery area" means the geographic area to be served by the local children's mental health collaborative and must include at a minimum a part of a county and school district or a special education cooperative.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.492.19">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 19.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/262/#laws.3.18.0">2014 c 262 art 3 s 18</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.492.20">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 20.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/207/#laws.11.12.0">1995 c 207 art 11 s 12</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.492.21">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 21.<span class="headnote">Target population.</span></h2>
          <p>"Target population" means children up to age 18 with an emotional or behavioral disturbance or who are at risk of suffering an emotional or behavioral disturbance as evidenced by a behavior or condition that affects the child's ability to function in a primary aspect of daily living including personal relations, living arrangements, work, school, and recreation, and a child who can benefit from:</p>
          <p>(1) multiagency service coordination and wraparound services; or</p>
          <p>(2) informal coordination of traditional mental health services provided on a temporary basis.</p>
          <p>Children between the ages of 18 and 21 who meet these criteria may be included in the target population at the option of the local children's mental health collaborative.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.492.22">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 22.<span class="headnote">Therapeutic support of foster care.</span></h2>
          <p>"Therapeutic support of foster care" has the definition provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.4871#stat.245.4871.34">245.4871, subdivision 34</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.492.23">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 23.<span class="headnote">Individualized rehabilitation services.</span></h2>
          <p>"Individualized rehabilitation services" are alternative, flexible, coordinated, and highly individualized services that are based on a multiagency plan of care. These services are designed to build on the strengths and respond to the needs identified in the child's multiagency assessment and to improve the child's ability to function in the home, school, and community. Individualized rehabilitation services may include, but are not limited to, residential services, respite services, services that assist the child or family in enrolling in or participating in recreational activities, assistance in purchasing otherwise unavailable items or services important to maintain a specific child in the family, and services that assist the child to participate in more traditional services and programs.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245.492.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/1/1/#laws.7.12.0">1Sp1993 c 1 art 7 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/647/#laws.13.18.0">1994 c 647 art 13 s 18</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/207/#laws.8.13.0">1995 c 207 art 8 s 13</a>-16; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.11.3.0">1998 c 397 art 11 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/14/#laws.11.11.0">1Sp2003 c 14 art 11 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/147/#laws.8.38.0">2007 c 147 art 8 s 38</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245.493">
        <h1 class="shn">245.493 LOCAL CHILDREN'S MENTAL HEALTH COLLABORATIVE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.493.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Qualification requirements.</span></h2>
          <p>In order to qualify as a local children's mental health collaborative and be eligible to receive start-up funds, the representatives of the local system of care and nongovernmental entities such as parents of children in the target population; parent and consumer organizations; community, civic, and religious organizations; private and nonprofit mental and physical health care providers; culturally specific organizations; local foundations; and businesses, or at a minimum one county, one school district or special education cooperative, one mental health entity, and, by July 1, 1998, one juvenile justice or corrections entity, must agree to the following:</p>
          <p>(1) to establish a local children's mental health collaborative and develop an integrated service system;</p>
          <p>(2) to commit resources to providing services through the local children's mental health collaborative; and</p>
          <p>(3) to develop a plan to contribute funds to the children's mental health collaborative.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.493.1a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1a.<span class="headnote">Duties of certain coordinating bodies.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) By mutual agreement of the collaborative and a coordinating body listed in this subdivision, a children's mental health collaborative or a collaborative established by the merger of a children's mental health collaborative and a family services collaborative under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142D.15">142D.15</a>, may assume the duties of a community transition interagency committee established under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.22">125A.22</a>; an interagency early intervention committee established under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.30">125A.30</a>; or a local advisory council established under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.4875#stat.245.4875.5">245.4875, subdivision 5</a>.</p>
          <p>(b) Two or more family services collaboratives or children's mental health collaboratives may consolidate decision making, pool resources, and collectively act on behalf of the individual collaboratives, based on a written agreement among the participating collaboratives.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.493.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Duties of the collaborative.</span></h2>
          <p>Each local children's mental health collaborative must:</p>
          <p>(1) notify the commissioner of human services within ten days of formation by signing a collaborative agreement and providing the commissioner with a copy of the signed agreement;</p>
          <p>(2) identify a service delivery area and an operational target population within that service delivery area. The operational target population must be economically and culturally representative of children in the service delivery area to be served by the local children's mental health collaborative. The size of the operational target population must also be economically viable for the service delivery area;</p>
          <p>(3) seek to maximize federal revenues available to serve children in the target population by designating local expenditures for services for these children and their families that can be matched with federal dollars;</p>
          <p>(4) in consultation with the local children's advisory council and the local coordinating council, if it is not the local children's mental health collaborative, design, develop, and ensure implementation of an integrated service system that meets the requirements for state and federal reimbursement and develop interagency agreements necessary to implement the system;</p>
          <p>(5) expand membership to include representatives of other services in the local system of care including prepaid health plans under contract with the commissioner of human services to serve the needs of children in the target population and their families;</p>
          <p>(6) create or designate a management structure for fiscal and clinical responsibility and outcome evaluation;</p>
          <p>(7) spend funds generated by the local children's mental health collaborative as required in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.491">245.491</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.495">245.495</a>;</p>
          <p>(8) explore methods and recommend changes needed at the state level to reduce duplication and promote coordination of services including the use of uniform forms for reporting, billing, and planning of services;</p>
          <p>(9) submit its integrated service system design to the Children's Cabinet for approval within one year of notifying the commissioner of human services of its formation;</p>
          <p>(10) provide an annual report and the collaborative's planned timeline to expand its operational target population to the Children's Cabinet; and</p>
          <p>(11) expand its operational target population.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.493.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Information sharing.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The members of a local children's mental health collaborative may share data on individuals being served by the collaborative or its members if the individual, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/13.02#stat.13.02.8">13.02, subdivision 8</a>, gives written informed consent and the information sharing is necessary in order for the collaborative to carry out duties under subdivision 2. Data on individuals shared under this subdivision retain the original classification as defined under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/13.02">13.02</a>, as to each member of the collaborative with whom the data is shared.</p>
          <p>(b) If a federal law or regulation impedes information sharing that is necessary in order for a collaborative to carry out duties under subdivision 2, the appropriate state agencies shall attempt to get a waiver or exemption from the applicable law or regulation.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245.493.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/1/1/#laws.7.13.0">1Sp1993 c 1 art 7 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/618/#laws.1.27.0">1994 c 618 art 1 s 27</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/207/#laws.8.17.0">1995 c 207 art 8 s 17</a>; art 11 s 11; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/203/#laws.5.2.0">1997 c 203 art 5 s 2</a>,3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/1/4/#laws.2.40.0">1Sp1997 c 4 art 2 s 40</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.11.3.0">1998 c 397 art 11 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/14/#laws.4.3.0">1Sp2003 c 14 art 4 s 3</a>; art 11 s 11; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/0/21/#laws.1.42.0">2015 c 21 art 1 s 42</a>-44; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/0/40/#laws.1.49.0">2017 c 40 art 1 s 49</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/80/#laws.4.26.0">2024 c 80 art 4 s 26</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/115/#laws.16.42.0">2024 c 115 art 16 s 42</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245.4931">
        <h1 class="shn">245.4931 INTEGRATED LOCAL SERVICE SYSTEM.</h1>
        <p>The integrated service system established by the local children's mental health collaborative must:</p>
        <p>(1) include a process for communicating to agencies in the local system of care eligibility criteria for services received through the local children's mental health collaborative and a process for determining eligibility. The process shall place strong emphasis on outreach to families, respecting the family role in identifying children in need, and valuing families as partners;</p>
        <p>(2) include measurable outcomes, timelines for evaluating progress, and mechanisms for quality assurance and appeals;</p>
        <p>(3) involve the family, and where appropriate the individual child, in developing multiagency service plans to the extent required in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.08">125A.08</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.4871#stat.245.4871.21">245.4871, subdivision 21</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.4881#stat.245.4881.4">245.4881, subdivision 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/253B.03#stat.253B.03.7">253B.03, subdivision 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.212#stat.260C.212.1">260C.212, subdivision 1</a>; and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.201#stat.260C.201.6">260C.201, subdivision 6</a>;</p>
        <p>(4) meet all standards and provide all mental health services as required in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.487">245.487</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.4889">245.4889</a>, and ensure that the services provided are culturally appropriate;</p>
        <p>(5) spend funds generated by the local children's mental health collaborative as required in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.491">245.491</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.495">245.495</a>;</p>
        <p>(6) encourage public-private partnerships to increase efficiency, reduce redundancy, and promote quality of care; and</p>
        <p>(7) ensure that, if the county participant of the local children's mental health collaborative is also a provider of child welfare targeted case management as authorized by the 1993 legislature, then federal reimbursement received by the county for child welfare targeted case management provided to children served by the local children's mental health collaborative must be directed to the integrated fund.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245.4931.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/1/1/#laws.7.14.0">1Sp1993 c 1 art 7 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.11.3.0">1998 c 397 art 11 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/139/#laws.4.2.0">1999 c 139 art 4 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/14/#laws.11.11.0">1Sp2003 c 14 art 11 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/147/#laws.8.38.0">2007 c 147 art 8 s 38</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245.4932">
        <h1 class="shn">245.4932 REVENUE ENHANCEMENT; AUTHORITY AND RESPONSIBILITIES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4932.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Collaborative responsibilities.</span></h2>
          <p>The children's mental health collaborative shall have the following authority and responsibilities regarding federal revenue enhancement:</p>
          <p>(1) the collaborative must establish an integrated fund;</p>
          <p>(2) the collaborative shall designate a lead county or other qualified entity as the fiscal agency for reporting, claiming, and receiving payments;</p>
          <p>(3) the collaborative or lead county may enter into subcontracts with other counties, school districts, special education cooperatives, municipalities, and other public and nonprofit entities for purposes of identifying and claiming eligible expenditures to enhance federal reimbursement;</p>
          <p>(4) the collaborative shall use any enhanced revenue attributable to the activities of the collaborative, including administrative and service revenue, solely to provide mental health services or to expand the operational target population. The lead county or other qualified entity may not use enhanced federal revenue for any other purpose;</p>
          <p>(5) the collaborative or lead county must develop and maintain an accounting and financial management system adequate to support all claims for federal reimbursement, including a clear audit trail and any provisions specified in the contract with the commissioner of human services;</p>
          <p>(6) the collaborative or its members may elect to pay the nonfederal share of the medical assistance costs for services designated by the collaborative; and</p>
          <p>(7) the lead county or other qualified entity may not use federal funds or local funds designated as matching for other federal funds to provide the nonfederal share of medical assistance.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4932.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/262/#laws.3.18.0">2014 c 262 art 3 s 18</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4932.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/262/#laws.3.18.0">2014 c 262 art 3 s 18</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.4932.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/262/#laws.3.18.0">2014 c 262 art 3 s 18</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245.4932.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/1/1/#laws.7.15.0">1Sp1993 c 1 art 7 s 15</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/207/#laws.8.18.0">1995 c 207 art 8 s 18</a>-21; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/277/#laws.0.3.0">2002 c 277 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/112/#laws.2.50.0">2003 c 112 art 2 s 50</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/14/#laws.11.11.0">1Sp2003 c 14 art 11 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/101/#laws.2.109.0">2009 c 101 art 2 s 109</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/216/#laws.12.5.0">2012 c 216 art 12 s 5</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.245.4933"><b>245.4933</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/262/#laws.3.18.0">2014 c 262 art 3 s 18</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.245.494"><b>245.494</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/262/#laws.3.18.0">2014 c 262 art 3 s 18</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245.495">
        <h1 class="shn">245.495 ADDITIONAL FEDERAL REVENUES.</h1>
        <p>(a) Each local children's mental health collaborative shall report expenditures eligible for federal reimbursement in a manner prescribed by the commissioner of human services under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256.01#stat.256.01.2">256.01, subdivision 2</a>, paragraph (o). The commissioner of human services shall pay all funds earned by each local children's mental health collaborative to the collaborative. Each local children's mental health collaborative must use these funds to expand the operational target population or to develop or provide mental health services through the local integrated service system to children in the target population. Funds may not be used to supplant funding for services to children in the target population.</p>
        <p>For purposes of this section, "mental health services" are community-based, nonresidential services, which may include respite care, that are identified in the child's multiagency plan of care.</p>
        <p>(b) The commissioner may set aside a portion of the federal funds earned under this section to repay the special revenue maximization account under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256.01#stat.256.01.2">256.01, subdivision 2</a>, paragraph (o). The set-aside must not exceed five percent of the federal reimbursement earned by collaboratives and repayment is limited to:</p>
        <p>(1) the costs of developing and implementing sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.491">245.491</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.495">245.495</a>, including the costs of technical assistance from the Departments of Human Services, Education, Health, and Corrections to implement the children's mental health integrated fund;</p>
        <p>(2) programming the information systems; and</p>
        <p>(3) any lost federal revenue for the central office claim directly caused by the implementation of these sections.</p>
        <p>(c) Any unexpended funds from the set-aside described in paragraph (b) shall be distributed to counties.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245.495.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/1/1/#laws.7.17.0">1Sp1993 c 1 art 7 s 17</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/207/#laws.8.25.0">1995 c 207 art 8 s 25</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.16.13.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 16 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/130/#laws.0.12.0">2003 c 130 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/14/#laws.11.11.0">1Sp2003 c 14 art 11 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/0/78/#laws.4.61.0">2015 c 78 art 4 s 61</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.245.496"><b>245.496</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/14/#laws.11.12.0">1Sp2003 c 14 art 11 s 12</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245.50">
        <h1 class="shn">245.50 INTERSTATE CONTRACTS, MENTAL HEALTH, CHEMICAL HEALTH, DETOXIFICATION SERVICES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.50.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>For purposes of this section, the following terms have the meanings given them.</p>
          <p>(a) "Bordering state" means Iowa, North Dakota, South Dakota, or Wisconsin.</p>
          <p>(b) "Receiving agency" means a public or private hospital, mental health center, chemical health treatment facility, detoxification facility, or other person or organization which provides mental health, chemical health, or detoxification services under this section to individuals from a state other than the state in which the agency is located.</p>
          <p>(c) "Receiving state" means the state in which a receiving agency is located.</p>
          <p>(d) "Sending agency" means a state or county agency which sends an individual to a bordering state for treatment or detoxification under this section.</p>
          <p>(e) "Sending state" means the state in which the sending agency is located.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.50.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Purpose and authority.</span></h2>
