      <div class="sr" id="stat.245.691"><b>245.691</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1979/0/324/#laws.0.50.0">1979 c 324 s 50</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.245.692"><b>245.692</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/572/#laws.0.18.0">1973 c 572 s 18</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.245.693"><b>245.693</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/572/#laws.0.18.0">1973 c 572 s 18</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.245.694"><b>245.694</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/572/#laws.0.18.0">1973 c 572 s 18</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.245.695"><b>245.695</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/572/#laws.0.18.0">1973 c 572 s 18</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245.696">
        <h1 class="shn">245.696 ADDITIONAL DUTIES OF COMMISSIONER.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.696.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Behavioral Health Division.</span></h2>
          <p>A Behavioral Health Division is created in the Department of Human Services. The division shall enforce and coordinate the laws administered by the commissioner of human services, relating to mental illness, which the commissioner assigns to the division. The Behavioral Health Division shall be under the supervision of an assistant commissioner of behavioral health appointed by the commissioner. The commissioner, working with the assistant commissioner of behavioral health, shall oversee and coordinate services to people with mental illness in community programs throughout the state.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.696.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Specific duties.</span></h2>
          <p>In addition to the powers and duties already conferred by law, the commissioner of human services shall:</p>
          <p>(1) review and evaluate local programs and the performance of administrative and mental health personnel and make recommendations to county boards and program administrators;</p>
          <p>(2) provide consultative staff service to communities and advocacy groups to assist in ascertaining local needs and in planning and establishing community mental health programs;</p>
          <p>(3) employ qualified personnel to implement this chapter;</p>
          <p>(4) adopt rules for minimum standards in community mental health services as directed by the legislature;</p>
          <p>(5) cooperate with the commissioners of health and employment and economic development to coordinate services and programs for people with mental illness;</p>
          <p>(6) evaluate the needs of people with mental illness as they relate to assistance payments, medical benefits, nursing home care, and other state and federally funded services;</p>
          <p>(7) provide data and other information, as requested, to the Advisory Council on Mental Health;</p>
          <p>(8) develop and maintain a data collection system to provide information on the prevalence of mental illness, the need for specific mental health services and other services needed by people with mental illness, funding sources for those services, and the extent to which state and local areas are meeting the need for services;</p>
          <p>(9) apply for grants and develop pilot programs to test and demonstrate new methods of assessing mental health needs and delivering mental health services;</p>
          <p>(10) study alternative reimbursement systems and make waiver requests that are deemed necessary by the commissioner;</p>
          <p>(11) provide technical assistance to county boards to improve fiscal management and accountability and quality of mental health services, and consult regularly with county boards, public and private mental health agencies, and client advocacy organizations for purposes of implementing this chapter;</p>
          <p>(12) promote coordination between the mental health system and other human service systems in the planning, funding, and delivery of services; entering into cooperative agreements with other state and local agencies for that purpose as deemed necessary by the commissioner;</p>
          <p>(13) conduct research regarding the relative effectiveness of mental health treatment methods as the commissioner deems appropriate, and for this purpose, enter treatment facilities, observe clients, and review records in a manner consistent with the Minnesota Government Data Practices Act, chapter 13;</p>
          <p>(14) enter into contracts and promulgate rules the commissioner deems necessary to carry out the purposes of this chapter; and</p>
          <p>(15) administer county mental health grants on a calendar year basis, unless that procedure hinders the achievement of the purposes of a particular grant.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245.696.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/342/#laws.0.1.0">1987 c 342 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/689/#laws.2.94.0">1988 c 689 art 2 s 94</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/282/#laws.4.55.0">1989 c 282 art 4 s 55</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/568/#laws.5.28.0">1990 c 568 art 5 s 28</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/483/#laws.0.1.0">1994 c 483 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/529/#laws.0.2.0">1994 c 529 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/206/#laws.0.52.0">2004 c 206 s 52</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/79/#laws.8.11.0">2024 c 79 art 8 s 11</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245.697">
        <h1 class="shn">245.697 STATE ADVISORY COUNCIL ON MENTAL HEALTH.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.697.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Creation.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A State Advisory Council on Mental Health is created. The council must have members appointed by the governor in accordance with federal requirements. In making the appointments, the governor shall consider appropriate representation of communities of color. The council must be composed of:</p>
          <p>(1) the assistant commissioner of the Department of Human Services who oversees behavioral health policy;</p>
          <p>(2) a representative of the Department of Human Services responsible for the medical assistance program;</p>
          <p>(3) a representative of Direct Care and Treatment;</p>
          <p>(4) a representative of the Department of Health;</p>
          <p>(5) one member of each of the following professions:</p>
          <p>(i) psychiatry;</p>
          <p>(ii) psychology;</p>
          <p>(iii) social work;</p>
          <p>(iv) nursing;</p>
          <p>(v) marriage and family therapy; and</p>
          <p>(vi) professional clinical counseling;</p>
          <p>(6) one representative from each of the following advocacy groups: Mental Health Association of Minnesota, NAMI-MN, Minnesota Disability Law Center, American Indian Mental Health Advisory Council, and a consumer-run mental health advocacy group;</p>
          <p>(7) providers of mental health services;</p>
          <p>(8) consumers of mental health services;</p>
          <p>(9) family members of persons with mental illnesses;</p>
          <p>(10) legislators;</p>
          <p>(11) social service agency directors;</p>
          <p>(12) county commissioners; and</p>
          <p>(13) other members reflecting a broad range of community interests, including family physicians, or members as the United States Secretary of Health and Human Services may prescribe by regulation or as may be selected by the governor.</p>
          <p>(b) The council shall select a chair. Terms, compensation, and removal of members and filling of vacancies are governed by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/15.059">15.059</a>. Notwithstanding provisions of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/15.059">15.059</a>, the council and its subcommittee on children's mental health do not expire. The commissioner of human services shall provide staff support and supplies to the council.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.697.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Duties.</span></h2>
          <p>The State Advisory Council on Mental Health shall:</p>
          <p>(1) advise the governor and heads of state departments and agencies about policy, programs, and services affecting people with mental illness;</p>
          <p>(2) advise the commissioner of human services on all phases of the development of mental health aspects of the biennial budget;</p>
          <p>(3) advise the governor about the development of innovative mechanisms for providing and financing services to people with mental illness;</p>
          <p>(4) encourage state departments and other agencies to conduct needed research in the field of mental health;</p>
          <p>(5) review recommendations of the subcommittee on children's mental health;</p>
          <p>(6) educate the public about mental illness and the needs and potential of people with mental illness;</p>
          <p>(7) review and comment on all grants dealing with mental health and on the development and implementation of state and local mental health plans; and</p>
          <p>(8) coordinate the work of local children's and adult mental health advisory councils and subcommittees.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.697.2a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2a.<span class="headnote">Subcommittee on Children's Mental Health.</span></h2>
          <p>The State Advisory Council on Mental Health (the "advisory council") must have a Subcommittee on Children's Mental Health. The subcommittee must make recommendations to the advisory council on policies, laws, regulations, and services relating to children's mental health. Members of the subcommittee must include:</p>
          <p>(1) the commissioners or designees of the commissioners of the Departments of Human Services, Health, Education, State Planning, and Corrections;</p>
          <p>(2) a designee of the Direct Care and Treatment executive board;</p>
          <p>(3) the commissioner of commerce or a designee of the commissioner who is knowledgeable about medical insurance issues;</p>
          <p>(4) at least one representative of an advocacy group for children with emotional disturbances;</p>
          <p>(5) providers of children's mental health services, including at least one provider of services to preadolescent children, one provider of services to adolescents, and one hospital-based provider;</p>
          <p>(6) parents of children who have emotional disturbances;</p>
          <p>(7) a present or former consumer of adolescent mental health services;</p>
          <p>(8) educators currently working with emotionally disturbed children;</p>
          <p>(9) people knowledgeable about the needs of emotionally disturbed children of minority races and cultures;</p>
          <p>(10) people experienced in working with emotionally disturbed children who have committed status offenses;</p>
          <p>(11) members of the advisory council;</p>
          <p>(12) one person from the local corrections department and one representative of the Minnesota District Judges Association Juvenile Committee; and</p>
          <p>(13) county commissioners and social services agency representatives.</p>
          <p>The chair of the advisory council shall appoint subcommittee members described in clauses (4) to (12) through the process established in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/15.0597">15.0597</a>. The chair shall appoint members to ensure a geographical balance on the subcommittee. Terms, compensation, removal, and filling of vacancies are governed by subdivision 1, except that terms of subcommittee members who are also members of the advisory council are coterminous with their terms on the advisory council. The subcommittee shall meet at the call of the subcommittee chair who is elected by the subcommittee from among its members. The subcommittee expires with the expiration of the advisory council.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.697.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Reports.</span></h2>
          <p>The State Advisory Council on Mental Health shall report from time to time on its activities to the governor, the chairs of the appropriate policy committees of the house of representatives and senate, and the commissioners of health, employment and economic development, and human services. It shall file a formal report with the governor not later than October 15 of each even-numbered year so that the information contained in the report, including recommendations, can be included in the governor's budget message to the legislature. It shall also report to the chairs of the appropriate policy committees of the house of representatives and senate not later than November 15 of each even-numbered year.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245.697.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/342/#laws.0.2.0">1987 c 342 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/629/#laws.0.45.0">1988 c 629 s 45</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/689/#laws.2.95.0">1988 c 689 art 2 s 95</a>,96; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/282/#laws.4.56.0">1989 c 282 art 4 s 56</a>-58; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/568/#laws.5.29.0">1990 c 568 art 5 s 29</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/292/#laws.6.27.0">1991 c 292 art 6 s 27</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/483/#laws.0.1.0">1994 c 483 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.16.13.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 16 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/7/#laws.2.33.0">1997 c 7 art 2 s 33</a>,34; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/192/#laws.0.32.0">1997 c 192 s 32</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/39/#laws.0.1.0">1999 c 39 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/112/#laws.1.17.0">2003 c 112 art 1 s 17</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/130/#laws.0.12.0">2003 c 130 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/206/#laws.0.52.0">2004 c 206 s 52</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/247/#laws.5.2.0">2012 c 247 art 5 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/0/30/#laws.13.2.0">2021 c 30 art 13 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/79/#laws.8.12.0">2024 c 79 art 8 s 12</a>,13; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/125/#laws.5.43.0">2024 c 125 art 5 s 43</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/127/#laws.50.43.0">2024 c 127 art 50 s 43</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.245.698"><b>245.698</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/282/#laws.4.64.0">1989 c 282 art 4 s 64</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.245.699"><b>245.699</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/133/#laws.2.13.0">2007 c 133 art 2 s 13</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245.70">
        <h1 class="shn">245.70 MENTAL HEALTH; FEDERAL AID.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.70.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Mentally ill.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of human services is designated the state agency to establish and administer a statewide plan for the care, treatment, diagnosis, or rehabilitation, of the mentally ill which are or may be required as a condition for eligibility for benefits under any federal law and in particular under the Federal Alcohol, Drug Abuse and Mental Health Block Grant Law, United States Code, title 42, sections 300X to 300X-9. The commissioner of human services is authorized and directed to receive, administer, and expend any funds that may be available under any federal law or from any other source, public or private, for such purposes.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.70.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Mental health block grants.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of human services is designated the state authority to establish and administer the state plan for the federal mental health funds available under the alcohol, drug abuse, and mental health services block grant, United States Code, title 42, sections 300X to 300X-9. The commissioner shall receive and administer the available federal mental health funds.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245.70.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1965/0/626/#laws.0.1.0">1965 c 626 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/607/#laws.0.1.0">1982 c 607 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/654/#laws.5.58.0">1984 c 654 art 5 s 58</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/252/#laws.0.1.0">1985 c 252 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245.71">
        <h1 class="shn">245.71 CONDITIONS TO FEDERAL AID FOR MENTALLY ILL.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.71.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Federal aid or block grants.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">The commissioner of human services may comply with all conditions and requirements necessary to receive federal aid or block grants with respect to the establishment, construction, maintenance, equipment or operation, for all the people of this state, of adequate facilities and services as specified in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.70">245.70</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.71.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed by amendment, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/133/#laws.2.8.0">2007 c 133 art 2 s 8</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245.71.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1965/0/626/#laws.0.2.0">1965 c 626 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/607/#laws.0.2.0">1982 c 607 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/640/#laws.0.32.0">1984 c 640 s 32</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/654/#laws.5.58.0">1984 c 654 art 5 s 58</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/252/#laws.0.2.0">1985 c 252 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/133/#laws.2.8.0">2007 c 133 art 2 s 8</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.245.711"><b>245.711</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/1/1/#laws.7.50.0">1Sp1993 c 1 art 7 s 50</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.245.712"><b>245.712</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/1/1/#laws.7.50.0">1Sp1993 c 1 art 7 s 50</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245.713">
        <h1 class="shn">245.713 ALLOCATION FORMULA.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.713.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, 1987 c 403 art 2 s 164]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.713.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Total funds available; allocation.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">Funds granted to the state by the federal government under United States Code, title 42, sections 300X to 300X-9 each federal fiscal year for mental health services must be allocated as follows:</p>
          <p>(a) Any amount set aside by the commissioner of human services for American Indian organizations within the state, which funds shall not duplicate any direct federal funding of American Indian organizations and which funds shall be at least 25 percent of the total federal allocation to the state for mental health services. Money from this source may be used for special committees to advise the commissioner on mental health programs and services for American Indians and other minorities or underserved groups. For purposes of this subdivision, "American Indian organization" means an American Indian tribe or band or an organization providing mental health services that is legally incorporated as a nonprofit organization registered with the secretary of state and governed by a board of directors having at least a majority of American Indian directors.</p>
          <p>(b) An amount not to exceed five percent of the federal block grant allocation for mental health services to be retained by the commissioner for administration.</p>
          <p>(c) Any amount permitted under federal law which the commissioner approves for demonstration or research projects for severely disturbed children and adolescents, the underserved, special populations or multiply disabled mentally ill persons. The groups to be served, the extent and nature of services to be provided, the amount and duration of any grant awards are to be based on criteria set forth in the Alcohol, Drug Abuse and Mental Health Block Grant Law, United States Code, title 42, sections 300X to 300X-9, and on state policies and procedures determined necessary by the commissioner. Grant recipients must comply with applicable state and federal requirements and demonstrate fiscal and program management capabilities that will result in provision of quality, cost-effective services.</p>
          <p>(d) The amount required under federal law, for federally mandated expenditures.</p>
          <p>(e) An amount not to exceed 15 percent of the federal block grant allocation for mental health services to be retained by the commissioner for planning and evaluation.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.713.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/403/#laws.2.164.0">1987 c 403 art 2 s 164</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.713.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/98/#laws.3.25.0">2005 c 98 art 3 s 25</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245.713.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/607/#laws.0.5.0">1982 c 607 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/654/#laws.5.58.0">1984 c 654 art 5 s 58</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/252/#laws.0.4.0">1985 c 252 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/403/#laws.2.41.0">1987 c 403 art 2 s 41</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/282/#laws.4.59.0">1989 c 282 art 4 s 59</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/98/#laws.3.25.0">2005 c 98 art 3 s 25</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/147/#laws.11.23.0">2007 c 147 art 11 s 23</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2022/0/98/#laws.6.6.0">2022 c 98 art 6 s 6</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.245.714"><b>245.714</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/262/#laws.3.18.0">2014 c 262 art 3 s 18</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.245.715"><b>245.715</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/262/#laws.3.18.0">2014 c 262 art 3 s 18</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.245.716"><b>245.716</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/98/#laws.3.25.0">2005 c 98 art 3 s 25</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.245.717"><b>245.717</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/262/#laws.3.18.0">2014 c 262 art 3 s 18</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.245.718"><b>245.718</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/262/#laws.3.18.0">2014 c 262 art 3 s 18</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.245.72"><b>245.72</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/355/#laws.0.34.0">1981 c 355 s 34</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.245.721"><b>245.721</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/262/#laws.3.18.0">2014 c 262 art 3 s 18</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245.73">
        <h1 class="shn">245.73 SERVICES FOR ADULTS WITH MENTAL ILLNESS; GRANTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.73.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Commissioner's duty.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall establish a statewide program to assist counties in ensuring provision of services to adult mentally ill persons. The commissioner shall make grants to county boards to provide community-based services to mentally ill persons through programs licensed under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245A.01">245A.01</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245A.16">245A.16</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.73.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Application; criteria.</span></h2>
          <p>County boards may submit an application and budget for use of the money in the form specified by the commissioner. The commissioner shall make grants only to counties whose applications and budgets are approved by the commissioner for residential programs for adults with mental illness to meet licensing requirements pursuant to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245A.01">245A.01</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245A.16">245A.16</a>. These grants shall not be used for room and board costs. For calendar year 1994 and subsequent years, the commissioner shall allocate the money appropriated under this section on a calendar year basis.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.73.2a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2a.<span class="headnote">Special programs.</span></h2>
          <p>Grants received pursuant to this section may be used to fund innovative programs in residential facilities, related to structured physical fitness programs designed as part of a mental health treatment plan.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.73.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Formula.</span></h2>
          <p>Grants made pursuant to this section shall finance 75 to 100 percent of the county's costs of expanding or providing services for adult mentally ill persons in residential facilities as provided in subdivision 2.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.73.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Rules; reports.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall promulgate an emergency and permanent rule to govern grant applications, approval of applications, allocation of grants, and maintenance of service and financial records by grant recipients. The commissioner shall specify requirements for reports, including quarterly fiscal reports, according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256.01#stat.256.01.2">256.01, subdivision 2</a>, paragraph (o). The commissioner shall require collection of data for compliance, monitoring and evaluation purposes and shall require periodic reports to demonstrate the effectiveness of the services in helping adult mentally ill persons remain and function in their own communities. As a part of the report required by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.461">245.461</a>, the commissioner shall report to the legislature as to the effectiveness of this program and recommendations regarding continued funding.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.73.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Transfer of funds.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may transfer money from adult mental health residential program grants to community support program grants under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256K.10">256K.10</a> if the county requests such a transfer and if the commissioner determines the transfer will help adults with mental illness to remain and function in their own communities. The commissioner shall consider past utilization of the residential program in determining which counties to include in the transferred fund.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245.73.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/360/#laws.2.14.0">1981 c 360 art 2 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/164/#laws.0.1.0">1983 c 164 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/640/#laws.0.32.0">1984 c 640 s 32</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/349/#laws.0.1.0">1986 c 349 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/384/#laws.2.1.0">1987 c 384 art 2 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/89/#laws.0.3.0">1989 c 89 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/282/#laws.2.55.0">1989 c 282 art 2 s 55</a>,56; art 4 s 60; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/568/#laws.5.30.0">1990 c 568 art 5 s 30</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/1/1/#laws.7.21.0">1Sp1993 c 1 art 7 s 21</a>-23; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/0/78/#laws.4.61.0">2015 c 78 art 4 s 61</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/80/#laws.8.68.0">2024 c 80 art 8 s 68</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245.735">
        <h1 class="shn">245.735 CERTIFIED COMMUNITY BEHAVIORAL HEALTH CLINIC SERVICES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.735.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.</h2>
          <p>MS 2020 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/0/30/#laws.11.8.0">2021 c 30 art 11 s 8</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.735.1a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1a.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) For the purposes of this section, the terms in this subdivision have the meanings given.</p>
          <p>(b) "Alcohol and drug counselor" has the meaning given in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245G.11">245G.11, subdivision </a>5.</p>
          <p>(c) "Care coordination" means the activities required to coordinate care across settings and providers for a person served to ensure seamless transitions across the full spectrum of health services. Care coordination includes outreach and engagement; documenting a plan of care for medical, behavioral health, and social services and supports in the integrated treatment plan; assisting with obtaining appointments; confirming appointments are kept; developing a crisis plan; tracking medication; and implementing care coordination agreements with external providers. Care coordination may include psychiatric consultation with primary care practitioners and with mental health clinical care practitioners.</p>
          <p>(d) "Community needs assessment" means an assessment to identify community needs and determine the community behavioral health clinic's capacity to address the needs of the population being served.</p>
          <p>(e) "Comprehensive evaluation" means a person-centered, family-centered, and trauma-informed evaluation meeting the requirements of subdivision 4b completed for the purposes of diagnosis and treatment planning.</p>
          <p>(f) "Designated collaborating organization" means an entity meeting the requirements of subdivision 3a with a formal agreement with a CCBHC to furnish CCBHC services.</p>
          <p>(g) "Functional assessment" means an assessment of a client's current level of functioning relative to functioning that is appropriate for someone the client's age and that meets the requirements of subdivision 4a.</p>
          <p>(h) "Initial evaluation" means an evaluation completed by a mental health professional that gathers and documents information necessary to formulate a preliminary diagnosis and begin client services.</p>
          <p>(i) "Integrated treatment plan" means a documented plan of care that is person- and family-centered and formulated to respond to a client's needs and goals.</p>
          <p>(j) "Mental health professional" has the meaning given in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245I.04">245I.04, subdivision </a>2.</p>
          <p>(k) "Mobile crisis services" has the meaning given in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.0624">256B.0624, subdivision </a>2.</p>
          <p>(l) "Preliminary screening and risk assessment" means a mandatory screening and risk assessment that is completed at the first contact with the prospective CCBHC service recipient and determines the acuity of client need.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.735.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p>MS 2020 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/0/30/#laws.11.8.0">2021 c 30 art 11 s 8</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.735.2a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2a.<span class="headnote">Establishment.</span></h2>
          <p>The certified community behavioral health clinic model is an integrated payment and service delivery model that uses evidence-based behavioral health practices to achieve better outcomes for individuals experiencing behavioral health concerns while achieving sustainable rates for providers and economic efficiencies for payors.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.735.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Certified community behavioral health clinics.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner shall establish state certification and recertification processes for certified community behavioral health clinics (CCBHCs) that satisfy all federal requirements necessary for CCBHCs certified under this section to be eligible for reimbursement under medical assistance, without service area limits based on geographic area or region. The commissioner shall consult with CCBHC stakeholders before establishing and implementing changes in the certification or recertification process and requirements. Any changes to the certification or recertification process or requirements must be consistent with the most recently issued Certified Community Behavioral Health Clinic Certification Criteria published by the Substance Abuse and Mental Health Services Administration. The commissioner must allow a transition period for CCBHCs to meet the revised criteria on or before January 1, 2025. The commissioner is authorized to amend the state's Medicaid state plan or the terms of the demonstration to comply with federal requirements.</p>
          <p>(b) As part of the state CCBHC certification and recertification processes, the commissioner shall provide to entities applying for certification or requesting recertification the standard requirements of the community needs assessment and the staffing plan that are consistent with the most recently issued Certified Community Behavioral Health Clinic Certification Criteria published by the Substance Abuse and Mental Health Services Administration.</p>
          <p>(c) The commissioner shall schedule a certification review that includes a site visit within 90 calendar days of receipt of an application for certification or recertification.</p>
          <p>(d) Entities that choose to be CCBHCs must:</p>
          <p>(1) complete a community needs assessment and complete a staffing plan that is responsive to the needs identified in the community needs assessment and update both the community needs assessment and the staffing plan no less frequently than every 36 months;</p>
          <p>(2) comply with state licensing requirements and other requirements issued by the commissioner;</p>
          <p>(3) employ or contract with a medical director. A medical director must be a physician licensed under chapter 147 and either certified by the American Board of Psychiatry and Neurology, certified by the American Osteopathic Board of Neurology and Psychiatry, or eligible for board certification in psychiatry. A registered nurse who is licensed under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/148.171">148.171</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/148.285">148.285</a> and is certified as a nurse practitioner in adult or family psychiatric and mental health nursing by a national nurse certification organization may serve as the medical director when a CCBHC is unable to employ or contract a qualified physician;</p>
          <p>(4) employ or contract for clinic staff who have backgrounds in diverse disciplines, including licensed mental health professionals and licensed alcohol and drug counselors, and staff who are culturally and linguistically trained to meet the needs of the population the clinic serves;</p>
          <p>(5) ensure that clinic services are available and accessible to individuals and families of all ages and genders with access on evenings and weekends and that crisis management services are available 24 hours per day;</p>
          <p>(6) establish fees for clinic services for individuals who are not enrolled in medical assistance using a sliding fee scale that ensures that services to patients are not denied or limited due to an individual's inability to pay for services;</p>
          <p>(7) comply with quality assurance reporting requirements and other reporting requirements included in the most recently issued Certified Community Behavioral Health Clinic Certification Criteria published by the Substance Abuse and Mental Health Services Administration;</p>
          <p>(8) provide crisis mental health and substance use services, withdrawal management services, emergency crisis intervention services, and stabilization services through existing mobile crisis services; screening, assessment, and diagnosis services, including risk assessments and level of care determinations; person- and family-centered treatment planning; outpatient mental health and substance use services; targeted case management; psychiatric rehabilitation services; peer support and counselor services and family support services; and intensive community-based mental health services, including mental health services for members of the armed forces and veterans. CCBHCs must directly provide the majority of these services to enrollees, but may coordinate some services with another entity through a collaboration or agreement, pursuant to subdivision 3a;</p>
          <p>(9) provide coordination of care across settings and providers to ensure seamless transitions for individuals being served across the full spectrum of health services, including acute, chronic, and behavioral needs;</p>
          <p>(10) be certified as a mental health clinic under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245I.20">245I.20</a>;</p>
          <p>(11) comply with standards established by the commissioner relating to CCBHC screenings, assessments, and evaluations that are consistent with this section;</p>
          <p>(12) be licensed to provide substance use disorder treatment under chapter 245G;</p>
          <p>(13) be certified to provide children's therapeutic services and supports under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.0943">256B.0943</a>;</p>
          <p>(14) be certified to provide adult rehabilitative mental health services under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.0623">256B.0623</a>;</p>
          <p>(15) be enrolled to provide mental health crisis response services under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.0624">256B.0624</a>;</p>
          <p>(16) be enrolled to provide mental health targeted case management under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.0625#stat.256B.0625.20">256B.0625, subdivision 20</a>;</p>
          <p>(17) provide services that comply with the evidence-based practices described in subdivision 3d;</p>
          <p>(18) provide peer services as defined in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.0615">256B.0615</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.0616">256B.0616</a>, and 245G.07, subdivision 2, clause (8), as applicable when peer services are provided; and</p>
          <p>(19) inform all clients upon initiation of care of the full array of services available under the CCBHC model.</p>
          <p class="see_note">[See Note.]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.735.3a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3a.<span class="headnote">Designated collaborating organizations.</span></h2>
          <p>If a certified CCBHC is unable to provide one or more of the services listed in subdivision 3, paragraph (d), clauses (8) to (19), the CCBHC may contract with another entity that has the required authority to provide that service and that meets the requirements of the most recently issued Certified Community Behavioral Health Clinic Certification Criteria published by the Substance Abuse and Mental Health Services Administration.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.735.3b">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3b.<span class="headnote">Exemptions to host county approval.</span></h2>
          <p>Notwithstanding any other law that requires a county contract or other form of county approval for a service listed in subdivision 3, paragraph (d), clause (8), a CCBHC that meets the requirements of this section may receive the prospective payment under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.0625#stat.256B.0625.5m">256B.0625, subdivision 5m</a>, for that service without a county contract or county approval.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.735.3c">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3c.<span class="headnote">Variances.</span></h2>
          <p>When the standards listed in this section or other applicable standards conflict or address similar issues in duplicative or incompatible ways, the commissioner may grant variances to state requirements if the variances do not conflict with federal requirements for services reimbursed under medical assistance. If standards overlap, the commissioner may substitute all or a part of a licensure or certification that is substantially the same as another licensure or certification. The commissioner shall consult with stakeholders before granting variances under this provision. For a CCBHC that is certified but not approved for prospective payment under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.0625">256B.0625, subdivision </a>5m, the commissioner may grant a variance under this paragraph if the variance does not increase the state share of costs.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.735.3d">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3d.<span class="headnote">Evidence-based practices.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall issue a list of required evidence-based practices to be delivered by CCBHCs and may also provide a list of recommended evidence-based practices. The commissioner may update the list to reflect advances in outcomes research and medical services for persons living with mental illnesses or substance use disorders. The commissioner shall take into consideration the adequacy of evidence to support the efficacy of the practice across cultures and ages, the workforce available, and the current availability of the practice in the state. At least 30 days before issuing the initial list or issuing any revisions, the commissioner shall provide stakeholders with an opportunity to comment.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.735.3e">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3e.<span class="headnote">Recertification.</span></h2>
          <p>A CCBHC must apply for recertification every 36 months.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.735.3f">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3f.<span class="headnote">Notice and opportunity for correction.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner shall provide a formal written notice to an applicant for CCBHC certification outlining the determination of the application and process for applicable and necessary corrective action required of the applicant signed by the commissioner or appropriate division director to applicant entities within 45 calendar days of the site visit.</p>
          <p>(b) The commissioner may reject an application if the applicant entity does not take all corrective actions specified in the notice and notify the commissioner that the applicant entity has done so within 60 calendar days.</p>
          <p>(c) The commissioner must send the applicant entity a final decision on the corrected application within 45 calendar days of the applicant entity's notice to the commissioner that the applicant has taken the required corrective actions.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.735.3g">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3g.<span class="headnote">Decertification process.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner must establish a process for decertification. The commissioner must require corrective action, medical assistance repayment, or decertification of a CCBHC that no longer meets the requirements in this section or that fails to meet the standards provided by the commissioner in the application, certification, or recertification process.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.735.3h">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3h.<span class="headnote">Minimum staffing standards.</span></h2>
          <p>A CCBHC must meet minimum staffing requirements required by the most recently issued Certified Community Behavioral Health Clinic Certification Criteria published by the Substance Abuse and Mental Health Services Administration.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.735.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.</h2>
          <p>MS 2020 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/0/30/#laws.11.8.0">2021 c 30 art 11 s 8</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.735.4a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4a.<span class="headnote">Functional assessment requirements.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) For adults, a functional assessment may be completed using a Daily Living Activities-20 tool.</p>
          <p>(b) Notwithstanding any law to the contrary, a functional assessment performed by a CCBHC that meets the requirements of this subdivision satisfies the requirements in:</p>
          <p>(1) section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.0623#stat.256B.0623.9">256B.0623, subdivision 9</a>;</p>
          <p>(2) section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.4711#stat.245.4711.3">245.4711, subdivision 3</a>; and</p>
          <p>(3) Minnesota Rules, part <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/9520.0914">9520.0914</a>, subpart 2.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.735.4b">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4b.<span class="headnote">Requirements for comprehensive evaluations.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A comprehensive evaluation must be completed for all new clients within 60 calendar days following the preliminary screening and risk assessment.</p>
          <p>(b) Only a mental health professional may complete a comprehensive evaluation. The mental health professional must consult with an alcohol and drug counselor when substance use disorder services are deemed clinically appropriate.</p>
          <p>(c) The comprehensive evaluation must consist of the synthesis of existing information including but not limited to an external diagnostic assessment, crisis assessment, preliminary screening and risk assessment, initial evaluation, and primary care screenings.</p>
          <p>(d) A comprehensive evaluation must be completed in the cultural context of the client and updated to reflect changes in the client's conditions and at the client's request or when the client's condition no longer meets the existing diagnosis.</p>
          <p>(e) The psychiatric evaluation and management service fulfills requirements for the comprehensive evaluation when a client of a CCBHC is receiving exclusively psychiatric evaluation and management services. The CCBHC shall complete the comprehensive evaluation within 60 calendar days of a client's referral for additional CCBHC services.</p>
          <p>(f) For clients engaging exclusively in substance use disorder services at the CCBHC, a substance use disorder comprehensive assessment as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245G.05">245G.05</a>, subdivision 2, that is completed within 60 calendar days of service initiation shall fulfill requirements of the comprehensive evaluation.</p>
          <p>(g) Notwithstanding any law to the contrary, a comprehensive evaluation performed by a CCBHC that meets the requirements of this subdivision satisfies the requirements in:</p>
          <p>(1) section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.462#stat.245.462.20">245.462, subdivision 20</a>, paragraph (c);</p>
          <p>(2) section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.4711#stat.245.4711.2">245.4711, subdivision 2</a>, paragraph (b);</p>
          <p>(3) section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.4871#stat.245.4871.6">245.4871, subdivision 6</a>;</p>
          <p>(4) section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.4881#stat.245.4881.2">245.4881, subdivision 2</a>, paragraph (c);</p>
          <p>(5) section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245G.04#stat.245G.04.1">245G.04, subdivision 1</a>;</p>
          <p>(6) section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245G.05#stat.245G.05.1">245G.05, subdivision 1</a>;</p>
          <p>(7) section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245I.10">245I.10, subdivisions 4</a> to 6;</p>
          <p>(8) section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.0623">256B.0623, subdivisions 3</a>, clause (4), 8, and 10;</p>
          <p>(9) section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.0943">256B.0943, subdivisions 3</a> and 6, paragraph (b), clause (1);</p>
          <p>(10) Minnesota Rules, part <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/9520.0909">9520.0909</a>, subpart 1;</p>
          <p>(11) Minnesota Rules, part <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/9520.0910">9520.0910</a>, subparts 1 and 2; and</p>
          <p>(12) Minnesota Rules, part <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/9520.0914">9520.0914</a>, subpart 2.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.735.4c">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4c.<span class="headnote">Requirements for initial evaluations.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A CCBHC must complete either an initial evaluation or a comprehensive evaluation as required by the most recently issued Certified Community Behavioral Health Clinic Certification Criteria published by the Substance Abuse and Mental Health Services Administration.</p>
          <p>(b) Notwithstanding any law to the contrary, an initial evaluation performed by a CCBHC that meets the requirements of this subdivision satisfies the requirements in:</p>
          <p>(1) section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.4711#stat.245.4711.4">245.4711, subdivision 4</a>;</p>
          <p>(2) section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.4881">245.4881, subdivisions 3</a> and 4;</p>
          <p>(3) section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245I.10#stat.245I.10.5">245I.10, subdivision 5</a>;</p>
          <p>(4) section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.0623">256B.0623, subdivisions 3</a>, clause (4), 8, and 10;</p>
          <p>(5) section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.0943">256B.0943, subdivisions 3</a> and 6, paragraph (b), clauses (1) and (2);</p>
          <p>(6) Minnesota Rules, part <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/9520.0909">9520.0909</a>, subpart 1;</p>
          <p>(7) Minnesota Rules, part <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/9520.0910">9520.0910</a>, subpart 1;</p>
          <p>(8) Minnesota Rules, part <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/9520.0914">9520.0914</a>, subpart 2;</p>
          <p>(9) Minnesota Rules, part <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/9520.0918">9520.0918</a>, subparts 1 and 2; and</p>
          <p>(10) Minnesota Rules, part <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/9520.0919">9520.0919</a>, subpart 2.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.735.4d">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4d.<span class="headnote">Requirements for integrated treatment plans.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) An integrated treatment plan must be completed within 60 calendar days following the preliminary screening and risk assessment and updated no less frequently than every six months or when the client's circumstances change.</p>
          <p>(b) Only a mental health professional may complete an integrated treatment plan. The mental health professional must consult with an alcohol and drug counselor when substance use disorder services are deemed clinically appropriate. An alcohol and drug counselor may approve the integrated treatment plan. The integrated treatment plan must be developed through a shared decision-making process with the client, the client's support system if the client chooses, or, for children, with the family or caregivers.</p>
          <p>(c) The integrated treatment plan must:</p>
          <p>(1) use the ASAM 6 dimensional framework; and</p>
          <p>(2) incorporate prevention, medical and behavioral health needs, and service delivery.</p>
          <p>(d) The psychiatric evaluation and management service fulfills requirements for the integrated treatment plan when a client of a CCBHC is receiving exclusively psychiatric evaluation and management services. The CCBHC must complete an integrated treatment plan within 60 calendar days of a client's referral for additional CCBHC services.</p>
          <p>(e) Notwithstanding any law to the contrary, an integrated treatment plan developed by a CCBHC that meets the requirements of this subdivision satisfies the requirements in:</p>
          <p>(1) section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245G.06#stat.245G.06.1">245G.06, subdivision 1</a>;</p>
          <p>(2) section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245G.09#stat.245G.09.3">245G.09, subdivision 3</a>, clause (6);</p>
          <p>(3) section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245I.10">245I.10, subdivisions 7</a> and 8;</p>
          <p>(4) section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.0623#stat.256B.0623.10">256B.0623, subdivision 10</a>; and</p>
          <p>(5) section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.0943#stat.256B.0943.6">256B.0943, subdivision 6</a>, paragraph (b), clause (2).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.735.4e">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4e.<span class="headnote">Additional licensing and certification requirements.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) This subdivision applies to programs and clinics that are a part of a CCBHC.</p>
          <p>(b) The requirements for initial evaluations under subdivision 4c, comprehensive evaluations under subdivision 4b, and integrated treatment plans under subdivision 4d are incorporated into the licensing requirements for substance use disorder treatment programs under chapter 245G.</p>
          <p>(c) The requirements for initial evaluations under subdivision 4c, comprehensive evaluations under subdivision 4b, and integrated treatment plans under subdivision 4d are incorporated into the certification requirements for mental health clinics under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245I.20">245I.20</a>.</p>
          <p>(d) The Department of Human Services licensing division will review, inspect, and investigate for compliance with the requirements in subdivisions 4b to 4d for programs or clinics subject to this subdivision.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.735.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Information systems support.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner and the state chief information officer shall provide information systems support to the projects as necessary to comply with state and federal requirements, including data reporting requirements.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.735.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Section 223 of the Protecting Access to Medicare Act entities.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner must request federal approval to participate in the demonstration program established by section 223 of the Protecting Access to Medicare Act and, if approved, to continue to participate in the demonstration program as long as federal funding for the demonstration program remains available from the United States Department of Health and Human Services. To the extent practicable, the commissioner shall align the requirements of the demonstration program with the requirements under this section for CCBHCs receiving medical assistance reimbursement under the authority of the state's Medicaid state plan. A CCBHC may not apply to participate as a billing provider in both the CCBHC federal demonstration and the benefit for CCBHCs under the medical assistance program.</p>
          <p>(b) The commissioner must follow federal payment guidance, including payment of the CCBHC daily bundled rate for services rendered by CCBHCs to individuals who are dually eligible for Medicare and medical assistance when Medicare is the primary payer for the service. Services provided by a CCBHC operating under the authority of the state's Medicaid state plan will not receive the prospective payment system rate for services rendered by CCBHCs to individuals who are dually eligible for Medicare and medical assistance when Medicare is the primary payer for the service.</p>
          <p>(c) Payment for services rendered by CCBHCs to individuals who have commercial insurance as the primary payer and medical assistance as secondary payer is subject to the requirements under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.37">256B.37</a>. Services provided by a CCBHC operating under the authority of the 223 demonstration or the state's Medicaid state plan will not receive the prospective payment system rate for services rendered by CCBHCs to individuals who have commercial insurance as the primary payer and medical assistance as the secondary payer.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.735.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Addition of CCBHCs to section 223 state demonstration programs.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) If the commissioner's request under subdivision 6 to reenter the demonstration program established by section 223 of the Protecting Access to Medicare Act is approved, upon reentry the commissioner must follow all federal guidance on the addition of CCBHCs to section 223 state demonstration programs.</p>
          <p>(b) Prior to participating in the demonstration, a CCBHC must meet the demonstration certification criteria and prospective payment system guidance in effect at that time and be certified as a CCBHC by the state. The Substance Abuse and Mental Health Services Administration attestation process for CCBHC expansion grants is not sufficient to constitute state certification. CCBHCs newly added to the demonstration must participate in all aspects of the state demonstration program, including but not limited to quality measurement and reporting, evaluation activities, and state CCBHC demonstration program requirements, such as use of state-specified evidence-based practices. A newly added CCBHC must report on quality measures before its first full demonstration year if it joined the demonstration program in calendar year 2023 out of alignment with the state's demonstration year cycle. A CCBHC may provide services in multiple locations and in community-based settings subject to federal rules of the 223 demonstration authority or Medicaid state plan authority.</p>
          <p>(c) If a CCBHC meets the definition of a satellite facility, as defined by the Substance Abuse and Mental Health Services Administration, and was established after April 1, 2014, the CCBHC cannot receive payment as a part of the demonstration program.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.735.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Grievance procedures required.</span></h2>
          <p>CCBHCs and designated collaborating organizations must allow all service recipients access to grievance procedures, which must satisfy the minimum requirements of medical assistance and other grievance requirements such as those that may be mandated by relevant accrediting entities.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245.735.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/0/71/#laws.2.16.0">2015 c 71 art 2 s 16</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/189/#laws.16.1.0">2016 c 189 art 16 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2018/0/182/#laws.2.6.0">2018 c 182 art 2 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/9/#laws.6.4.0">1Sp2019 c 9 art 6 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2020/1/2/#laws.2.1.0">1Sp2020 c 2 art 2 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/0/30/#laws.11.1.0">2021 c 30 art 11 s 1</a>-3,7; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2022/0/98/#laws.4.9.0">2022 c 98 art 4 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/70/#laws.18.1.0">2023 c 70 art 18 s 1</a>-20; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/127/#laws.61.4.0">2024 c 127 art 61 s 4</a></p>
      </div>
      <p><b>NOTE:</b> The amendment to subdivision 3 by Laws 2024, chapter 127, article 61, section 4, is effective upon federal approval. The commissioner of human services shall notify the revisor of statutes when federal approval is obtained. Laws 2024, chapter 127, article 61, section 4, the effective date.</p>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.245.74"><b>245.74</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/403/#laws.2.164.0">1987 c 403 art 2 s 164</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245.75">
        <h1 class="shn">245.75 FEDERAL GRANTS FOR MINNESOTA INDIANS.</h1>
        <p>The commissioner of human services is authorized to enter into contracts with the Department of Health, Education, Welfare and the Department of Interior, Bureau of Indian Affairs, for the purpose of receiving federal grants for the welfare and relief of Minnesota Indians.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245.75.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1965/0/886/#laws.0.23.0">1965 c 886 s 23</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/654/#laws.5.58.0">1984 c 654 art 5 s 58</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/10/#laws.1.44.0">2005 c 10 art 1 s 44</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.245.76"><b>245.76</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/403/#laws.2.164.0">1987 c 403 art 2 s 164</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245.765">
        <h1 class="shn">245.765 REIMBURSEMENT OF COUNTY FOR CERTAIN INDIAN WELFARE COSTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.765.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Reimbursement.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of human services, to the extent that state and federal money is available therefor, shall reimburse any county for all welfare costs expended by the county to any Indian who is an enrolled member of the Red Lake Band of Chippewa Indians and resides upon the Red Lake Indian Reservation. The commissioner may advance payments to a county on an estimated basis subject to audit and adjustment at the end of each state fiscal year. Reimbursements shall be prorated if the state appropriation for this purpose is insufficient to provide full reimbursement.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.765.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Rules.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may promulgate rules for the carrying out of the provisions of subdivision 1, and may negotiate for and accept grants from the United States for the purposes of this section.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245.765.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1971/0/935/#laws.0.1.0">1971 c 935 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/360/#laws.1.20.0">1981 c 360 art 1 s 20</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/654/#laws.5.58.0">1984 c 654 art 5 s 58</a>; 1986 c 444</p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.245.77"><b>245.77</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/262/#laws.3.18.0">2014 c 262 art 3 s 18</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.245.771"><b>245.771</b> [Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142F.05">142F.05</a>]</div>

      <div class="sr" id="stat.245.781"><b>245.781</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/333/#laws.0.20.0">1987 c 333 s 20</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.245.782"><b>245.782</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/333/#laws.0.20.0">1987 c 333 s 20</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.245.783"><b>245.783</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/333/#laws.0.20.0">1987 c 333 s 20</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.245.79"><b>245.79</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/243/#laws.0.15.0">1976 c 243 s 15</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.245.791"><b>245.791</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/333/#laws.0.20.0">1987 c 333 s 20</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.245.792"><b>245.792</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/333/#laws.0.20.0">1987 c 333 s 20</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.245.80"><b>245.80</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/243/#laws.0.15.0">1976 c 243 s 15</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.245.801"><b>245.801</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/333/#laws.0.20.0">1987 c 333 s 20</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245.802">
        <h1 class="shn">245.802 FACILITIES FOR PEOPLE WITH MENTAL ILLNESS; RULES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.802.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/333/#laws.0.20.0">1987 c 333 s 20</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.802.1a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1a.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/333/#laws.0.20.0">1987 c 333 s 20</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.802.1b">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1b.<span class="headnote">Monitoring of facilities.</span></h2>
          <p>After June 30, 1989, no residential facility licensed by the commissioner of human services or the commissioner of health, other than facilities specifically licensed for people with mental illness, may have more than four residents with a diagnosis of mental illness. The commissioner of health, with the cooperation of the commissioner of human services, shall monitor licensed boarding care, board and lodging, and supervised living facilities to assure that this requirement is met. By January 1, 1989, the commissioner of health shall recommend to the legislature an appropriate mechanism for enforcing this requirement.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.802.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/333/#laws.0.20.0">1987 c 333 s 20</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.802.2a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2a.<span class="headnote">Specific review of rules.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall:</p>
          <p>(1) provide in rule for various levels of care to address the residential treatment needs of persons with mental illness;</p>
          <p>(2) review Category I and II programs established in Minnesota Rules, parts <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/9520.0500">9520.0500</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/9520.0670">9520.0670</a> to ensure that the categories of programs provide a continuum of residential service programs for persons with mental illness;</p>
          <p>(3) provide in rule for a definition of the term "treatment" as used in relation to persons with mental illness;</p>
          <p>(4) adjust funding mechanisms by rule as needed to reflect the requirements established by rule for services being provided;</p>
          <p>(5) review and recommend staff educational requirements and staff training as needed; and</p>
          <p>(6) review and make changes in rules relating to residential care and service programs for persons with mental illness as the commissioner may determine necessary.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.802.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/333/#laws.0.20.0">1987 c 333 s 20</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.802.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/333/#laws.0.20.0">1987 c 333 s 20</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.802.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Housing services for persons with mental illness.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of human services shall study the housing needs of people with mental illness and shall articulate a continuum of services from residential treatment as the most intensive service through housing programs as the least intensive. The commissioner shall develop recommendations for implementing the continuum of services and shall present the recommendations to the legislature by January 31, 1988.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245.802.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/243/#laws.0.7.0">1976 c 243 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1977/0/305/#laws.0.45.0">1977 c 305 s 45</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/618/#laws.0.18.0">1980 c 618 s 18</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/360/#laws.2.15.0">1981 c 360 art 2 s 15</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/1/4/#laws.1.115.0">1Sp1981 c 4 art 1 s 115</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/424/#laws.0.130.0">1982 c 424 s 130</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/542/#laws.0.6.0">1984 c 542 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/654/#laws.5.58.0">1984 c 654 art 5 s 58</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/658/#laws.0.2.0">1984 c 658 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/248/#laws.0.70.0">1985 c 248 s 70</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/197/#laws.0.1.0">1987 c 197 s 1</a>-4; 1994 465 art 3 s 8; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/158/#laws.1.214.0">2016 c 158 art 1 s 214</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.245.803"><b>245.803</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/333/#laws.0.20.0">1987 c 333 s 20</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.245.804"><b>245.804</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/333/#laws.0.20.0">1987 c 333 s 20</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.245.805"><b>245.805</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/333/#laws.0.20.0">1987 c 333 s 20</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.245.81"><b>245.81</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/243/#laws.0.15.0">1976 c 243 s 15</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.245.811"><b>245.811</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/333/#laws.0.20.0">1987 c 333 s 20</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.245.812"><b>245.812</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/333/#laws.0.20.0">1987 c 333 s 20</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.245.813"><b>245.813</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/542/#laws.0.2.0">1980 c 542 s 2</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245.814">
        <h1 class="shn">245.814 LIABILITY INSURANCE FOR LICENSED PROVIDERS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.814.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Insurance for foster home providers.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of human services shall within the appropriation provided purchase and provide insurance to individuals licensed as foster home providers to cover their liability for:</p>
          <p>(1) injuries or property damage caused or sustained by persons in foster care in their home; and</p>
          <p>(2) actions arising out of alienation of affections sustained by the birth parents of a foster child or birth parents or children of a foster adult.</p>
          <p>For purposes of this subdivision, insurance for homes licensed to provide adult foster care shall be limited to family adult foster care homes as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245A.02#stat.245A.02.6f">245A.02, subdivision 6f</a>, and family adult day services licensed under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245A.143">245A.143</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.814.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Application of coverage.</span></h2>
          <p>Coverage shall apply to all foster homes licensed by the Department of Human Services, licensed by a federally recognized tribal government, or established by the juvenile court and certified by the commissioner of corrections pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260B.198#stat.260B.198.1">260B.198, subdivision 1</a>, paragraph (a), clause (3), item (v), to the extent that the liability is not covered by the provisions of the standard homeowner's or automobile insurance policy. The insurance shall not cover property owned by the individual foster home provider, damage caused intentionally by a person over 12 years of age, or property damage arising out of business pursuits or the operation of any vehicle, machinery, or equipment.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.814.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Compensation provisions.</span></h2>
          <p>If the commissioner of human services is unable to obtain insurance through ordinary methods for coverage of foster home providers, the appropriation shall be returned to the general fund and the state shall pay claims subject to the following limitations.</p>
          <p>(a) Compensation shall be provided only for injuries, damage, or actions set forth in subdivision 1.</p>
          <p>(b) Compensation shall be subject to the conditions and exclusions set forth in subdivision 2.</p>
          <p>(c) The state shall provide compensation for bodily injury, property damage, or personal injury resulting from the foster home providers activities as a foster home provider while the foster child or adult is in the care, custody, and control of the foster home provider in an amount not to exceed $250,000 for each occurrence.</p>
          <p>(d) The state shall provide compensation for damage or destruction of property caused or sustained by a foster child or adult in an amount not to exceed $250 for each occurrence.</p>
          <p>(e) The compensation in paragraphs (c) and (d) is the total obligation for all damages because of each occurrence regardless of the number of claims made in connection with the same occurrence, but compensation applies separately to each foster home. The state shall have no other responsibility to provide compensation for any injury or loss caused or sustained by any foster home provider or foster child or foster adult.</p>
          <p>This coverage is extended as a benefit to foster home providers to encourage care of persons who need out-of-home care. Nothing in this section shall be construed to mean that foster home providers are agents or employees of the state nor does the state accept any responsibility for the selection, monitoring, supervision, or control of foster home providers which is exclusively the responsibility of the counties which shall regulate foster home providers in the manner set forth in the rules of the commissioner of human services.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.814.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Liability insurance; risk pool.</span></h2>
          <p>If the commissioner determines that appropriate commercial liability insurance coverage is not available for a licensed foster home, group home, developmental achievement center, or day care provider, and that coverage available through the joint underwriting authority of the commissioner of commerce or other public entity is not appropriate for the provider or a class of providers, the commissioner of human services and the commissioner of commerce may jointly establish a risk pool to provide coverage for licensed providers out of premiums or fees paid by providers.
