The commissioners may set limits on coverage, establish premiums or fees, determine the proportionate share of each provider to be collected in a premium or fee based on the provider's claim experience and other factors the commissioners consider appropriate, establish eligibility and application requirements for coverage, and take other action necessary to accomplish the purposes of this subdivision. A human services risk pool fund is created for the purposes of this subdivision. Fees and premiums collected from providers for risk pool coverage are appropriated to the risk pool fund. Interest earned from the investment of money in the fund must be credited to the fund and money in the fund is appropriated to the commissioner of human services to pay administrative costs and covered claims for participating providers. In the event that money in the fund is insufficient to pay outstanding claims and associated administrative costs, the commissioner of human services may assess providers participating in the risk pool amounts sufficient to pay the costs. The commissioner of human services may not assess a provider an amount exceeding one year's premiums collected from that provider.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.814.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Foster care parent liability insurance.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of children, youth, and families may use federal reimbursement money earned on an expenditure for foster care parent liability insurance premiums to offset the costs of the premiums. The commissioners of children, youth, and families and human services will work with the insurance provider to transition coverage and responsibility as appropriate while avoiding a lapse in coverage.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245.814.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1977/0/360/#laws.0.1.0">1977 c 360 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/614/#laws.0.125.0">1980 c 614 s 125</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/654/#laws.5.58.0">1984 c 654 art 5 s 58</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/313/#laws.0.10.0">1986 c 313 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/455/#laws.0.61.0">1986 c 455 s 61</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/689/#laws.2.99.0">1988 c 689 art 2 s 99</a>-101; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/465/#laws.1.62.0">1994 c 465 art 1 s 62</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/631/#laws.0.31.0">1994 c 631 s 31</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/139/#laws.4.2.0">1999 c 139 art 4 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/9/#laws.11.2.0">1Sp2001 c 9 art 11 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/379/#laws.1.113.0">2002 c 379 art 1 s 113</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/288/#laws.1.1.0">2004 c 288 art 1 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/1/6/#laws.7.23.0">1Sp2017 c 6 art 7 s 23</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2020/0/83/#laws.1.67.0">2020 c 83 art 1 s 67</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/25/#laws.0.119.0">2023 c 25 s 119</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/80/#laws.1.45.0">2024 c 80 art 1 s 45</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.245.82"><b>245.82</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/243/#laws.0.15.0">1976 c 243 s 15</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245.821">
        <h1 class="shn">245.821 TREATMENT FACILITIES FOR PERSONS WITH DISABILITIES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.821.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Notice required.</span></h2>
          <p>Notwithstanding any law to the contrary, no private or public facility for the treatment, housing, or counseling of more than five persons with mental illness, physical disability, developmental disability, substance use disorder, or another form of dependency, nor any correctional facility for more than five persons, shall be established without 30 days' written notice to the affected municipality or other political subdivision.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.821.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">State funds available.</span></h2>
          <p>No state funds shall be made available to or be expended by any state or local agency for facilities or programs enumerated in this section unless and until the provisions of this section have been complied with in full.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245.821.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/274/#laws.0.3.0">1974 c 274 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/21/#laws.0.5.0">1985 c 21 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/464/#laws.1.55.0">1992 c 464 art 1 s 55</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/56/#laws.0.1.0">2005 c 56 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2022/0/98/#laws.4.51.0">2022 c 98 art 4 s 51</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/125/#laws.1.2.0">2024 c 125 art 1 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/127/#laws.46.2.0">2024 c 127 art 46 s 2</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245.825">
        <h1 class="shn">245.825 AVERSIVE AND DEPRIVATION PROCEDURES; LICENSED FACILITIES AND SERVICES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.825.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Rules governing aversive and deprivation procedures.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of human services shall by October, 1983, promulgate rules governing the use of aversive and deprivation procedures in all licensed facilities and licensed services serving persons with developmental disabilities. No provision of these rules shall encourage or require the use of aversive and deprivation procedures. The rules shall prohibit: (1) the application of certain aversive and deprivation procedures in facilities except as authorized and monitored by the commissioner; (2) the use of aversive and deprivation procedures that restrict the consumers' normal access to nutritious diet, drinking water, adequate ventilation, necessary medical care, ordinary hygiene facilities, normal sleeping conditions, and necessary clothing; and (3) the use of faradic shock without a court order. The rule shall further specify that consumers may not be denied ordinary access to legal counsel and next of kin. In addition, the rule may specify other prohibited practices and the specific conditions under which permitted practices are to be carried out. For any persons receiving faradic shock, a plan to reduce and eliminate the use of faradic shock shall be in effect upon implementation of the procedure.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.825.1a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1a.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/86/#laws.2.6.0">1999 c 86 art 2 s 6</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.825.1b">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1b.<span class="headnote">Review and approval.</span></h2>
          <p>Notwithstanding the provisions of Minnesota Rules, parts <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/9525.2700">9525.2700</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/9525.2810">9525.2810</a>, the commissioner may designate the county case manager to authorize the use of controlled procedures as defined in Minnesota Rules, parts <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/9525.2710">9525.2710</a>, subpart 9, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/9525.2740">9525.2740</a>, subparts 1 and 2, after review and approval by the interdisciplinary team and the internal review committee as required in Minnesota Rules, part <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/9525.2750">9525.2750</a>, subparts 1a and 2. Use of controlled procedures must be reported to the commissioner in accordance with the requirements of Minnesota Rules, part <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/9525.2750">9525.2750</a>, subpart 2a.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.825.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/207/#laws.11.12.0">1995 c 207 art 11 s 12</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245.825.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/637/#laws.0.1.0">1982 c 637 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/654/#laws.5.58.0">1984 c 654 art 5 s 58</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/21/#laws.0.6.0">1985 c 21 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/110/#laws.0.1.0">1987 c 110 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/464/#laws.1.55.0">1992 c 464 art 1 s 55</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/207/#laws.11.4.0">1995 c 207 art 11 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/86/#laws.2.3.0">1999 c 86 art 2 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/56/#laws.0.1.0">2005 c 56 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/125/#laws.1.3.0">2024 c 125 art 1 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/127/#laws.46.3.0">2024 c 127 art 46 s 3</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245.8251">
        <h1 class="shn">245.8251 RULES FOR POSITIVE SUPPORT STRATEGIES AND PROHIBITIONS AND LIMITS ON RESTRICTIVE INTERVENTIONS; LICENSED FACILITIES AND PROGRAMS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.8251.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Rules governing the use of positive support strategies and restrictive interventions.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of human services shall, by August 31, 2015, adopt rules to govern the use of positive support strategies, and ensure the applicability of chapter 245D prohibitions and limits on the emergency use of manual restraint and on the use of restrictive interventions to facilities and services governed by the rules. The rules apply to all facilities and services licensed under chapter 245D, and all licensed facilities and licensed services serving persons with a developmental disability or related condition, except child care is governed by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142B.63">142B.63</a>. For the purposes of this section, "developmental disability or related condition" has the meaning given in Minnesota Rules, part <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/9525.0016">9525.0016</a>, subpart 2, items A to E.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.8251.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Data collection.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner shall, with stakeholder input, identify data elements specific to incidents of emergency use of manual restraint and positive support transition plans for persons receiving services from licensed facilities and licensed services under chapter 245D and in licensed facilities and licensed services serving persons with a developmental disability or related condition as defined in Minnesota Rules, part <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/9525.0016">9525.0016</a>, subpart 2, effective January 1, 2014. Licensed facilities and licensed services shall report the data in a format and at a frequency determined by the commissioner of human services to the commissioner and the Office of the Ombudsman for Mental Health and Developmental Disabilities.</p>
          <p>(b) Beginning July 1, 2013, licensed facilities and licensed services regulated under Minnesota Rules, parts <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/9525.2700">9525.2700</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/9525.2810">9525.2810</a>, shall submit data regarding the use of all controlled procedures identified in Minnesota Rules, part <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/9525.2740">9525.2740</a>, in a format and at a frequency determined by the commissioner to the commissioner and the Office of the Ombudsman for Mental Health and Developmental Disabilities.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.8251.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">External program review committee.</span></h2>
          <p>Rules adopted according to this section shall establish requirements for an external program review committee appointed by the commissioner to monitor the implementation of the rules and make recommendations to the commissioner about any needed policy changes after adoption of the rules.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.8251.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Interim review panel.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner shall establish an interim review panel by August 15, 2014, for the purpose of reviewing requests for emergency use of procedures that have been part of an approved positive support transition plan when necessary to protect a person from imminent risk of serious injury as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.91#stat.245.91.6">245.91, subdivision 6</a>, due to self-injurious behavior. The panel must make recommendations to the commissioner to approve or deny these requests based on criteria to be established by the interim review panel. The interim review panel shall operate until the external program review committee is established as required under subdivision 3.</p>
          <p>(b) Members of the interim review panel shall be selected based on their expertise and knowledge related to the use of positive support strategies as alternatives to the use of restrictive interventions. The commissioner shall seek input and recommendations in establishing the interim review panel. Members of the interim review panel shall include the following representatives:</p>
          <p>(1) an expert in positive supports;</p>
          <p>(2) a mental health professional, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.462">245.462</a>;</p>
          <p>(3) a licensed health professional as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245D.02#stat.245D.02.14">245D.02, subdivision 14</a>; and</p>
          <p>(4) a representative of the Department of Health.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245.8251.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/108/#laws.8.4.0">2013 c 108 art 8 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/312/#laws.27.5.0">2014 c 312 art 27 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2018/0/163/#laws.0.1.0">2018 c 163 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/80/#laws.2.74.0">2024 c 80 art 2 s 74</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/115/#laws.16.41.0">2024 c 115 art 16 s 41</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245.826">
        <h1 class="shn">245.826 USE OF RESTRICTIVE TECHNIQUES AND PROCEDURES IN FACILITIES SERVING EMOTIONALLY DISTURBED CHILDREN.</h1>
        <p>When amending rules governing facilities serving emotionally disturbed children that are licensed under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245A.09">245A.09</a> and Minnesota Rules, parts <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/2960.0510">2960.0510</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/2960.0530">2960.0530</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/2960.0580">2960.0580</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/2960.0700">2960.0700</a>, the commissioner of human services shall include provisions governing the use of restrictive techniques and procedures. No provision of these rules may encourage or require the use of restrictive techniques and procedures. The rules must prohibit: (1) the application of certain restrictive techniques or procedures in facilities, except as authorized in the child's case plan and monitored by the county caseworker responsible for the child; (2) the use of restrictive techniques or procedures that restrict the clients' normal access to nutritious diet, drinking water, adequate ventilation, necessary medical care, ordinary hygiene facilities, normal sleeping conditions, and necessary clothing; and (3) the use of corporal punishment. The rule may specify other restrictive techniques and procedures and the specific conditions under which permitted techniques and procedures are to be carried out.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245.826.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/542/#laws.0.6.0">1990 c 542 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/158/#laws.1.86.0">2016 c 158 art 1 s 86</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245.8261">
        <h1 class="shn">245.8261 RESTRICTIVE PROCEDURES PLANNING AND REPORTING.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.8261.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Scope.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) This section applies to providers of the following mental health services for children:</p>
          <p>(1) emergency services as defined in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.4871#stat.245.4871.14">245.4871, subdivision 14</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.4879">245.4879</a>;</p>
          <p>(2) family community support services as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.4871#stat.245.4871.17">245.4871, subdivision 17</a>;</p>
          <p>(3) day treatment services as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.4871#stat.245.4871.10">245.4871, subdivision 10</a>;</p>
          <p>(4) therapeutic support of foster care as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.4871#stat.245.4871.34">245.4871, subdivision 34</a>;</p>
          <p>(5) professional home-based family treatment as defined in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.4871">245.4871</a>, subdivision 31, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.4884#stat.245.4884.3">245.4884, subdivision 3</a>; and</p>
          <p>(6) mental health crisis services as defined in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.4871#stat.245.4871.24a">245.4871, subdivision 24a</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.488#stat.245.488.3">245.488, subdivision 3</a>.</p>
          <p>(b) Providers of mental health services for children under paragraph (a) must meet the requirements of this section before using a restrictive procedure with a child.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.8261.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Restrictive procedures plan.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A services provider under subdivision 1, paragraph (a), shall have on file and available for viewing a restrictive procedures plan for children in its program that must include at least the following:</p>
          <p>(1) the list of restrictive procedures the provider intends to use;</p>
          <p>(2) how the provider will monitor and control the use of restrictive procedures;</p>
          <p>(3) a description of the training that staff who use restrictive procedures must complete prior to staff implementation of restrictive procedures;</p>
          <p>(4) how the provider will document information needed to prepare the annual report required in subdivision 15; and</p>
          <p>(5) how the provider will ensure that the child receives treatment for any injury caused by the use of a restrictive procedure.</p>
          <p>(b) For purposes of this section, allowable restrictive procedures include those procedures allowed under subdivision 4, paragraph (a).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.8261.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) For the purposes of this section, the terms in this subdivision have the meanings given them.</p>
          <p>(b) "Commissioner" means the commissioner of human services.</p>
          <p>(c) "Child" means a person under 18 years of age.</p>
          <p>(d) "Individual treatment plan" has the meaning given in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.4871">245.4871</a>, subdivision 21, as required for children's mental health services providers in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.4876#stat.245.4876.3">245.4876, subdivision 3</a>. The individual treatment plan must be based on a diagnostic assessment, which includes assessments and review of medical conditions and risks of psychological trauma that might be incurred by use of seclusion or restraint.</p>
          <p>(e) "Mechanical restraints" means the use of devices to limit a child's movement or hold a child immobile. The term does not mean mechanical restraints used to:</p>
          <p>(1) treat a child's medical needs;</p>
          <p>(2) protect a child known to be at risk of injury resulting from lack of coordination or frequent loss of consciousness; or</p>
          <p>(3) position a child with physical disabilities in a manner specified in the child's plan of care.</p>
          <p>(f) "Physical escort" means physical intervention or contact used as a behavior management technique to guide or carry a child to safety or away from an unsafe or potentially harmful and escalating situation.</p>
          <p>(g) "Physical holding" means physical intervention intended to hold a child immobile or limit a child's movement by using body contact as the only source of physical restraint. The term does not mean physical contact:</p>
          <p>(1) used to facilitate a child's response or completion of a task when the child does not resist or the child's resistance is minimal in intensity and duration; and</p>
          <p>(2) necessary to conduct a medical examination or treatment.</p>
          <p>(h) "Restrictive procedures" means application of an action, force, or condition that controls, constrains, or suppresses the action, behavior, intention, bodily placement, or bodily location of a child in a manner that is involuntary, unintended by that child, depriving, or aversive to that child.</p>
          <p>(i) "Time out" means removing a child from an activity to a location where the child cannot participate or observe the activity and includes moving or ordering a child to an unlocked room.</p>
          <p>(j) "Seclusion" involves the confining of a child alone in a room from which egress is beyond the child's control or prohibited by a mechanism such as a lock or by a device or object positioned to hold the door closed or otherwise prevent the child from leaving the room. The room used for seclusion must be well-lighted, well-ventilated, clean, have an observation window that allows staff to directly monitor the child in seclusion, fixtures that are tamperproof, electrical switches located immediately outside the door, and doors that open out and are unlocked or locked with keyless locks that have immediate release mechanisms.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.8261.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Allowable procedures.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A provider may use one or more of the following restrictive procedures:</p>
          <p>(1) physical escort;</p>
          <p>(2) physical holding;</p>
          <p>(3) seclusion; and</p>
          <p>(4) the limited use of mechanical restraints only in emergency situations.</p>
          <p>(b) A provider shall permit use of restrictive procedures only by program staff who have completed the required training and who are acting under the clinical supervision of a mental health professional.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.8261.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Parental consent and notification.</span></h2>
          <p>Parental consent for use of seclusion and restraint procedures must be obtained when a child begins receiving services; the agreement must be reviewed at least quarterly. A provider shall notify the child's parent or guardian of the use of a restrictive procedure on the same day the procedure is used, unless the parent or guardian notifies the provider that the parent or guardian does not want to receive notification or the parent or guardian requests a different notification schedule.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.8261.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Physical escort requirements.</span></h2>
          <p>The physical escort of a child may be used to control a child who is being guided to a place where the child will be safe and to help de-escalate interactions between the child and others. A provider who uses physical escorting with a child shall meet the following requirements:</p>
          <p>(1) staff shall be trained according to subdivision 11;</p>
          <p>(2) staff shall document the use of physical escort and note the technique used, the time of day, and the names of the staff and child involved; and</p>
          <p>(3) the use of physical escort shall be consistent with the child's treatment plan.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.8261.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Physical holding or seclusion.</span></h2>
          <p>Physical holding or seclusion may be used in emergency situations as a response to imminent serious risk of physical harm to the child or others and when less restrictive interventions are ineffective. A provider who uses physical holding or seclusion shall meet the following requirements:</p>
          <p>(1) an immediate intervention must be necessary to protect the child or others from physical harm;</p>
          <p>(2) the physical holding or seclusion used must be the least intrusive intervention that will effectively react to an emergency;</p>
          <p>(3) the use of physical holding or seclusion must end when the threat of harm ends;</p>
          <p>(4) the child must be constantly and directly observed by staff during the use of physical holding or seclusion;</p>
          <p>(5) the use of physical holding or seclusion must be used under the supervision of a mental health professional;</p>
          <p>(6) staff shall contact the mental health professional to inform the mental health professional about the use of physical holding or seclusion and to ask for permission to use physical holding or seclusion as soon as it may safely be done, but no later than 30 minutes after initiating the use of physical holding or seclusion;</p>
          <p>(7) before staff uses physical holding or seclusion with a child, staff shall complete the training required in subdivision 11 regarding the use of physical holding or seclusion at the program;</p>
          <p>(8) when the need for the use of physical holding or seclusion ends, the child must be assessed to determine if the child can safely be returned to the ongoing activities at the program;</p>
          <p>(9) staff shall treat the child respectfully throughout the procedure;</p>
          <p>(10) the staff person who implemented the use of physical holding or seclusion shall document its use immediately after the incident concludes and the documentation must include at least the following information:</p>
          <p>(i) a detailed description of the incident which led to the use of physical holding or seclusion;</p>
          <p>(ii) an explanation of why the procedure chosen needed to be used;</p>
          <p>(iii) why less restrictive measures failed or were found to be inappropriate;</p>
          <p>(iv) the time the physical hold or seclusion began and the time the child was released;</p>
          <p class="in">(v) documentation of the child's behavioral change and change in physical status for each 15-minute interval the procedure is used; and</p>
          <p>(vi) the names of all staff involved in the use of the procedure and the names of all witnesses to the use of the procedure; and</p>
          <p>(11) if seclusion is used, the room used for the seclusion must:</p>
          <p>(i) be well-lighted, well-ventilated, and clean;</p>
          <p>(ii) have an observation window which allows staff to directly monitor a child in seclusion;</p>
          <p>(iii) have fixtures that are tamperproof, with electrical switches located immediately outside the door;</p>
          <p>(iv) have doors that open out and are unlocked or are locked with keyless locks that have immediate release mechanisms; and</p>
          <p>(v) have objects that may be used by a child to injure the child's self or others removed from the child and the seclusion room before the child is placed in seclusion.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.8261.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Exempt techniques and procedures.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Use of the instructional techniques and intervention procedures listed in this subdivision is not subject to the restrictions established by this section. The child's individual treatment plan, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.4871#stat.245.4871.21">245.4871, subdivision 21</a>, and as required in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.4876#stat.245.4876.3">245.4876, subdivision 3</a>, must address the use of these exempt techniques and procedures. Exempt techniques and procedures include:</p>
          <p>(1) corrective feedback or prompt to assist a child in performing a task or exhibiting a response;</p>
          <p>(2) physical contact to facilitate a child's completion of a task or response that is directed at increasing adaptive behavior when the child does not resist or the child's resistance is minimal in intensity and duration;</p>
          <p>(3) physical contact or a physical prompt to redirect a child's behavior when:</p>
          <p>(i) the behavior does not pose a serious threat to the child or others;</p>
          <p>(ii) the behavior is effectively redirected with less than 60 seconds of physical contact by staff; or</p>
          <p>(iii) the physical contact is used to conduct a necessary medical examination or treatment; and</p>
          <p>(4) manual or mechanical restraint to treat a child's medical needs or to protect a child known to be at risk of injury from an ongoing medical or psychological condition.</p>
          <p>(b) The exemptions under this subdivision must not be used to circumvent the requirements for controlling the use of manual restraint. The exemptions under this subdivision are intended to allow providers the opportunity to deal effectively and naturally with instruction and treatment interventions.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.8261.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Conditions on use of restrictive procedures.</span></h2>
          <p>Restrictive procedures must not:</p>
          <p>(1) be implemented with a child in a manner that constitutes sexual abuse, neglect, or physical abuse under chapter 260E, the reporting of maltreatment of minors;</p>
          <p>(2) restrict a child's normal access to a nutritious diet, drinking water, adequate ventilation, necessary medical care, ordinary hygiene facilities, or necessary clothing or to any protection required by state licensing standards and federal regulations governing the program;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) be used as punishment or for the convenience of staff; or</p>
          <p>(4) deny the child visitation or contact with legal counsel and next of kin.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.8261.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Prohibitions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The following actions or procedures are prohibited:</p>
          <p>(1) using corporal punishment such as hitting, pinching, slapping, or pushing;</p>
          <p>(2) speaking to a child in a manner that ridicules, demeans, threatens, or is abusive;</p>
          <p>(3) requiring a child to assume and maintain a specified physical position or posture, for example, requiring a child to stand with the hands over the child's head for long periods of time or to remain in a fixed position;</p>
          <p>(4) use of restrictive procedures as a disciplinary consequence;</p>
          <p>(5) totally or partially restricting a child's senses, except at a level of intrusiveness that does not exceed:</p>
          <p>(i) placing a hand in front of a child's eyes as a visual screen; or</p>
          <p>(ii) playing music through earphones worn by the child at a level of sound that does not cause discomfort;</p>
          <p>(6) presenting an intense sound, light, noxious smell, taste, substance, or spray, including water mist;</p>
          <p>(7) denying or restricting a child's access to equipment and devices such as walkers, wheelchairs, hearing aids, and communication boards that facilitate the child's functioning, except as provided under paragraph (b).</p>
          <p>(b) When the temporary removal of the equipment or device is necessary to prevent injury to the child or others or serious damage to the equipment or device, the equipment or device shall be returned to the child as soon as possible.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.8261.11">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11.<span class="headnote">Training for staff.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Staff who use restrictive procedures shall successfully complete training in the following skills and knowledge areas before using restrictive procedures with a child:</p>
          <p>(1) the needs and behaviors of children;</p>
          <p>(2) relationship-building;</p>
          <p>(3) alternatives to restrictive procedures, including techniques to identify events and environmental factors that may trigger behavioral escalation;</p>
          <p>(4) de-escalation methods;</p>
          <p>(5) avoiding power struggles;</p>
          <p>(6) documentation standards for the use of restrictive procedures;</p>
          <p>(7) how to obtain emergency medical assistance;</p>
          <p>(8) time limits for restrictive procedures;</p>
          <p>(9) obtaining approval for use of restrictive procedures;</p>
          <p>(10) the proper use of the restrictive procedures approved for the program, including simulated experiences of administering and receiving physical restraint;</p>
          <p>(11) thresholds for employing and ceasing restrictive procedures;</p>
          <p>(12) the physiological and psychological impact of physical holding and seclusion;</p>
          <p>(13) how to monitor and respond to the child's physical signs of distress; and</p>
          <p>(14) recognizing symptoms of and interventions with potential to cause positional asphyxia.</p>
          <p>(b) Training under this subdivision must be repeated every two years.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.8261.12">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 12.<span class="headnote">Administrative review.</span></h2>
          <p>The provider shall complete an administrative review of the use of each restrictive procedure within three working days after the use of the restrictive procedure. The administrative review shall be conducted by someone other than the person who decided to impose the restrictive procedure, or that person's immediate supervisor. The child or the child's representative shall have an opportunity to present evidence and argument to the reviewer about why the procedure was unwarranted. The record of the administrative review of the use of a restrictive procedure must state whether:</p>
          <p>(1) the required documentation was recorded;</p>
          <p>(2) the restrictive procedure was used in accordance with the treatment plan;</p>
          <p>(3) the standards governing the use of restrictive procedures were met; and</p>
          <p>(4) the staff who implemented the restrictive procedures were properly trained.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.8261.13">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 13.<span class="headnote">Review of patterns of use of restrictive procedures.</span></h2>
          <p>At least quarterly, the treatment provider shall review the provider's patterns of the use of restrictive procedures. The review must be completed by the treatment provider or the program's advisory committee. The review shall consider:</p>
          <p>(1) any patterns or problems indicated by similarities in the time of day, day of the week, duration of the use of a procedure, individuals involved, or other factors associated with the use of restrictive procedures;</p>
          <p>(2) any injuries resulting from the use of restrictive procedures;</p>
          <p>(3) actions needed to correct deficiencies in the program's implementation of restrictive procedures;</p>
          <p>(4) an assessment of opportunities missed to avoid the use of restrictive procedures; and</p>
          <p>(5) proposed actions to be taken to minimize the use of physical holding or seclusion.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.8261.14">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 14.<span class="headnote">Annual report.</span></h2>
          <p>A provider using restrictive procedures shall annually submit a report to the commissioner stating the number and types of restrictive procedures performed. The report shall be submitted in a form and manner prescribed by the commissioner. Agencies with high use of restrictive procedures will be reviewed by the commissioner to determine needed changes in policies and procedures, including staff training.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245.8261.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/234/#laws.0.1.0">2008 c 234 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/86/#laws.1.40.0">2009 c 86 art 1 s 40</a>-42; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/0/79/#laws.0.3.0">2017 c 79 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2020/1/2/#laws.8.40.0">1Sp2020 c 2 art 8 s 40</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.245.827"><b>245.827</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/262/#laws.3.18.0">2014 c 262 art 3 s 18</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.245.83"><b>245.83</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/282/#laws.2.219.0">1989 c 282 art 2 s 219</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.245.84"><b>245.84</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/282/#laws.2.219.0">1989 c 282 art 2 s 219</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.245.85"><b>245.85</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/282/#laws.2.219.0">1989 c 282 art 2 s 219</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.245.86"><b>245.86</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/689/#laws.2.269.0">1988 c 689 art 2 s 269</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.245.87"><b>245.87</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/689/#laws.2.269.0">1988 c 689 art 2 s 269</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.245.871"><b>245.871</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/282/#laws.2.219.0">1989 c 282 art 2 s 219</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.245.872"><b>245.872</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/282/#laws.2.219.0">1989 c 282 art 2 s 219</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.245.873"><b>245.873</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/282/#laws.2.219.0">1989 c 282 art 2 s 219</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.245.88"><b>245.88</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/333/#laws.0.20.0">1987 c 333 s 20</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.245.881"><b>245.881</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/333/#laws.0.20.0">1987 c 333 s 20</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.245.882"><b>245.882</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/333/#laws.0.20.0">1987 c 333 s 20</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.245.883"><b>245.883</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/333/#laws.0.20.0">1987 c 333 s 20</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.245.884"><b>245.884</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/333/#laws.0.20.0">1987 c 333 s 20</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.245.885"><b>245.885</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/333/#laws.0.20.0">1987 c 333 s 20</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245.891">
        <h1 class="shn">245.891 OPIOID OVERDOSE SURGE ALERT SYSTEM.</h1>
        <p>The commissioner must establish a voluntary, statewide opioid overdose surge text message alert system to prevent opioid overdose by cautioning people to refrain from substance use or to use harm reduction strategies when there is an overdose surge in their surrounding area. The alert system may include other forms of electronic alerts. The commissioner may collaborate with local agencies, other state agencies, and harm reduction organizations to promote and improve the surge alert system.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245.891.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/61/#laws.5.5.0">2023 c 61 art 5 s 5</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245.90">
        <h1 class="shn">245.90 COURT AWARDED FUNDS, DISPOSITION.</h1>
        <p>The commissioner of human services shall notify the house of representatives Ways and Means and senate Finance Committees of the terms of any contractual arrangement entered into by the commissioner and the attorney general, pursuant to an order of any court of law, which provides for the receipt of funds by the commissioner.</p>
        <p>Any funds recovered or received by the commissioner pursuant to an order of any court of law shall be placed in the general fund.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245.90.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/434/#laws.0.24.0">1975 c 434 s 24</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/654/#laws.5.58.0">1984 c 654 art 5 s 58</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/284/#laws.2.16.0">2004 c 284 art 2 s 16</a></p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">OMBUDSMAN FOR MENTAL HEALTH<br>AND DEVELOPMENTAL DISABILITIES</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245.91">
        <h1 class="shn">245.91 DEFINITIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.91.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Applicability.</span></h2>
          <p>For the purposes of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.91">245.91</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.97">245.97</a>, the following terms have the meanings given them.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.91.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Agency.</span></h2>
          <p>"Agency" means the divisions, officials, or employees of the state Departments of Human Services, Direct Care and Treatment, Health, and Education, and of local school districts and designated county social service agencies as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256G.02#stat.256G.02.7">256G.02, subdivision 7</a>, that are engaged in monitoring, providing, or regulating services or treatment for mental illness, developmental disability, substance use disorder, or emotional disturbance.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.91.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Client.</span></h2>
          <p>"Client" means a person served by an agency, facility, or program, who is receiving services or treatment for mental illness, developmental disability, substance use disorder, or emotional disturbance. Client also includes a now deceased person who had been served by an agency, facility, or program.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.91.3a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3a.<span class="headnote">Emergency use of manual restraint.</span></h2>
          <p>"Emergency use of manual restraint" has the meaning given in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245D.02#stat.245D.02.8a">245D.02, subdivision 8a</a>, and applies to services licensed under chapter 245D.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.91.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Facility or program.</span></h2>
          <p>"Facility" or "program" means a nonresidential or residential program as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245A.02">245A.02</a>, subdivisions 10 and 14, and any agency, facility, or program that provides services or treatment for mental illness, developmental disability, substance use disorder, or emotional disturbance that is required to be licensed, certified, or registered by the commissioner of human services, health, or education; a sober home as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/254B.01#stat.254B.01.11">254B.01, subdivision 11</a>; peer recovery support services provided by a recovery community organization as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/254B.01#stat.254B.01.8">254B.01, subdivision 8</a>; and an acute care inpatient facility that provides services or treatment for mental illness, developmental disability, substance use disorder, or emotional disturbance.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.91.4a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4a.<span class="headnote">Gather.</span></h2>
          <p>"Gather" means access to information or data for purposes of inspection and to receive copies of information or data.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.91.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Regional center.</span></h2>
          <p>"Regional center" means a state-operated treatment program as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/253B.02#stat.253B.02.18d">253B.02, subdivision 18d</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.91.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Serious injury.</span></h2>
          <p>"Serious injury" means:</p>
          <p>(1) fractures;</p>
          <p>(2) dislocations;</p>
          <p>(3) evidence of internal injuries;</p>
          <p>(4) head injuries with loss of consciousness or potential for a closed head injury or concussion without loss of consciousness requiring a medical assessment by a health care professional, whether or not further medical attention was sought;</p>
          <p>(5) lacerations involving injuries to tendons or organs, and those for which complications are present;</p>
          <p>(6) extensive second-degree or third-degree burns, and other burns for which complications are present;</p>
          <p>(7) extensive second-degree or third-degree frostbite, and others for which complications are present;</p>
          <p>(8) irreversible mobility or avulsion of teeth;</p>
          <p>(9) injuries to the eyeball;</p>
          <p>(10) ingestion of foreign substances and objects that are harmful;</p>
          <p>(11) near drowning;</p>
          <p>(12) heat exhaustion or sunstroke;</p>
          <p>(13) attempted suicide; and</p>
          <p>(14) all other injuries and incidents considered serious after an assessment by a health care professional, including but not limited to self-injurious behavior, a medication error requiring medical treatment, a suspected delay of medical treatment, a complication of a previous injury, or a complication of medical treatment for an injury.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245.91.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/352/#laws.0.2.0">1987 c 352 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/543/#laws.0.1.0">1988 c 543 s 1</a>-3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/282/#laws.2.57.0">1989 c 282 art 2 s 57</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/1/4/#laws.7.42.0">1Sp1997 c 4 art 7 s 42</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/130/#laws.0.12.0">2003 c 130 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/56/#laws.0.1.0">2005 c 56 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/219/#laws.0.1.0">2008 c 219 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/108/#laws.8.5.0">2013 c 108 art 8 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/1/6/#laws.8.8.0">1Sp2017 c 6 art 8 s 8</a>,9; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2020/1/2/#laws.6.123.0">1Sp2020 c 2 art 6 s 123</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2022/0/98/#laws.4.51.0">2022 c 98 art 4 s 51</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/25/#laws.0.120.0">2023 c 25 s 120</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/61/#laws.4.3.0">2023 c 61 art 4 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/79/#laws.8.14.0">2024 c 79 art 8 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/125/#laws.3.2.0">2024 c 125 art 3 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/127/#laws.48.2.0">2024 c 127 art 48 s 2</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245.92">
        <h1 class="shn">245.92 OFFICE OF OMBUDSMAN; CREATION; QUALIFICATIONS; FUNCTION.</h1>
        <p>The ombudsman for persons receiving services or treatment for mental illness, developmental disability, substance use disorder, or emotional disturbance shall promote the highest attainable standards of treatment, competence, efficiency, and justice. The ombudsman may gather information and data about decisions, acts, and other matters of an agency, facility, or program, and shall monitor the treatment of individuals participating in a University of Minnesota Department of Psychiatry clinical drug trial. The ombudsman is appointed by the governor, serves in the unclassified service, and may be removed only for just cause. The ombudsman must be selected without regard to political affiliation and must be a person who has knowledge and experience concerning the treatment, needs, and rights of clients, and who is highly competent and qualified. No person may serve as ombudsman while holding another public office.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245.92.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/352/#laws.0.3.0">1987 c 352 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/543/#laws.0.4.0">1988 c 543 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/56/#laws.0.1.0">2005 c 56 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/219/#laws.0.3.0">2008 c 219 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/189/#laws.1.22.0">2016 c 189 art 1 s 22</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2022/0/98/#laws.4.51.0">2022 c 98 art 4 s 51</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245.93">
        <h1 class="shn">245.93 ORGANIZATION OF OFFICE OF OMBUDSMAN.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.93.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Staff.</span></h2>
          <p>The ombudsman may appoint a deputy and a confidential secretary in the unclassified service and may appoint other employees as authorized by the legislature. The ombudsman and the full-time staff are members of the Minnesota State Retirement Association.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.93.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Advocacy.</span></h2>
          <p>The function of mental health and developmental disability client advocacy in the Department of Human Services is transferred to the Office of Ombudsman according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/15.039">15.039</a>. The ombudsman shall maintain at least one client advocate in each regional center.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.93.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Delegation.</span></h2>
          <p>The ombudsman may delegate to members of the staff any authority or duties of the office except the duty of formally making recommendations to an agency or facility or reports to the governor or the legislature.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245.93.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/352/#laws.0.4.0">1987 c 352 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/56/#laws.0.1.0">2005 c 56 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245.94">
        <h1 class="shn">245.94 POWERS OF OMBUDSMAN; REVIEWS AND EVALUATIONS; RECOMMENDATIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.94.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Powers.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The ombudsman may prescribe the methods by which complaints to the office are to be made, reviewed, and acted upon. The ombudsman may not levy a complaint fee.</p>
          <p>(b) The ombudsman is a health oversight agency as defined in Code of Federal Regulations, title 45, section 164.501. The ombudsman may access patient records according to Code of Federal Regulations, title 42, section 2.53. For purposes of this paragraph, "records" has the meaning given in Code of Federal Regulations, title 42, section 2.53(a)(1)(i).</p>
          <p>(c) The ombudsman may mediate or advocate on behalf of a client.</p>
          <p>(d) The ombudsman may investigate the quality of services provided to clients and determine the extent to which quality assurance mechanisms within state and county government work to promote the health, safety, and welfare of clients.</p>
          <p>(e) At the request of a client, or upon receiving a complaint or other information affording reasonable grounds to believe that the rights of one or more clients who may not be capable of requesting assistance have been adversely affected, the ombudsman may gather information and data about and analyze, on behalf of the client, the actions of an agency, facility, or program.</p>
          <p>(f) The ombudsman may gather, on behalf of one or more clients, records of an agency, facility, or program, or records related to clinical drug trials from the University of Minnesota Department of Psychiatry, if the records relate to a matter that is within the scope of the ombudsman's authority. If the records are private and the client is capable of providing consent, the ombudsman shall first obtain the client's consent. The ombudsman is not required to obtain consent for access to private data on clients with developmental disabilities and individuals served by the Minnesota Sex Offender Program. The ombudsman may also take photographic or videographic evidence while reviewing the actions of an agency, facility, or program, with the consent of the client. The ombudsman is not required to obtain consent for access to private data on decedents who were receiving services for mental illness, developmental disability, substance use disorder, or emotional disturbance. All data collected, created, received, or maintained by the ombudsman are governed by chapter 13 and other applicable law.</p>
          <p>(g) Notwithstanding any law to the contrary, the ombudsman may subpoena a person to appear, give testimony, or produce documents or other evidence that the ombudsman considers relevant to a matter under inquiry. The ombudsman may petition the appropriate court in Ramsey County to enforce the subpoena. A witness who is at a hearing or is part of an investigation possesses the same privileges that a witness possesses in the courts or under the law of this state. Data obtained from a person under this paragraph are private data as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/13.02#stat.13.02.12">13.02, subdivision 12</a>.</p>
          <p>(h) The ombudsman may, at reasonable times in the course of conducting a review, enter and view premises within the control of an agency, facility, or program.</p>
          <p>(i) The ombudsman may attend Direct Care and Treatment Review Board and Special Review Board proceedings; proceedings regarding the transfer of clients, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/246.50#stat.246.50.4">246.50, subdivision 4</a>, between institutions operated by the Direct Care and Treatment executive board; and, subject to the consent of the affected client, other proceedings affecting the rights of clients. The ombudsman is not required to obtain consent to attend meetings or proceedings and have access to private data on clients with developmental disabilities and individuals served by the Minnesota Sex Offender Program.</p>
          <p>(j) The ombudsman shall gather data of agencies, facilities, or programs classified as private or confidential as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/13.02">13.02, subdivisions 3</a> and 12, regarding services provided to clients with developmental disabilities and individuals served by the Minnesota Sex Offender Program.</p>
          <p>(k) To avoid duplication and preserve evidence, the ombudsman shall inform relevant licensing or regulatory officials before undertaking a review of an action of the facility or program.</p>
          <p>(l) The Office of Ombudsman shall provide the services of the Civil Commitment Training and Resource Center.</p>
          <p>(m) The ombudsman shall monitor the treatment of individuals participating in a University of Minnesota Department of Psychiatry clinical drug trial and ensure that all protections for human subjects required by federal law and the Institutional Review Board are provided.</p>
          <p>(n) Sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.91">245.91</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.97">245.97</a> are in addition to other provisions of law under which any other remedy or right is provided.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.94.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Matters appropriate for review.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) In selecting matters for review by the office, the ombudsman shall give particular attention to unusual deaths or injuries of a client or reports of emergency use of manual restraint as identified in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245D.061">245D.061</a>, served by an agency, facility, or program, or actions of an agency, facility, or program that:</p>
          <p>(1) may be contrary to law or rule;</p>
          <p>(2) may be unreasonable, unfair, oppressive, or inconsistent with a policy or order of an agency, facility, or program;</p>
          <p>(3) may be mistaken in law or arbitrary in the ascertainment of facts;</p>
          <p>(4) may be unclear or inadequately explained, when reasons should have been revealed;</p>
          <p>(5) may result in abuse or neglect of a person receiving treatment;</p>
          <p>(6) may disregard the rights of a client or other individual served by an agency or facility;</p>
          <p>(7) may impede or promote independence, community integration, and productivity for clients; or</p>
          <p>(8) may impede or improve the monitoring or evaluation of services provided to clients.</p>
          <p>(b) The ombudsman shall, in selecting matters for review and in the course of the review, avoid duplicating other investigations or regulatory efforts.</p>
          <p>(c) The ombudsman shall give particular attention to the death or unusual injury of any individual who is participating in a University of Minnesota Department of Psychiatry clinical drug trial.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.94.2a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2a.<span class="headnote">Mandatory reporting.</span></h2>
          <p>Within 24 hours after a client suffers death or serious injury, the agency, facility, program director, or lead investigator of a clinical drug trial at the University of Minnesota Department of Psychiatry shall notify the ombudsman of the death or serious injury. The emergency use of manual restraint must be reported to the ombudsman as required under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245D.061#stat.245D.061.8">245D.061, subdivision 8</a>. The ombudsman is authorized to receive identifying information about a deceased client according to Code of Federal Regulations, title 42, section 2.15, paragraph (b).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.94.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Complaints.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The ombudsman may receive a complaint from any source concerning an action of an agency, facility, or program. After completing a review, the ombudsman shall inform the complainant and the agency, facility, or program. No client may be punished nor may the general condition of the client's treatment be unfavorably altered as a result of an investigation, a complaint by the client, or by another person on the client's behalf. An agency, facility, or program shall not retaliate or take adverse action against a client or other person, who in good faith makes a complaint or assists in an investigation. The ombudsman may classify as confidential, the identity of a complainant, upon request of the complainant.</p>
          <p>(b) The ombudsman shall receive a complaint from any source concerning an action or inaction of the University of Minnesota Department of Psychiatry related to an individual who is enrolled in a department-approved clinical drug trial. No individual participating in the trial may be punished, nor may the general condition of the individual's treatment be unfavorably altered, as a result of an investigation or a complaint by the individual or the individual's advocate. The university shall not retaliate or take adverse action against any person who in good faith makes a complaint or assists in an investigation. The ombudsman may classify the identity of the complainant as confidential, upon request of the complainant.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.94.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Recommendations to agency.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) If, after reviewing a complaint or conducting an investigation and considering the response of an agency, facility, or program and any other pertinent material, the ombudsman determines that the complaint has merit or the investigation reveals a problem, the ombudsman may recommend that the agency, facility, or program:</p>
          <p>(1) consider the matter further;</p>
          <p>(2) modify or cancel its actions;</p>
          <p>(3) alter a rule, order, or internal policy;</p>
          <p>(4) explain more fully the action in question; or</p>
          <p>(5) take other action.</p>
          <p>(b) At the ombudsman's request, the agency, facility, or program shall, within a reasonable time, inform the ombudsman about the action taken on the recommendation or the reasons for not complying with it.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.94.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Recommendations to University of Minnesota.</span></h2>
          <p>If, after reviewing a complaint or conducting an investigation and considering the response of the clinical drug trial's primary investigator or the Department of Psychiatry, the ombudsman determines that the complaint has merit or the investigation reveals noncompliance with the federal protection of human subjects requirements or the requirements of the Institutional Review Board, the ombudsman shall recommend that the Board of Regents of the University of Minnesota take corrective action to remedy the violations.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245.94.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/352/#laws.0.5.0">1987 c 352 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/543/#laws.0.5.0">1988 c 543 s 5</a>-8; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/282/#laws.2.58.0">1989 c 282 art 2 s 58</a>,59; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/351/#laws.0.16.0">1989 c 351 s 16</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/398/#laws.0.1.0">1990 c 398 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/451/#laws.6.2.0">1996 c 451 art 6 s 2</a>,3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/56/#laws.0.1.0">2005 c 56 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/219/#laws.0.4.0">2008 c 219 s 4</a>,5; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/108/#laws.8.6.0">2013 c 108 art 8 s 6</a>,7; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/275/#laws.1.37.0">2014 c 275 art 1 s 37</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/158/#laws.1.87.0">2016 c 158 art 1 s 87</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/189/#laws.1.23.0">2016 c 189 art 1 s 23</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/1/6/#laws.8.10.0">1Sp2017 c 6 art 8 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2022/0/98/#laws.4.51.0">2022 c 98 art 4 s 51</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/79/#laws.8.15.0">2024 c 79 art 8 s 15</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/125/#laws.5.43.0">2024 c 125 art 5 s 43</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/127/#laws.50.43.0">2024 c 127 art 50 s 43</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245.945">
        <h1 class="shn">245.945 REIMBURSEMENT TO OMBUDSMAN FOR MENTAL HEALTH AND DEVELOPMENTAL DISABILITIES.</h1>
        <p>The commissioner shall obtain federal financial participation for eligible activity by the ombudsman for mental health and developmental disabilities. The ombudsman shall maintain and transmit to the Department of Human Services documentation that is necessary in order to obtain federal funds.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245.945.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/14/#laws.6.3.0">1Sp2003 c 14 art 6 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/56/#laws.0.1.0">2005 c 56 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245.95">
        <h1 class="shn">245.95 RECOMMENDATIONS AND REPORTS TO GOVERNOR.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.95.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Specific reports.</span></h2>
          <p>The ombudsman may send conclusions and suggestions concerning any matter reviewed to the governor. Before making public a conclusion or recommendation that expressly or implicitly criticizes an agency, facility, program, or any person, the ombudsman shall consult with the governor and the agency, facility, program, or person concerning the conclusion or recommendation. When sending a conclusion or recommendation to the governor that is adverse to an agency, facility, program, or any person, the ombudsman shall include any statement of reasonable length made by that agency, facility, program, or person in defense or mitigation of the office's conclusion or recommendation. For purposes of this subdivision, "agency, facility, program, or any person" includes the University of Minnesota Department of Psychiatry and its employees working in clinical drug trials.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.95.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">General reports.</span></h2>
          <p>In addition to whatever conclusions or recommendations the ombudsman may make to the governor on an ad hoc basis, the ombudsman shall, at the end of each biennium, report to the governor concerning the exercise of the ombudsman's functions during the preceding biennium.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245.95.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/352/#laws.0.6.0">1987 c 352 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/543/#laws.0.9.0">1988 c 543 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/451/#laws.6.4.0">1996 c 451 art 6 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/189/#laws.1.24.0">2016 c 189 art 1 s 24</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245.96">
        <h1 class="shn">245.96 CIVIL ACTIONS.</h1>
        <p>The ombudsman and designees of the ombudsman are not civilly liable for any action taken under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.91">245.91</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.97">245.97</a> if the action was taken in good faith, was within the scope of the ombudsman's authority, and did not constitute willful or reckless misconduct.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245.96.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/352/#laws.0.7.0">1987 c 352 s 7</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245.97">
        <h1 class="shn">245.97 OMBUDSMAN COMMITTEE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.97.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Membership.</span></h2>
          <p>The Ombudsman Committee consists of 15 members appointed by the governor to three-year terms. Members shall be appointed on the basis of their knowledge of and interest in the health and human services system subject to the ombudsman's authority. In making the appointments, the governor shall try to ensure that the overall membership of the committee adequately reflects the agencies, facilities, and programs within the ombudsman's authority and that members include consumer representatives, including clients, former clients, and relatives of present or former clients; representatives of advocacy organizations for clients and other individuals served by an agency, facility, or program; human services and health care professionals, including specialists in psychiatry, psychology, internal medicine, and forensic pathology; and other providers of services or treatment to clients.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.97.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Compensation; chair.</span></h2>
          <p> Members do not receive compensation, but are entitled to receive reimbursement for reasonable and necessary expenses incurred. The governor shall designate one member of the committee to serve as its chair at the pleasure of the governor.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.97.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Meetings.</span></h2>
          <p>The committee shall meet at least four times a year at the request of its chair or the ombudsman.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.97.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Duties.</span></h2>
          <p>The committee shall advise and assist the ombudsman in selecting matters for attention; developing policies, plans, and programs to carry out the ombudsman's functions and powers; and making reports and recommendations for changes designed to improve standards of competence, efficiency, justice, and protection of rights. The committee shall function as an advisory body.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.97.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Medical Review Subcommittee.</span></h2>
          <p>At least five members of the committee, including at least three physicians, one of whom is a psychiatrist, must be designated by the governor to serve as a Medical Review Subcommittee. Terms of service, vacancies, and compensation are governed by subdivision 2. The governor shall designate one of the members to serve as chair of the subcommittee. The Medical Review Subcommittee may have access to private and confidential data collected or created by the ombudsman that are necessary to fulfill the duties of the Medical Review Subcommittee under this section and may:</p>
          <p>(1) make a preliminary determination of whether the death of a client that has been brought to its attention is unusual or reasonably appears to have resulted from causes other than natural causes and warrants investigation;</p>
          <p>(2) review the causes of and circumstances surrounding the death;</p>
          <p>(3) request the county coroner or medical examiner to conduct an autopsy;</p>
          <p>(4) assist an agency in its investigations of unusual deaths and deaths from causes other than natural causes;</p>
          <p>(5) make a preliminary determination of whether the death of a participant in a clinical drug trial conducted by the University of Minnesota Department of Psychiatry appears to have resulted from causes other than natural causes and warrants investigation and reporting as required by federal laws on the protection of human subjects; and</p>
          <p>(6) submit a report regarding the death of a client to the committee, the ombudsman, the client's next-of-kin, and the facility where the death occurred and, where appropriate, make recommendations to prevent recurrence of similar deaths to the head of each affected agency or facility, or the Board of Regents of the University of Minnesota.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.97.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Terms, compensation, and removal.</span></h2>
          <p>The membership terms, compensation, and removal of members of the committee and the filling of membership vacancies are governed by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/15.0597">15.0597</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.97.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/286/#laws.8.40.0">2014 c 286 art 8 s 40</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245.97.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/352/#laws.0.8.0">1987 c 352 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/543/#laws.0.10.0">1988 c 543 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/629/#laws.0.46.0">1988 c 629 s 46</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/286/#laws.0.26.0">1993 c 286 s 26</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/451/#laws.6.5.0">1996 c 451 art 6 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/133/#laws.2.9.0">2007 c 133 art 2 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/219/#laws.0.6.0">2008 c 219 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/189/#laws.1.25.0">2016 c 189 art 1 s 25</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/1/6/#laws.8.11.0">1Sp2017 c 6 art 8 s 11</a></p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">OMBUDSPERSON FOR FAMILY CHILD CARE PROVIDERS</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245.975">
        <h1 class="shn">245.975 OMBUDSPERSON FOR FAMILY CHILD CARE PROVIDERS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.975.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Appointment.</span></h2>
          <p>The governor shall appoint an ombudsperson in the unclassified service to assist family child care providers with licensing, compliance, and other issues facing family child care providers. The ombudsperson must be selected without regard to the person's political affiliation and must have been a licensed family child care provider for at least three years. The ombudsperson shall serve a term of four years, which may be renewed, and may be removed prior to the end of the term for just cause.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.975.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Duties.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The ombudsperson's duties shall include:</p>
          <p>(1) advocating on behalf of a family child care provider to address all areas of concern related to the provision of child care services, including licensing monitoring activities, licensing actions, and other interactions with state and county licensing staff;</p>
          <p>(2) providing recommendations for family child care improvement or family child care provider education;</p>
          <p>(3) operating a telephone line to answer questions, receive complaints, and discuss agency actions when a family child care provider believes that the provider's rights or program may have been adversely affected; and</p>
          <p>(4) assisting a family child care license applicant with navigating the application process.</p>
          <p>(b) The ombudsperson must report annually by December 31 to the commissioner of children, youth, and families and the chairs and ranking minority members of the legislative committees with jurisdiction over child care on the services provided by the ombudsperson to child care providers, including the number and locations of child care providers served and the activities of the ombudsperson in carrying out the duties under this section. The commissioner shall determine the form of the report and may specify additional reporting requirements.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245.975.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Staff.</span></h2>
          <p>The ombudsperson may appoint and compensate out of available funds a deputy, confidential secretary, and other employees in the unclassified service as authorized by law. The ombudsperson and the full-time staff are members of the Minnesota State Retirement Association.
